- **Added: the first load's question** (`maiamode/startup.ts` `offer`, `prefs.ts` `offers`; ADR 0058's second amendment). A member the gate allows who never turned Maia Mode on is asked once, on the loading overlay, in the chooser's look and words ("Open your office in": Maia Mode or Desk Mode), without the "Ask every time" checkbox, Desk Mode the default. Maia Mode opens the disclosure over the desks (nothing turns on until it's accepted); Desk Mode, Esc or "Not now" leave the desks. Either answer saves `prefs.maiaMode.offered: true`, so it's never asked again on any device. Only on `/v1/me`'s own answer, never for anyone with a disclosure on record.

It’s really 0rtiz-mail.example.", look-alike letters, a name that held an address) and a quiet mark on a warned row; Report spam in a conversation's menu and on a Request (`courier.report`), with Undo; the learning filter's line in Spam ("Courier’s filter put this in Spam. Not spam teaches it."); blocked senders in Accounts with Unblock, and Unblock from a reply to a blocked address; Save to Files beside each attachment (`courier.attachment.save`), a dangerous type only after its second press in place, the line at the foot naming the path in Files.

## Unreleased - Courier's transports through the relay: the desk's address and members' sealed messages (`sub/mm-courier-transports`, the Courier launch plan's Wave 2.5; deskd and the test kit; Courier still behind `COURIER_READY`, in no release)
- **Added (deskd): `courier/relay`,** one transport through Platform's relay (ma-platform contract 0.4.0; draft ADR `draft-courier-transports`) for mail to the desk's own address (sealed by the mail door) and sealed messages between members' desks. It collects at start, on `notify relay` and every 10 minutes, **never waking a desk**; stores each envelope once, then acks; leaves what may pass later (the directory unreachable, a drop's link not issued today, the door's key unknown) for the next drain; and refuses for good, counted by reason, what doesn't open, isn't for this desk, is older than the relay's wait (replay, Security CK-I6), is a forged sender or has no keys to check. Tombstones keep a message the member deleted from coming back.
- **Added (deskd): key pinning, safety numbers and invites.** The identity pinned per member address (`courier.db` version 4: `ma_pins`, `ma_held`, `relay_seen`): first contact trusts the directory and is a Request; a message under a new identity is held and blocks sending to that address until the member checks the new number (`courier.contact.verify`), which releases it and sends what waited; an invite link's fingerprint pins as checked (`courier.invite.open`). New methods: `courier.address` (the address, its invite link, the relay's state), `courier.invite.open`, `courier.contacts.add` (the office's Add, the shape `sub/mm-courier-ui` proposed), `courier.contact.keys`, `courier.contact.verify`, `courier.compose.pick` (design §8.3's default delivery and its reason). API.md §21, "The desk's address and members' messages".
- **Added (deskd): sending.** `ma` sealed per recipient to the pinned keys (long text in the ciphertext drop, one `Idempotency-Key` per row and recipient); email from the desk's address through the `CourierSend` seam (`PlatformCourierSend`, the format proposed for contract 0.4.1), except to members' addresses, which get it sealed. Delivery notes from the door set each recipient's status. Kill switches `ma-off`, `ma-send-off`, `door-off`, `door-send-off`.
- **Changed (deskd):** `AgentClient.courier` for routes answering 201 and 202 with signed extra headers; `GatewayLink.onRelay` for `notify relay`; `MimeWriter` public for the desk's address.
- **Added (courier-testkit):** `courier-send` on the fake relay (accepted and refused recipients, idempotency, a lost answer, the provider off), the door's delivery and removal notes, `doorKeys` and `sending` in the addresses answer, raw envelope injection; drop reservations sign `Content-Length` and `x-amz-tagging` as staging's do, and `RelayClient.upload` sends them.
- **Security's stand-in read:** one High, three Mediums, two Lows, five Infos; all fixed but CT-I4 (Platform's drop links, in the ADR's 0.4.1 notes): no envelope stops a drain (CT-H1), a sender off the Courier domain is refused without a lookup (CT-M1), an invite with a pin is checked against the box keys recorded for it and never silently replaces a verified pin (CT-M2), the drain holds about one large body at a time (CT-M3), held caps by bytes (CT-L1), an hour's grace for a door key (CT-L2), loopback drop links only for the test kit (CT-I1), and CT-I2, I3, I5; confirmed, with CT-L3 (a door-key miss re-reads at most every 5 minutes) and CT-I6 (only the directory's answers about the address are lasting) fixed after.
- **Proof:** `RelayTransportTest` 19, `RelayMethodsTest` 6, `PlatformRelayApiTest` 4, `RelaySoakTest` (2,000 through every relay fault: 0 lost, 0 doubled), 10,000 sealed messages drained in about 7 s; the real staging relay as two self-hosted test desks, 22 of 22 (`services/deskd/e2e/courier-relay-proof.mjs`, `RelayStagingIT`). `docs/sub-status/mm-courier-transports.md`.

## Unreleased - Courier's spam and safety, and attachments into Files (`sub/mm-courier-safety`, the Courier launch plan's Waves 3.2 and 3.1; deskd, the test kit; Courier still behind `COURIER_READY`, in no release)
- **Added (deskd): the learning filter** (`courier/store/SpamFilter`; API.md §21, "Spam and safety"; the draft ADR `draft-courier-store` §17): naive Bayes per desk, taught only by the member (into Spam is spam; Not spam, Let in and writing to someone are wanted), judging only strangers' mail that would be a Request or Other, acting after 10 of each at a score of 0.95. Tokens are kept as SipHash-2-4 under the desk's own salt, never as words; nothing leaves the desk, and on a linked mailbox the provider isn't told. What it screens is `screen: filter`, and Not spam or a later Let in brings it back. Kill switch `filter-off`.
- **Added (deskd): sender warnings** (`courier/store/Senders`), made as a stranger's message arrives: `not_usual_address` (a known contact's name on another address), `lookalike` (UTS #39 confusables, one letter off a well-known name, or a well-known domain in front of another; against the member's own domains, known contacts' and well-known ones, MA's included), `punycode` (an internationalized domain that mixes scripts or passes for Latin) and `name_held_address`. On `courier.message` and `courier.thread` as `warnings`; a list row says `warned`.
- **Added (deskd): `courier.report`** ("Report spam", with `block`): to Spam, a linked mailbox's to its provider's Junk, the filter taught, and a report owed to the mail door for mail to the desk's own address (`CourierStore.owedReports`, for Wave 2.5's transport).
- **Fixed (deskd, Security CS-I2):** a Request's Block now writes a block for each of the sender's addresses, listed in `courier.blocks.list`, and Unblock ends exactly what its block covered, answering `not_found` with nothing changed when there's no such block.
- **Added (deskd): attachments** (`courier/CourierAttachments`; API.md §21, "Attachments"): `courier.attachment.save` into Files' `Courier/<date> <subject>/` through Files' own door (never replacing anything), `courier.attachment.read` (Download), `.image` (a preview drawn again in the sandbox), `.archive` (a list read without unpacking) and `courier.message.raw`, each on `bulk` as `files.read` sends a file, staged in an unlinked file. Dangerous types need `allowDanger`; a file that starts like a program is one whatever its name; nothing is ever opened on the desk. `CourierSandbox` reads a part's counted answer into exactly its size.
- **Schema 3** of `courier.db`: `messages.screen` and `warn`, `contacts.name_key`, `endpoints.skel`, the filter's tables, `reports`.
- **Added (courier-testkit): `SpamCorpus`,** a generated, labelled spam and wanted-mail corpus (8 spam families, 7 wanted ones), deterministic for its seed.
- **Proof:** `docs/sub-status/mm-courier-safety.md`.

- **Fixed: the app zoom popout's slider was the browser's own,** whose empty track in Zen and Dark is Chromium's near-black with a grey rim, a stripe on the slate popout. It's now the office's one range style, `.ma-range` (`src/styles/app.css`), drawn from tokens: a 4 px `surface.fillStrong` track, its filled part and the 16 px thumb the accent, the thumb with a 2 px `surface.overlay` rim, the office's focus ring; the system's own control under forced colors. A menu that opens anyway (the top bar's, a desk's, the logo's, the bottom bar's zoom, as one does in a race with the tour's start) comes first: the tour steps back (`.tour.yield`: no scrim, no card, no presses, not modal), lets the office go and hears no key, so the menu is the member's; as it shuts the tour is back on the same step, the office held again and the keyboard on Next. A sheet that menu opens still ends the tour as Skip does, leaving the keyboard where the member put it.
- **Fixed: Light's `text.link` was 4.48:1 on `surface.fill`** (and 4.00:1 on `fillStrong`). It's the kit's `#355F91` now (`docs/assets/brand/ui/tokens.json`, the Designer's Nov 11 pick, `accent.hover`'s value): 4.96:1 or more on every Light ground. Zen and Dark already held 4.5:1 everywhere. `tokens.test.ts` checks the link on every ground, fills included, with no rounding allowance; `linkToken.test.ts` holds the office to the kit. `check-token-parity.mjs` now lists Light `text.link` against the landing's `--ma-link` (`#3d6fa8`), for Platform's next landing pass.
- **Changed: agent identity tones, twelve to the Designer's seven cool ones** (`web/agent/src/look/tones.ts`; desk-agent's `TeamTools.TONES`): `graphite` (the default), `steel`, `blue`, `indigo`, `harbor`, `navy`, `iris`, all within HSL hue 195 to 290, delta E 2000 of 9.02 or more in each theme, the mark 4.56:1 or more. No green, brown or warm tile. Retired ids map to the nearest kept one in both lists (`RETIRED_TONES`; desk-agent's `TeamStore.RETIRED_TONES`, which `TeamSchema` applies to stored rows once at open and every input on the way in): `rose`, `violet`, `plum` to `iris`; `clay` to `navy`; `amber`, `cyan` to `steel`; `olive`, `green`, `teal`, `moss` to `harbor`; `sky` to `blue`. The Agent app's mock takes a retired id as desk-agent does. The office draws an agent's tile from the same table, so its tiles change with it.

## Unreleased - Courier's keys and envelopes, and the rest of its test kit (`sub/mm-courier-keys`, the Courier launch plan's Waves 1.4 and 0.4; deskd, a new library, the image recipe, tools; in no release)
- **Added: `services/courier-envelope`,** Courier's envelopes on ma-platform contract 0.4.0 §2 (draft ADR `draft-courier-envelope`): HPKE (RFC 9180 base mode, X25519, HKDF-SHA256, ChaCha20-Poly1305) and Ed25519 from Bouncy Castle (`bcprov-jdk18on` 1.86; no primitive of our own), the contract's binding and fingerprint, `info` and `aad`, `0x80` padding to the relay's buckets, the signed `{p, s}` payload with sealed sender, the drop's chunked AEAD under a random content key, pin checks on the identity key (`KeyCheck`), Signal-style safety numbers over the identity keys, and published test vectors for Platform's mail door. 32 tests, RFC 9180 A.2.1 and RFC 8032 among them.
- **Added (deskd): the desk's Courier keys** (`courier/keys/`): an Ed25519 identity and X25519 box keys in `/data/keys` (0700; files 0600, written atomically, read without links; a damaged file set aside, never overwritten), the box key rotated every 180 days with retired ones kept 97 days to open what's waiting, published with `PUT /v1/agent/keys` at start and after a change while Courier may run on the desk, and `courier.keys` (API.md §21) with a contact's safety number. No `/data/keys`: `unavailable` `{reason: keys}`, nothing else changes. The AppCDS training loads and publishes keys. 16 tests.
- **Added (image recipe, not baked): `/data/keys`,** made by `desk-datadirs` (1100:1100 0700), checked by `verify.sh`, mounted into deskd's container by `docker/run` where it exists (`MA_KEYS_DIR` for tests; `test-release-scripts.sh` checks both ways). **`tools/desk-data` and `tools/desk-move`:** `keys` is a machine part, never in a download or a move (a move carrying it is Wave 3.3).
- **Security's stand-in read: CLEAR** (one Low, CK-L1: a `mail` payload is now bound to a `mail` envelope and its own signing domain, from the reserved `mailer-daemon@`; Infos CK-I1 to I4, I7 and I8 fixed; I5 and I6 written into the ADR for Wave 2.5).
- **Added (courier-testkit):** a fake of Platform's relay (every agent route of contract 0.4.0 §3 with its caps, drops, expiry, push and faults) with a fake mail door that seals mail as proposed for 0.4.1, a relay client and a fake peer desk; a fake BlueBubbles Server (REST, Socket.IO over a WebSocket, webhooks, Private API on or off, faults); and the soak harness (plan, channels for IMAP, the door, members and iMessage, the source-side faults, a ledger that resumes, a report of lost, doubled, unexpected and never-sent markers), proven by a self-test through every fake and fault. 38 new tests.

Chromium's keys in a tab fold in; a site's remembered level is erased; nothing is read in the phone view; `browser.pinch` and `browser.field.at` divide by the zoom; the Agent's tabs stay at 1280x800, ratio 1.

## Unreleased - Courier's linked mailboxes over IMAP and SMTP (`sub/mm-courier-imap`, the Courier launch plan's Wave 2.1; deskd, desk-agent, the Desk app; Courier still behind `COURIER_READY`, in no release)
- **Added (deskd): linked mailboxes, Courier's first transport** (`courier/linked`; API.md §21, "Linked mailboxes"; the draft ADR `draft-courier-store` §15). Its own IMAP client (IDLE, CONDSTORE and QRESYNC, UIDPLUS, MOVE, SPECIAL-USE, ESEARCH, Gmail's labels, each where the server has it, with fallbacks); a paced first sync, newest first and Sent first, the last 12 months before older mail, its old mail `backfill` (never given by `courier_new`); live on IDLE, catch-up after sleep, UIDVALIDITY resets matched again without fetches; the member's read, Trash, Spam, restore, delete for good and block both ways. SMTP submission from the account's own address, with a Sent copy where the provider files none and an unclear end looked for in Sent before a retry. The store's schema 2 (folders, places, orphans, ops) commits with the sink, so 0 missed and 0 doubled across faults.
- **Added (deskd): `courier.accounts.add`, `signin`, `remove`, `list`, `providers` with the transport attached.** A sign-in is checked against IMAP and SMTP before it's sealed, never sent back or logged; the owner's session only. A named provider's servers are the desk's own: the office sends the tile's `provider`, and with none the address says which (its domain, or its MX all at one named provider). `sync.provider` names the tile "Sign in again" opens.
- **Where it connects:** names resolved at every connect, private, loopback, link-local, IMDS and the desk's own addresses refused, the checked address dialled; 993, 465 and 587 only; TLS 1.2 or 1.3 with the host checked, STARTTLS required on 587. The sign-in check ends within 50 s and a send within 15 minutes.
- **Changed (deskd): the sender line.** DMARC counts only for the From's own domain (Security CP-L2), and a linked provider's results come from the topmost `Authentication-Results` field alone, when it carries the provider's authserv-id.
- **Changed (desk-agent):** an attachment's parse waits 40 s, past `desk-svc`'s 35 s kill (CP-I1).
- **Changed (the Desk app's Courier):** a named tile sends its provider; "Sign in again" opens the tile the desk names. No visible change.
- **Added (`services/courier-testkit`):** `FakeImapServer` and `FakeSmtpServer`, scripted servers with faults, for Wave 2.1's tests.
- **Security's stand-in read: CLEAR** after three Mediums (a server's answers could make millions of objects inside every byte limit, by UIDs and literals, then by flags on kept FETCHes; a field the sender wrote could fill a DMARC result the provider left out), three Lows and two Infos were fixed and confirmed (`docs/sub-status/mm-courier-imap.md`).
- **Release:** deskd and desk-agent releases carry it; real mail also needs System's image change (`desk-parse-courier`, `/data/courier`, the tracker list, `desk-data`; `mm-courier-parse.md`). Courier's tile stays "Coming later" until `COURIER_READY` turns true after that.

Pictures from the web stay off, and say so. Attachments are named, never opened; a dangerous type says what it is.

Closing the tab with words not yet on the desk asks first.

- **Added (desk-agent tests):** `harness/`: the fault matrix (10 kill points × desk-agent killed, deskd restarted, the desk stopped × 6 kinds of call, the store away and full, and every deskd tool: 280 cases against `CountingDeskd`, a deskd that keeps its contract and counts what it ran; 0 double executions, 0 silent losses and, with W3 and the durable resume on main, no known failures; the list is strict), its self-test, the audit canary (a string in every argument of every declared tool, found in no audit row, byte or log line), one test per §10 threat and design §9.7's fixture site (12 attacker pages). **A new tool joins with one line in `harness/ToolCases`; `HarnessCoverageTest` fails until it has one.**
- **Added (deskd tests):** §10's threats 3, 7, 13, 14 and 17 and deskd's faults on the real `ToolHost`, tickets and Files and Notebook tools; `HarnessToolsPerf` (Files over 10,000 files); the local rig `HarnessRig` (deskd's relay and tools, the real desk-agent jar and desk-notebook, a stand-in or real model, every call counted), started by `tools/agent-harness/rig.sh`.
- **Added (`tools/agent-harness/`):** the capability suite (50 Files and 50 Notebook tasks, 90% and 0 policy violations per area; 100 of 100 on the stand-in), the soak runner, the stand-in model and their contract (`CONTRACT.md`).
- **Added (`web/tests/real-desk/`):** `rt-agent-tools.mjs`, the real-desk script per tool (cases per tool in `agent-tools/cases/`, a stand-in mode as the release record's), and `rt-agent-perf.mjs`, §17's client numbers at RTT 30, 70 and 90 ms and lossy; `agent-tools/NIGHTLY.md` proposes the nightly suite for the regression controller.
- **Registered since main moved (Nov 14):** Courier's four read tools, `desk_message` and `desk_answer`, and Desk Maia's `office_desks`, `office_desk_status`, `office_show` and `ask_member`: 47 tools. Threat 19 checks W6's envelopes on both sides (deskd `desks/ThreatsDeskdDesksTest`); threat 20's caps run with W3 on main.
- **Security's stand-in read: CLEAR**, its Lows and Infos fixed: a known matrix case must have exactly its known problem; threat 8's read-back is checked in a clean run; the rig answers only under a random path token; the fleet scripts probe the desk's lock file itself and can't be retargeted; the fleet key's last 4 is no longer in the repo.
- **Found:** an unasked call cut off by a kill showed nowhere the member looks (now settled by W3's rows and the resume); 24 cases end `interrupted` though the call ran, at a deskd restart or a desk stop (deskd's kept replies go with it); one step's cards list out of the step's order; `files_search` answers from its index while the tree is away; `browser.session.grant` keeps the granted site in the log (§7's origin; a question). Details and owners: `docs/sub-status/mm-harness.md`.

## 2026-11-14 - Maia Mode Phase 0, release 3 on staging (desk-notebook, deskd and desk-agent `2026.11.14-45b8173`, Desk app `2026.11.14-45b8173d`; not on the owner's desk or in prod)
- **Released to staging only:** main at `45b8173d`: release 2, the fix bundle, Courier read, the Browser tools' bar part 1 and the fixes for release 2's three bugs (`sub/mm-release-fixes`). Not in it: Desk Maia's runtime, the Maia Mode room, the Browser fixes (`172a7366`) and Courier parse, merged after it was built. On the TEST desk and a fleet account's Work and Personal desks; the Desk app is staging's stable. Record: `docs/sub-status/phase0-release-3.md`.
- **Proven with a real model ($6.29 at list price):** core's step 6 (a card drawn through a desk-agent restart) and step 5, the kill with a call in flight (done once, no `key_mismatch`), desk messages' wait across a restart, To-do's tile count, and auto-approve with Undo on a new agent's clean thread; release 2's passes re-checked. **Found:** an open window's stale card after a restart and its silence after a deskd restart (a reopen shows the truth), `desks.sent` audited twice, the New agent form's missing model, and a call after a running one in a step always asking.

## Unreleased - Release 3's six bugs and release 4's room bugs, fixed (`sub/mm-fix-bundle-2`, Maia Mode Phase 0 and 1; desk-agent, deskd and the Desk app; in no release)
- **Fixed: an open Agent window catches up live after a desk-agent kill** (release 3's bug A). A whole-thread read after a cut snapshot answers what the desk had when it was asked, and was put over the window, so a card that ended meanwhile went back to "Approved by you · Running". It's now merged: what an event touched while it was out stays, and a snapshot that came meanwhile means it's read again.
- **Fixed: an open Agent window hears its desk again after a deskd restart** (bug B). The window's new client subscribed while the old one's SUB was still on its way; deskd gives both the same subscription, and the old one's late UNSUB ended it. A topic left while its SUB is out is now kept, and a listener that comes back keeps it and gets a fresh snapshot.
- **Fixed: `desks.sent` is audited, and a grant's use counted, once per envelope** (bug C), when a resumed `desk_message` gets its envelope back by its key.
- **Fixed: New agent with a key picked makes the agent** (bug D): the form sends the key's first model (`agent.key.models`' good default) with it, as desk-agent needs both or neither. The mock now refuses a key alone, as the desk does.
- **Fixed: a write after a look-up that can't add untrusted content is done as the agent's switch says** (bug E). desk-agent asked for any call after a running one in a step; now only after one whose result can add `U` (`Approvals.NEVER_ADDS_UNTRUSTED` names those that never can: a Files listing, the plan, the workspace listing, a write's receipt). After a Notebook or To-do look-up, which deskd may mark, it still asks, as release 3's own case did. Security's FB2-L1 (`browser_use_my_session` can add `U`) fixed.
- **Fixed: `files_read`'s offset past a file's end is refused `offset`, not `path`** (bug F), so the model doesn't read a right path as wrong.
- **Fixed: a new member's first message in Maia Mode offers the key choice** (release 4's B1). A send the desk refused for want of a key (`no_key`) said only "That didn't reach Maia"; it now says "Maia needs one of your API keys on your home desk." with "Use {Lead}'s key", which binds the desk's Lead's key and model to her once however often it's pressed, then sends the refused messages in order (Security's FB2-I3 and FB2-I4).
- **Fixed: "Show my desks" and Alt+Shift+M put the keyboard focus back in the office** (B2). The focus was moved while the office was still `visibility: hidden` (React hadn't dropped `.mm-on` yet), so it fell to the page; it now lands once the office is visible.
- **Fixed: a member Maia Mode doesn't list fetches nothing of it** (B4). The startup chunk starts only once `/v1/me` lists the member (or their prefs keep it on, or an "off" is owed), not on client-config's switch alone, and the room's stylesheet is out of `ma-release.json`'s `css[]`, which the loader injects for everyone. Courier's message-frame stylesheet is out of `css[]` too: its `html` and `body` rules are for the sandboxed frame, not the office. Initial JS doesn't grow (255,914 B gzip; main 255,925).
- **Release:** desk-agent, deskd and the Desk app, in any order. Status: `docs/sub-status/mm-fix-bundle-2.md`.

## Unreleased - The Browser tools' bar, part 4: the memory cap counts what a page holds, and the bench (`sub/mm-browser-bar`, Maia Mode Phase 0, the draft ADR's §17 Browser row; deskd; in no release)
- **Fixed: the 300 MB cap no longer closes a tab for garbage.** deskd read a tab's committed V8 heap, which keeps what earlier pages left until V8 collects it: a heavy tab navigated within its site read up to 1,375 MB while holding 22 to 28 MB. A first look over the cap is now taken again after a full collection in each of the tab's renderer processes (`HeapProfiler.collectGarbage`, now on the Agent's CDP allow-list), and only a page still over it is closed. The collection pauses that renderer briefly, only then; in the member's session it can be one the member's own tabs of that site share.
- **Measured: the draft ADR's §17 Browser row** (`AgentBrowsingBench`, not a test, run by hand; numbers and what's open in `docs/sub-status/mm-browser-bar.md`): reads on 47 of the 58 corpus pages with this fix (46 before; 6 of the others refuse the build box itself); the MDoc 478 ms after load at p95; deskd's measure of a tab 283 MB at most, Chromium's whole cost up to 1,883 MB; the act 21 ms p50 and 44 ms p95 at most at RTT 30, 70 and 90 ms and lossy (145 ms p95 on a heavy page's navigation when a collection ran), and up to 207 ms p50 with the read it returns on a 5,000-element page; the member's page at 58 fps or more while the Agent browses, on 2 cores with no encoder.
- **Fixed (Security's part 4 read, BB-L6):** a process the look after the collection can't read in time keeps the first look's figure, so a heap too big to collect quickly is still closed.
- **Release:** deskd alone.

## Unreleased - Maia Mode's room (`sub/mm-maia-room`, Maia Mode Phase 1, CONCEPT.md Part 1 "What you see"; the Desk app; dark by default, in no release)
- **Added: Maia Mode,** the office as a quiet room with Maia in it, in the owner's pick A2: Maia in the middle, about three times her corner size and moving, hovering over the chat, whose oldest lines fade into her; the To-do column on the right with her status line and its foot line, collapsing with a chevron (or Alt+Shift+T) to a slim edge with the count and the "Now" dot, remembered for the member on every device; "Show my desks" and the member's avatar top right. `web/src/maiamode/`, its own lazy chunk and stylesheet; about 0.5 KB in the first load.
- **Dark by default, one account at a time** (ma-platform `desks.md` "Maia Mode"): only for a member `/v1/me` `maiaMode.enabled` lists, never a teen or Free, and nobody while client-config's `maiaMode` has neither `enabled` nor `listed`. Anyone else sees nothing new; their browser fetches only the small startup chunk (never the room's), while anyone is listed or where it kept Maia Mode on for the member it belongs to. When the gate closes on a member who had it on, the office turns it off on their home desk.
- **Turning it off** from the account menu goes to the home desk at once, and again from any device until a desk has it ("Maia Mode turns off on your home desk as soon as it’s awake."); "Delete Maia Mode history" deletes her chat, note log and choice counts there, after one question. The desk is the authority: a desk that says off closes the room.
- **Turning it on:** a "Maia Mode" switch in the account menu, apart from the themes, and a button in Support Maia's panel header. The first time, the disclosure (CONCEPT.md §9's draft, for the Sales Engineer to finalise), accepted with a checkbox. The desks fade and Maia grows from her corner. **Leaving:** "Show my desks" gives the office back exactly as it was (its camera, windows and focus). Alt+Shift+M goes either way.
- **The viewport:** the real app from one of the member's own desks, live, from a closed list of apps and never a URL; it slides over the right at about two thirds of the width, To-do folds into pills with "Now" first, and Maia and the chat move left. "Open on my desk" leaves for that desk with the app open; Esc closes it.
- **Her choices:** 2 to 4 short answers, her pick first, plus "Other", by pointer, keyboard (Left and Right) and screen reader ("…, her pick"), and full-width rows in thumb reach on a phone.
- **The startup chooser** on the loading overlay (ADR 0058's amendment): "Open your office in: Maia Mode or Desk Mode?", the last used on Enter, with "Ask every time I open my office", which follows the member; only for members who turned Maia Mode on and accepted its disclosure. A page that opens in Maia Mode never mounts the canvas.
- **The phone:** one column, Maia at the top, To-do a pill in the top bar that opens a sheet, the viewport a sheet at 88 % of the height.
- **Light, Zen (over the member's office picture) and Dark,** from tokens (`MAIA_MODE` in `tokens.ts`); no green, brown or warm tint, and "Now" is the accent blue and weight.
- **Desk Maia** is reached over the home desk's own data channel through the runtime's contract (deskd API.md §23, `sub/mm-maia-runtime`): her thread, the room's state stream, what the office observes, choices, notes' Undo, snooze, on and off. Nothing goes to the platform. Until a home desk carries the runtime, the room says it's connecting.

## Unreleased - Courier: desk-parse's Courier mode and the vault (`sub/mm-courier-parse`, the Courier launch plan's Waves 1.2, 1.3 and 0.4; deskd, desk-agent's image, two new modules; in no release)
- **Added: `desk-parse`'s Courier mode** (`services/courier-parse`, its README). Untrusted mail becomes safe, structured parts, only inside ADR 0097's sandbox (uid 1113, no network, no `/data`, 160 MiB, `-Xmx72m`), as `java @/app/courier-parse.options` from desk-agent's image. deskd still never parses mail.
  - **What it reads:** MIME (mime4j, lenient), headers in every encoding, charsets including UTF-7, and `format=flowed`.
  - **HTML, sanitized twice:** an allow-list rebuild, then a re-parse that must serialize to the same string and pass every rule again, or the HTML is dropped and the text kept.
  - **Inert HTML:** links carry `data-ma-href` with tracking parameters removed, warnings and the real destination, and no `href`. Remote images carry `data-ma-src` and no `src`.
  - **Tracking pixels removed:** by ADR 0076's lists, by size, by being hidden, or by path.
  - **Images drawn again** from their pixels, with size checks before decoding.
  - **Archives listed** without unpacking.
  - **`danger` named** by the real extension and the bytes: `program`, `script`, `macros`, `shortcut`, `disk_image`, `archive_programs`, `html`, `archive_unread`.
  - **Limits:** 2,000 parts, nesting 20, 40 MiB, 10 s, each a refusal.
  - **The wire's other requests:** a part's bytes, an image, and an archive's list.
- **Added: deskd's `CourierSandbox`,** wired as Courier's parser (`DESKD_COURIER_PARSE_SOCKET`; `run` mounts `/run/desk-parse-courier` read-only and `/data/courier` where the image has them). One request at a time, a 20 s deadline (past `desk-svc`'s 15 s kill, so a silent worker never gets the next request), answers read with a token cap, and `err busy` tried again. With no sandbox, mail waits as `pending` and nothing is lost. A refusal, a timeout or a bad answer is a clean refusal, never a crash (API.md §21, the draft ADR §4).
- **Added: the Courier vault** (`courier/vault/`, the draft ADR §9). Linked mailboxes' sign-ins (passwords, app passwords, OAuth tokens) are sealed in ADR 0042's format in `/data/courier/keys`, with the `accounts` row as the commit point. They fail closed on any change to a sealed file.
  - **`courier.accounts.add`** (write-only; checked with the provider by the transport, so `unavailable` `{reason: medium}` until Wave 2.1 registers its verifier).
  - **`courier.accounts.remove`,** and `courier.accounts.list` gains `signedIn`. Never in a log or an answer, never to the platform.
- **Added: Courier's test kit** (`services/courier-testkit`, Wave 0.4): a deterministic MIME corpus of 5,155 messages, 351 hostile HTML vectors, and a fake IMAP and SMTP server (GreenMail) for Wave 2.1.
- **Changed: `desk-data` leaves `courier/keys` out of a download** (Security CP-L3), so a download never holds a linked mailbox's master key beside its sealed sign-ins. The tick to include them and a move's carrying them are Wave 3.3's.
- **Changed: a message with more than one From** (two fields, or two mailboxes in one) is refused `malformed`, so a DMARC verdict is never shown beside an address it didn't check (Security CP-L2).
- **desk-agent's image** gains `/app/courier-parse.options`, `/app/courier/trackers.txt` (an empty placeholder) and courier-parse's jars; its build parses a message through it. desk-agent's own behaviour doesn't change.
- **The bar:**
  - **Corpus:** 5,155 of 5,155 read or cleanly refused, through the socket into the store too.
  - **Fuzz:** 10 million mutated inputs with 0 crashes and 0 escapes; deskd's side fed hostile answers.
  - **Browser:** a real Chromium with scripts on and no CSP showed every sanitized document with 0 scripts run and 0 requests.
  - **The real sandbox on Docker:** every limit held (peak 107 of 160 MiB, the slowest 3.7 s on one CPU).
  - **The vault:** the round trip, tamper and audit canary tests pass.
- **Not yet (the System Engineer's, written in `docs/sub-status/mm-courier-parse.md`):** the Courier sandbox's unit and socket, the tracker list at bake, and `/data/courier` in `desk-datadirs`. Until then deskd finds no socket and mail waits.

## Unreleased - Desk Maia: Maia Mode Phase 1's desk side (`sub/mm-maia-runtime`; desk-agent and deskd; dark by default, the owner's alone first; in no release)
- **Added: Desk Maia** (the draft ADR [Desk Maia](docs/decisions/draft-desk-maia.md); deskd `API.md` §23): Maia Mode's Maia as an agent of desk-agent on the member's home desk, id `maia`, on the member's own key and limits. Her thread is an agent thread (`agent.conversation:maia`); she isn't a team agent (not listed, counted, linked or proposed), and no agent may message her.
- **Dark by default, listed desks only, with a kill switch:** nothing runs until the member turns it on with the disclosure (`agent.maia.set`, a session that may act), and only on a desk its switch lists: `/data/agent/maia-switch` saying `on` (or `DESK_AGENT_MAIA=on`); with neither, `forbidden` `not_listed` (`{maia}.listed`). The same file saying `off` (or `DESK_AGENT_MAIA=off`) turns it off at once. Turning it off revokes every grant and stops her runs. Teens never; a Free tier (refused by deskd itself) or a Guild Desk refuse it.
- **Her chats go at 90 days:** her messages, their approvals and runs, her reply extras and note log, every 10 minutes and at start; held notes at 7 days.
- **The room's state stream, topic `agent.maia`:** the status line, the To-do short list and spend today, all code (`maia/Attention`): at most 3 items and one Now, urgency only by the fixed rules (an approval expiring within the hour, a task stopped by a limit, a failed desk, spend at 80% of her cap, a to-do due within 2 hours), merged per desk, batched every 30 minutes or on return, nothing new while the member types, an interrupt at most once every 15 minutes and never a sound. The office sends what it sees with `agent.maia.observe`, kept in RAM only.
- **Her tools:** `office_desks`, `office_desk_status`, `office_show` (an app and an id, never a URL), `ask_member` (2 to 4 choices and Other), the home desk's To-do and `desk_message` under her grants; nothing that approves, sees a key, reaches another person, deletes, pays or changes her own limits or grants.
- **Briefs under grants:** `agent.maia.grant.set` (scopes `brief`, `show`; 7 days, at most 30; "keeps to itself"), kept by desk-agent and recorded by deskd itself (`agent/MaiaRecord`). Without a live `brief` grant a brief is refused `maia_grant` before anyone is asked; under one it goes without a card, unless her run read a private answer from a desk that keeps to itself (deskd's desk origins, desk-agent's `D:` flags), when it asks. A brief from a run holding private data is `private` in its envelope: the answering run starts with `S`, so its browsing asks; its answer back to her desk stays `own` unless a tool there gave it that desk's private data (Security's DMA-M1, DMA-L5).
- **Multiple-choice replies with the member's weight** (the owner's ESSENTIAL ask): counts only, on the home desk, reset with `agent.maia.choices.reset`; a tap is `agent.maia.choose`.
- **Notes (use case A):** a clean note-taker reads only the member's own messages; code files them in the From Maia tab without asking under her switch (`Approvals.admitUnasked`: never a card), with "Noted for Work · Undo" on her reply; unsure notes are asked about once, by code.
- **To-dos from Courier (use case B):** a reader with no tools, then code's checks (in the batch, plain, no link or Markdown, a real date, the day's cap) and `todo_add` with a Courier source deskd checks. Off until the member switches it on for an agent.
- **The team view:** `agent.usage.summary` on every desk, counts and dollars per agent per day.
- **Tests:** desk-agent `maia/*` (flow, rules, attention, the injection and coordinator evals on a scripted stand-in model), deskd `MaiaRecordTest`, `MaiaRelayTest`, `DeskToolsMaiaTest`, and over the real sockets `RealDeskAgentMaiaIT` and `RealDeskAgentMaiaBriefsIT`. Record: `docs/sub-status/mm-maia-runtime.md`.
- **Release order:** deskd with or before desk-agent (an older deskd answers the new methods `not_found` and has no `MaiaRecord`, so it refuses nothing of hers), then the room's Desk app. Every desk she may brief needs this release too (an older one drops a brief's `private`). Phase 1 lists the owner's desk only: the rollout writes `on` to its `/data/agent/maia-switch`.

## Unreleased - The Browser tools' bar, part 3: the CDP recording and the corpus's fixes (`sub/mm-browser-bar`, Maia Mode Phase 0, the draft ADR's §17 Browser row; deskd; in no release)
- **Proven: nothing deskd sends Chromium clears or writes storage, cookies, cache or history, on any path.** The real-Chromium ITs (`AgentBrowsingChromiumIT`, `ChromiumIT`, `MinifiedChromiumIT`) record every CDP command deskd sends (`CdpClient.Tap`, a test's hook that is never set on a desk; `AgentCdp` marks its own) and, as each test ends, find: no command in a storage, network, cookie or cache domain but a read (`Storage.getCookies` for `data.browser.cookies`), no history, cache or cookie writer; every command an Agent path sent on its allow-list; no other command naming an Agent session, target or context; and no address in any log line of the Browser's or the tools' packages, at any level. `CdpRecorderTest` shows each of these fires.
- **Fixed: a tab the 300 MB cap closed answers `too_large` (`memory`) to any later call, and to the call it was closed under,** never `not_found`: a heavy page whose memory passed the cap while it loaded was closed by the 30 s watch, and `browser_open` or the next `browser_read` said there was no such tab, so a model would open it again. Only the run's own tab says so; another run's id is still `not_found`.
- **Fixed: a command racing the end of what it names fails as gone, not as deskd's bug.** A frame that detached between deskd enabling its page and letting it run got `AgentCdp.NotAllowed` (an uncaught exception on the frame's thread, and a failed tool call on one page): the allow-list refused the command because the session was no longer the Agent's. `AgentCdp` now answers a command refused only for naming a session, target or context that was the Agent's and just went (`AgentCdp.Scope.went`, the last 4,096) as Chromium answers one that's gone; nothing is sent either way, and anything else (off the list, a script, a parameter it may never say, an id that was never the Agent's) is still refused as a bug.
- **Release:** deskd alone.

## Unreleased - The Browser tools' bar, part 2: caps, the freeze and no fixed wait (`sub/mm-browser-bar`, Maia Mode Phase 0, the draft ADR's §17 Browser row; deskd; in no release)
- **Fixed (gap 4): the member's-session tabs count against the desk's cap.** The cap of 2 Agent tabs (1 on a free desk) counted only the Agent's own context's, so a run could add 2 signed-in tabs on top, and each run its own. Every Agent tab counts now; `browser_use_my_session` is refused `tab_cap` before anyone is asked when its site's tab would be a new one past the cap.
- **Fixed (gap 6): a freeze no longer closes an Agent's tabs mid-run.** The freeze (ADR 0045) closes deskd's connection to Chromium, and every hidden Agent tab and context with it, so a card the member answered more than a minute later, with the Browser window closed, found its page gone, typed text and all. A run's open tabs now hold off the freeze for 10 minutes after its last Browser call (`BrowserFreeze.Tabs.agentHolds`, `AgentBrowsing.holdsChromium`); a run's end lets it come at once, and an Agent call still thaws a frozen Chromium first.
- **Added (gap 7): 300 MB an Agent tab.** deskd reads a tab's memory with `Runtime.getHeapUsage` (a read-only method, now on the Agent's CDP allow-list) over the tab and its out-of-process frames: the V8 heap committed, Blink's heap and array buffers. A tab past 300 MB is closed, at the call that finds it (`too_large`, reason `memory`) or by a look every 30 s between calls while there are Agent tabs and Chromium isn't frozen.
- **Changed (gap 5): no fixed 300 ms wait after a click, a key or Back.** The wait is Chromium's own signs: a navigation the act or its script asked for in the tab (`Page.frameRequestedNavigation`, which comes before the act's reply), its start, a move within the document or a refusal at the request, then its load. A link, a submit or Back gets up to 1 s to show a first sign. A navigation the page starts later on a timer isn't waited for (its requests still stop at deskd), and the next read shows it. The Chromium IT went from about 19 s to 13 s, and passed 15 runs in a row, 10 headless and 5 headful.
- **Proven: no context leak after 1,000 runs** (`AgentBrowsingLeakIT`, real headless Chromium 1248): each run opens a page that shows its cookies and local storage, finds nothing of an earlier run's or the member's, stores its own, reads them back in its own context and ends; every 100 runs Chromium's own list of contexts holds none of the Agent's, deskd holds no Agent tab and the member's cookies are unchanged. Two runs at once see only their own. Chromium's resident memory was 1,126 MB at run 100 and 1,040 MB after run 1,000. 3 min 35 s on the build box.
- **Fixed (Security's part 2 read):** an act that was done is answered as done, with what followed it (`stopped: off_site` or `blocked`, `closed: memory`, `view: unknown`), never as a failure that would have a member approve the same send or spend again (BB-L5; an off-site redirect after a click used to fail the click). The memory watch survives a frame or a tab going under it (BB-L4), and a renderer process shared by several of a tab's frames is counted once, by `Runtime.getIsolateId` (BB-I4, also on the allow-list, read only).
- **Release:** deskd alone.

## Unreleased - Release 2's three bugs, fixed (`sub/mm-release-fixes`, Maia Mode Phase 0; desk-agent, deskd and the Desk app; for release 3)
- **Fixed: the Agent window draws a waiting card after a reload or a desk-agent restart.** API.md §18 says the conversation snapshot and `agent.thread.get` carry `approvals`, every approval of the messages in that page, and the Agent app draws its cards from there; desk-agent only put each approval in its reply, so after a reload, a reopened window or any restart a card waiting on the desk was never drawn and the member could only press Stop. desk-agent now sends the page's `approvals` beside each reply's own (counted twice in the 1 MiB page; the newest reply alone, too big, sends both copies without payloads, `payloadOmitted`, as an event over 32 KiB does). deskd's `EventFit` keeps a snapshot's approvals with the messages it keeps (an approval of a message it leaves out goes too) and, for the newest message alone, drops payloads before anything else. The Agent app also reads each reply's own `approvals` (`pageApprovals`, by id, the page's copy first), so the card shows with any desk-agent, the owner's release 4 included, once the Desk app is promoted. The mock desk sends both, or each reply's only with `?approvalsWire=messages`; `agent.reload.shell.spec.ts` reloads with a card waiting in both shapes and opens the window again on a fresh subscription.
- **Fixed: a call left in flight by a desk-agent restart is no longer refused `key_mismatch` when asked again.** deskd's key binds the call's `context` (`ToolHost.fingerprint`), which desk-agent took from its policy's memory of the look; a step picked up after a restart isn't classified again, so the resend went without it, and a write that ran read "didn't work" while desk messages' durable wait was never taken (the answer went to the thread and the reply said none came). `ToolPolicy.resumed` now gives each checkpointed call its context back before the step runs, and a resend refused `key_mismatch` ends `interrupted` ("may have happened"), never `failed`. The rig's deskd and the desk-messages test deskd now keep replies by key with deskd's fingerprint (`testing/KeyFingerprint`) and refuse another call under a key, so `ResumeKillTest` (8 cases failed before the fix) and `DeskMessagesFlowTest`'s new kill of the sending desk while its message waits reproduce it.
- **Fixed: To-do's tile shows its count on a real desk.** The office waited for To-do's `badge` on a `summary` topic deskd doesn't serve (§5); the count is §22's `todo.summary`. `desk/todoBadge.ts` reads it on each `todo` topic event (its snapshot included, so after every reconnect) and again just after the desk's midnight in its zone, never on a Guild Desk's panel or with To-do's kill switch off. The mock desk no longer puts To-do's count in `summary`, and its `todo` topic sends its snapshot over an open session, as deskd does.

## Unreleased - The Browser tools' bar, part 1: the class is deskd's, never the model's (`sub/mm-browser-bar`, Maia Mode Phase 0, the draft ADR's §17 Browser row; deskd; in no release)
- **Fixed: a form's submit is `send` whatever its label.** A click's class was judged by the control's words alone, so a form's submit control labelled "Next", "Save", "Go" or an icon (a `<button>` in a form submits by default) was a `read` in the Agent's own context and a `write` in the member's session. deskd now asks its own half on the element itself before anyone is asked (`agentClick`'s look, `AgentBrowser.clickLook`), and a submit is `send`; a GET form holding a search box stays a search, as Enter in that box is (a `read` in the Agent's own context, `send` in the member's session). The payload binds `submit: true`, and a click that submits a form without the member's yes for one is refused `changed`, by deskd's tools and again by the action proxy at the click (`ActionProxy.Request.submit`, `proxy.js`'s `a.submit`). Where the element says it goes, a form's address included, is checked before asking (`off_site`, `blocked`).
- **Fixed (Security's BB-L1): Enter is judged by what its form submits,** by deskd's half on the field's form (its implicit submission and default button), not by the field's kind alone: Enter in a search box whose form posts is `send`, never done unasked, and Enter in a field whose form's button says "Place order" is `spend`, by the button's own words (its text or `aria-label`, an image input's `alt`), never its `value` (BB-L3). A look moves nothing: no click, focus or scroll before anyone is asked (BB-I2), and a click's look follows what the browser would activate: a span inside a submit button, a submit button inside a link, a label's submit control, and a link around any other control (BB-I1, BB-I3).
- **Fixed (Security's BB-L2): an approval deskd didn't redeem never reaches a tool.** `ToolHost` drops one carried by a call whose class doesn't ask, so an area's "approved" always means the member's redeemed yes, never desk-agent's word.
- **Fixed: the model can't set `spend` or `submit`.** `browser_click` took `spend: true` from any call's arguments and passed it to the action proxy, which then let a click on a page with a payment provider's frame through; now only an approved call's arguments, which are the card's payload, count.
- **Fixed: a control is judged by deskd's last read, never by the model's words.** An expanded ref (`{node, fp, block, rev}`) for a node the last read didn't show took its `label` and `role` from the model's own arguments, which then decided the class (a pay button called "Next", a link). It's `not_found` now unless an approval bound them.
- No change to any card's words or the Agent app. **Release:** deskd alone.

Not in it: the fix bundle (`f640853c`) and Courier read (`71c1bf59`), merged after the build. The prod Desk app still goes to prod before this desk-agent reaches a member's desk.

## Unreleased - The desk Agent's Courier read tools (`sub/mm-courier-read`, Maia Mode Phase 0, W5, the draft ADR's §12; deskd, desk-agent and the Agent app's words; in no release)
- **Added: `courier_list`, `courier_search`, `courier_read` and `courier_new`** (deskd's `tools/CourierTools` over Courier's `CourierRead`, merged at `46fe6a11`), offered to the desk's agents only while Courier's store is there and on, and only to an agent whose Courier switch the member turned on (off by default, §16). Read only: **no send tool of any kind**. Wire: `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §21, "The agent's Courier tools".
- **Every result is `U` and `S`,** fenced as "Courier, the member's messages and email, written by other people", so from the first Courier call every browse action, memory save and desk message asks, and §15's auto-approve stops for all but adding a to-do. Senders are Courier's contact record with `senderKnown` and `senderConfirmed`; Requests, Spam and Trash are never read; `courier_new` never gives bulk mail or the member's own; text is plain (links as their domain, quotes folded, never HTML), attachments named only; every string a sender wrote is cleaned again of hidden and bidi characters (Security's Q6 condition).
- **Each result reaches the model whole:** fitted to 18,000 characters as desk-agent's fence carries it (whole rows; a page's `next` moved back; one message too long alone cut with `textNext`), as the run loop cuts every result at 20,000.
- **`courier_new`'s cursor** moves only at a `run_end` of `done`, to the last cursor desk-agent received (`run_end`'s new `args: {state, courier}`), never after a reply it lost; a lost reply asked again by its key gets the same messages; an agent's first look starts 24 hours back. desk-agent sends the state from `AgentService.finish`.
- **To-do's provenance** takes a Courier source only from a message `courier_read` or `courier_new` gave the run whole (not a search's hit), labelled "Email from {contact} · {subject}", or by the address with "(unconfirmed)" for a sender Courier couldn't confirm.
- **Fixed in Courier's store:** `courier_new`'s read uses the cursor's index (`INDEXED BY messages_received`); with the lanes' index SQLite read and sorted every row of the lanes (about 60 ms a call at 50,000 messages).
- **The bar (§17's Courier row):** a soak driver (7 simulated days, wakes, catch-ups, parser outages, let-ins, Spam and back, clock steps, deskd and desk-agent restarts, lost replies): 0 misses and 0 repeats at 10,000 messages; the MIME corpus read or cleanly refused through the tools; a 50,000-message store drained exactly once; 100 new messages and a search well inside 500 and 300 ms p95 on x86_64. `docs/sub-status/mm-courier-read.md` has the numbers, what the real 7-day soak and real desks need (the image's `/data/courier` and a transport), Security's stand-in read (CR-L1 to CR-L3, CR-I1, CR-I2, all fixed) and the brand review (words).
- **Release order:** deskd with or before desk-agent. An older desk-agent sends no `run_end` args, so a cursor never moves (repeats, never a skip).

## Unreleased - The fix bundle (`sub/mm-fix-bundle`, Maia Mode Phase 0's follow-ups; the Desk app, deskd and desk-agent; not shipped)
- **Changed: no green, amber or brown status colour anywhere** (the owner's rule). `status.online` is each theme's accent blue and `status.warn` its strong cool neutral (Light the kit's graphite, Zen and Dark their light ink), in Light, Zen and Dark (`web/src/theme/tokens.ts`; the kit's `ui/tokens.json` and `contrast.txt`). So an agent online, or waiting on another of the member's desks, has a blue dot; one that needs you, a graphite or near-white one; an approved card's check, "Applied" and a diff's insertion are blue; the warn banners, a meter near its limit, the send and spend edge, the bandwidth ring and Commons' speaking ring follow; the simplified view's rating star takes warn's ink, never gold. A desk's warn dot on the canvas ("reconnecting…", "needs you", "going to sleep") is the desk's own ink, as its words are, so it holds on every desk style. Red stays, for a real failure only. Agent identity tones (teal, plum and the rest) and the member avatar tints are unchanged (the owner, Nov 12); the Product Designer has the list (`docs/sub-status/mm-fix-bundle.md`).
- **To-do:** a tab's count holds 4.5:1 in every theme (it was 4.48:1 in Light on an unselected tab), and the quiet lines bold their counts as the pick sheet does ("**1 new** from agents · **1** to confirm", "Everything else: **6** coming up · …"). An item made from another desk's message reads "Made from a message another of your desks sent." (one "from", not two). Its History says which an agent asked: "asked if it's still needed" and "answered “Not needed any more?”" for a not-needed suggestion (it said "done" for both).
- **desk-agent:** a To-do closed as not needed says "Marked a to-do not needed" on its chip (it said "Marked a to-do done"), and a suggestion of it "Asked if a to-do is still needed".
- **deskd (security hardening, no member-visible change):** To-do's undo by approval id always names the agent and takes back only that agent's events; the two older forms without the check (`TodoTools.undo`, the one-argument `TodoStore.undoByApproval`), which nothing called, are removed.

## Unreleased - Durable runs' resume: runs that survive a desk-agent restart, approvals and waits with them (`sub/mm-durable-resume`; desk-agent, deskd and the Agent app; Maia Mode Phase 0, Design 1; in no release)
- **Added (desk-agent):** a run picks up after a restart (a kill, a rollout, the desk's stop) from its last checkpoint in `agent.db`: its start context once, each step's plan before anything of it is asked or runs, the gate's verdicts, each call's result with its record, its taint and gate counts with each (never reset). A call left in flight is asked again by its key: deskd's kept result, exactly once; a key deskd no longer holds (deskd restarted, the desk stopped) ends it `interrupted`, and its card says so, never run again; a desk-agent tool's ends `interrupted`, a read `failed`. At most 2 MiB a run; past it, a run with attachments, a broken checkpoint or the kill switch (`/data/agent/resume-switch` saying `off`), it ends `interrupted` at the restart as before. The reply says it picked up (`resumed`), audited `run.resume`.
- **With auto-approve:** a call done without asking keeps its own row and stays unasked when its step is admitted again after a restart; the run's unasked count (20 a run) and deskd's refused payloads carry over.
- **Changed (desk-agent):** a pending card survives a desk-agent restart, still answerable, until its own time; it no longer expires `restart`. SIGTERM pauses runs (ADR 0020's `paused`) instead of stopping them. A timer whose run resumes stays `running` and settles as its run ends.
- **Added (desk-agent):** `run.RunWaits`, a call's wait on something outside its run, kept with its first deadline and any answer across a restart; the agent shows `statusDetail: "waiting_<kind>"` meanwhile. Desk messages' 120 s wait (§14) is kept through it (`run.DurableDeskWaits` in place of `DeskWaits.Memory`): an answer that comes while the desk-agent is down is the resumed call's result, and one a run never read goes to the thread. A run answering another desk or agent never resumes.
- **Changed (deskd):** an agent `working` with `statusDetail` `waiting_<kind>` doesn't keep the desk awake, as `awaiting_approval` doesn't.
- **Added (the Agent app):** a reply that picked up says "Picked up where it left off after the desk restarted."; a chip cut off by a restart reads "Didn’t finish" and "It may have happened: check {target} before you ask again."; a card whose ticket a deskd restart lost says "The desk restarted before this ran, so nothing was done."
- **Schema:** `runs`, `run_steps`, `run_results` and `run_waits` in `agent.db` (additive, `user_version` stays 1), and `messages.resumed` added once by `ALTER TABLE`; a rollback still opens the file, and checkpoints whose reply an older desk-agent ended are dropped at the next open.
- **Docs:** the draft ADR's §3 ("Restarts"), §9, §10 (threat 10), §17 and Consequences; ADR 0020's amendment (the states as built); `API.md` §18 ("Runs across a restart"); desk-agent's README; web/agent's README. Status: `docs/sub-status/mm-durable-resume.md`.
- **Tests:** `tool/ResumeKillTest` (176 kill cases), `tool/ResumePropertyTest` (1,000 seeded runs with random decisions and faults), `tool/ResumeTest` (7), deskd `agent/ApprovalRelayTest` (a durable wait isn't work), web/agent `model/resume.test.ts`.

## Unreleased - To-do, the app (`sub/mm-todo-app`, Maia Mode Phase 0, W4's member UI; the Desk app; on by default, `todo: {enabled: false}` in client-config is its kill switch)
- **Added: To-do on every personal and work desk** (the owner named it, Nov 12; id `todo`; the product design is the To-do VISION, look A, Focus, its default while no look is picked). A tile goes on each desk quietly at the first free place, as Files' does (`apps/tiles.ts`, now both apps'; never on a Guild Desk's panel), and opens the To-do window: **Needs you** (only what's overdue, due today or kept there, five at most and "+N more", then one quiet line "2 new from agents · 1 to confirm", To confirm with "Looks done?" Done and Not yet, and "Everything else"), **All** (Overdue, Today, a heading a day, No date, Snoozed folded; search; From Courier, You and each agent, with what that agent did today), **Done** (by the day each closed, who closed it, Reopen) and **Trash** (30 days, Restore). One to-do whole: title and note, the date, Snooze (later today, tomorrow morning, next week, a day you pick), Keep in Needs you, where it came from with Open (in that desk's own app), who made and changed it, the untrusted line, and History with Undo on every change. Every change says itself once in the shell's live region with Undo at the foot. Export as a Markdown checklist or a JSON file, over the desk's `bulk`.
- **All desks**, for a member with more than one desk: each desk's list put together in the browser in Needs you's own order, each item with its desk; a sleeping desk says so with "Open it". Nothing of it touches a server.
- **The tile's count** is overdue plus due today, on the existing badge path (`summary`), in the accent blue, said as "To-do, 2 due"; To-do's menu turns it off (`todoTileCount` in the layout document's `prefs`, so it follows the member).
- **The Agent app** (`web/agent`) has the To-do tools' cards and chips in §22's words ("Add “…” to To-do", "Change “…” in To-do", "Mark “…” done in To-do" or "not needed", a due date in words; the chips in desk-agent's words for what each call did: "Added a to-do", "Merged into a to-do", "Already on your to-dos", "Asked if a to-do is done"…), and a `same_as` add's card names the item it merges into (`target.mergeInto`). "Looks done?" says when its run had read an email or a page (`suggestion.untrusted`).
- **The wire** is deskd's `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §22, as merged (`sub/mm-todo-store`): `apps/todo/client.ts` is the adapter (one `idem` across a lost reply, a stale `rev` sent again with only the member's fields), `wire.ts` reads every reply field by field, `feed.ts` follows the `todo` topic and catches up with `todo.changes`. The mock desk (`desk/mockDeskTodo.ts`) serves §22 for dev and the shell tests. A deskd before §22 gets "This desk's To-do needs an update."
- **The glyph** is a draft in the final 3D family's finish (the VISION's in-tray), a chunk of its own (`apps/todo/glyph3d.ts`, loaded with the first To-do tile), for the Product Designer to finish.
- **Bytes:** the first load +0.5 KB (254,608 B of 256,000); the window's own chunk about 17 KB gzipped.
- **Not built here:** the trusted-device cache and its queue (the office has no trusted device yet), "Undo today's" for an agent (§22 has no such call), the provider badge on an agent's name, the Agent app's chips opening the item in To-do, push or sound reminders (not at launch). Security's stand-in read: CLEAR, its Lows fixed. `docs/sub-status/mm-todo-app.md`.

## Unreleased - Auto-approve for low-risk writes: an agent's "without asking" switches act (`sub/mm-auto-approve`, Maia Mode Phase 0, the draft ADR's §15, work breakdown W3; desk-agent, deskd, desk-notebook and the Agent app; in no release)
- **Added (the owner, Nov 12: auto-approve for low-risk writes, chosen per agent at setup; asking stays the default):** with a switch the member turned on for an agent, it adds pages and notes in one Notebook tab, or saves new files and folders in one folder, with no card. Nothing else is ever done unasked: no delete, send, spend, egress, member's sign-in, replace, move or rename; nothing after the run read a web page, a file or a message (the To-do tools' own exception is W4's); nothing on a Guild Desk; nothing the member declined in that run; at most 20 a run and 100 an agent a day.
- **Checked twice:** desk-agent's gate (the group, the class, the place, the taint, the counts) and deskd from its own record of the switch and its place, its own class, taint copy and ledger (`/data/desk/agent-auto.jsonl`: each approval id once, the counts reserved before each call, what each call made). Where the write happens, desk-notebook checks the page's tab in its queue and Files checks the path part by part with no link followed. A refusal is `approval_unknown`, and the same call asks on a card if the model tries again.
- **Every unasked call shows:** its own approval row (`decidedBy: "auto"`, `auto`, `undoneAt`) in `agent.approvals.list`, its chip ("Done without asking · Undo"), the reply's `auto` and the thread's line ("Did 3 things without asking: … · Undo all · See them"), and the audit (`approval.auto`, `approval.undo`, ids and hashes only).
- **Undo** (`agent.approval.undo`, a session that may act): deskd takes the call back itself, from its own ledger: a file to Files' trash while it's still what the agent wrote, an empty folder, an append's blocks while unchanged (else the page from before, in History), a new page to the Trash. Undo all is one call per row.
- **"Don't ask again"** on a card in the class turns the switch on through `agent.access.set` from the member's session, named in words ("Save new files in {folder} without asking"), then approves the card; never through a decision.
- **Fixed (the test desk's release check, Nov 13):** a page's Undo of an agent's edit takes back the newest agent edit of the page first, each to exactly the page before it (its `pre_agent` version). After an append and then a replace on one page, the foot's Undo had taken the append out from under the replace, which stayed. desk-notebook now refuses an older edit's undo while a newer one stands (`conflict` `newer`); the Notebook app's foot reads the page's edits again and says to undo that one first; the Agent app's Undo all goes newest first.
- **Security:** a stand-in read of the merged wiring before the build (AAW-L1 to L4, AAW-I1 to I6, all folded in), and of this branch before its merge (AAB-L1, AAB-I1 to I6, fixed: what a save takes away holds in deskd's record before desk-agent hears, whatever it answers, and a delete forgets the agent as it goes; deskd's ledger line is kept before an unasked call runs, or nothing runs, and one it never saw end answers `unknown`; a call cut off by a restart shows "May have been done", with Undo; a tab whose title another shares is named with its notebook; desk-notebook's newest-first check comes after its grant and Trash checks).
- **To-do (W4) under W3's counts:** an unasked to-do call goes through deskd's ledger like every group's (each id once, 20 a run, 100 an agent a day: Security's TD-I3 from To-do's read), and `agent.approval.undo` reaches To-do's own undo by its approval id. `docs/sub-status/mm-auto-approve.md` has both.
- **Brand review:** words only. A failed or never-done chip says "Tried without asking", not "Done"; the `newer` refusal says "Undo the later change first"; a tab whose title another shares is named "Work › Inbox", as Notebook's Search names it; Activity names a `newer` refusal.
- **Release order:** desk-notebook, then deskd, with or before desk-agent, and the Desk app with desk-agent: an older deskd refuses every unasked call (`bad_request` `autoApprovalId` or `approval_unknown`), so nothing runs unasked; with an older desk-notebook, whose dry run says no `tabId`, deskd refuses every unasked append (`approval_unknown`, then a card), and its undo isn't newest first (the test desk's bug stays until desk-notebook ships). An older Desk app shows no auto rows' Undo and no `newer` words; it still works.

## Unreleased - To-do: the store and the agent's To-do tools (`sub/mm-todo-store`; deskd and desk-agent; Maia Mode Phase 0, W4; on, with a kill switch; in no release)
- **Added (the owner named it To-do, Nov 12; the To-do VISION's Part 2):** `services/deskd/.../todo/`: `/data/desk/todo.db` (SQLite, WAL, `synchronous=FULL`, one writer thread): items, their sources, events (each change's fields before and after, the history and Undo), the desk-wide `rev` and change log, each member mutation's result by its `idem` for 30 days (a trusted device's queue can replay after a restart), and search. Dates are the member's, in the account's time zone. Retention: closed items a year, the Trash 30 days. A Guild Desk has none; a teen's desk has it, with the Agent off.
- **The member's `todo.*` and the topic `todo`** (`services/deskd/docs/API.md` §22): `list` (Needs you, open, done, Trash; by maker, source, words), `get`, `summary` (the tile's count), `changes`, `add`, `update` (by `rev`), `close`, `reopen`, `snooze`, `pin`, `answer` (a suggestion), `trash`, `restore`, `undo`, `seen`, and `export` (Markdown or JSON on `bulk`). The topic says `{op, id, rev}`, ids only, within 100 ms of a commit. The contract the To-do app (`sub/mm-todo-app`) builds to.
- **The agent's `todo_list`, `todo_get`, `todo_add`, `todo_update` and `todo_close`** (deskd's `tools/TodoTools` through `ToolHost`; desk-agent's declarations; scopes `todo:read` and `todo:write`; restart rules: reads safe, writes by key). deskd decides each call from its own facts: the item's maker and run, its copy of the run's taint, and whether the member started the run (its own record of the `agent.message.send` it relayed). An add, and a change or close of the agent's own item, is in the `todos` auto-approve group (unasked when the member's switch is on); anyone else's item always asks, and from a run nobody attended an update of it is refused `not_yours`; a close the agent may not make is a suggestion ("Looks done?") for the member's tap, never a card (`todo_close`'s floor is `own`, and only a close rises to `write`).
- **Provenance:** a source must be one this run was given by a tool deskd hosts (`tools/Provenance`, recorded in deskd's run ledger from deskd's own results: Courier messages, Notebook pages, URLs read, Files paths) or the member's message that started the run; else `source_unknown`. Labels are deskd's from that record, never the model's. **Security's ATA-L1 fixed:** after untrusted content, an add is unasked only with a third party's source the run read (a Courier message or a page).
- **Security (Nov 13, a stand-in read; fixed before the merge):** a suggestion from a run that read untrusted content marks the item (TD-M1); an agent-named merge (`same_as`) after untrusted content adds the source only, never moves the date, counts toward the run's 10, and its card names the item (TD-M2); a file's or a Notebook page's source after untrusted content asks, and only reads count as read (TD-L1); the member's or another agent's item always asks (TD-L2); a close runs only as its look in that same call decided (TD-I1); a member's note alone keeps the untrusted mark (TD-I2); a source is recorded after its taint (TD-I4). `~/work/maia-mode/todo-store/security.md`; TD-I3 (§15's unasked counts) is W3's.
- **Dedupe and caps in code** (VISION §2): the same message twice, at once or after a restart, is one row; reminders in the same Courier channel, page, file or site merge; something closed in the last 30 days isn't added again; the agent's `same_as` merges into an open item it names. 10 adds a run, 30 an agent a day, 100 a desk a day, 2,000 open, 20 suggestions an agent a day, each held under concurrent calls.
- **Measured:** a create 3.5 ms p50 and 5 ms p95 with 1,000 items, on disk before it returns (budget 50 ms); the member's view hears an agent's add within 500 ms over the real session path. Dedupe on a hand-labelled set of 530 to-dos from 476 messages: the code's rules never merge two different tasks (precision 1.0); its recall on paraphrased repeats (4.5%) is why `same_as` exists, and the model's recall with it is its capability run's to measure (`docs/sub-status/mm-todo-store.md`).
- **Docs:** the draft ADR's §13 is final (as built), with §2, §7, §15 and §17 to match; `API.md` §22 and §18's tool table. Status: `docs/sub-status/mm-todo-store.md`.
- **Release order:** deskd before or with desk-agent: a desk-agent with the To-do declarations under an older deskd isn't offered them (deskd's hello doesn't list them).
