## Unreleased - Messages between the member's own desks: an agent on one desk messages an agent on another, carried by the member's office (`sub/mm-desk-messages`; deskd, desk-agent, the office and the Agent app; draft ADR "the Agent's tools and approvals" §14, work breakdown W6; Maia Mode Phase 0; in no release)
- **Added (the owner, Nov 12: desk-to-desk "must be an explicit grant the member chooses"; off by default, revocable):** an agent the member lets message another of their desks (the setup screen's Other desks, already built) gets `desk_message {desk, text}`. The other desk's member sees "{X} on your {A} desk wants to message agents here. Choose which agent answers it" with Allow and Not now, and either desk's Stop revokes its half at once. The answering agent's run starts on its own desk, untrusted from the start, one hop, unattended; its reply goes back, and asks the member there first when it read their private data. An answer within 120 s is the tool's result; a later one comes to the thread as "From {Y} on your {desk} desk" and starts no run. Never on a Guild Desk, never across members.
- **deskd:** the desk's own keys (Ed25519, X25519; `/data/desk/desk-keys.json`, 0600), the chat spike's envelope (signed, HPKE-sealed, opened and verified: `desks/Envelope`, `Hpke`), and its own record of the grants' halves (`desk-grants.json`, HMAC'd, failing closed). Every envelope that comes in is checked before desk-agent hears of it: for this desk, sealed, signed by the key pinned for its grant, this owner's account, no older than 7 days, this desk's half active, 20 a grant a day. `agent.desks.list`, `.answer`, `.take`, `.deliver`, `.done`, the topic `agent.desks.outbox`, `desk_message` and `desk_answer` as deskd's tools (`own`, or `send` from a run holding `S`, which runs only on a relayed approval), and the offers, accepts and revokes the member's choices call for, handed to desk-agent's outbox. `waiting_desk` doesn't keep the desk awake.
- **desk-agent:** the answer halves, the outbox and the inbox in `agent.db` (additive tables; every envelope in once by its id, in order per grant by its `seq`, in one transaction); the answering run (origin `desk`) and its answer as the run's closing call through the gate; failures sent back in place of an answer (a run that can't start, fails, is stopped, is declined or is cut off by a restart: never a second run); the 120 s wait behind `desks/DeskWaits`, for durable runs to keep; "Not delivered" after 7 days, and the notices.
- **The office (`web/src`):** the carrier (`desk/deskCarrier.ts`): take, deliver in order, done, one loop per ordered pair, refusals reported, back off and resume; never a Guild Desk, never a stopped desk. **The Agent app:** the offer card, "Answers your other desks" in the setup screen and settings, the thread's desk rows and notices, the two cards, `waiting_desk`.
- **The office's trust at pairing** is stated in the ADR (Security's ATA-I2): keys are exchanged through the office, so against the office itself the signature and the seal protect only against bugs.
- **Docs:** the draft ADR's §14 (the office's trust, and what's built), `API.md` §11 and §18 ("Messages between the member's desks"), desk-agent's README and `docs/proposals.md` §5, `web/agent/README.md`, `web/CLAUDE.md`. Status: `docs/sub-status/mm-desk-messages.md`.
- **Release order:** deskd with or before desk-agent, then the office. An older deskd answers `agent.desks.*` `not_found` and the office carries nothing; `agent.db`'s new tables are additive, so a rollback still opens the file.
- **To-do's source kind `desk`** (the To-do store's question 4): a to-do an agent adds from a message from another of the member's desks names it as its source, `{kind: "desk", ref: {}}`, which deskd fills from its own record of the envelopes it opened (`desks/DeskSources`): the message that started the answering run, or an answer the waiting `desk_message` call took. Untrusted content, as Courier's and the Browser's are. The To-do app shows it with its source line, the untrusted mark and Open in the Agent.
- **Security's stand-in read** (`DM-L1`, `DM-I1` to `DM-I6`, all handled, confirmed by a second read): deskd makes an offer only for a desk the member switched on in that call, and takes a half away before desk-agent hears; `desk_answer` runs only as a run's own closing call; a summary that folds another desk's words is untrusted; an offer from a desk not in the member's office says so and can't be allowed; an answer is a run's To-do source only within the wait.
- **Tests:** see `docs/sub-status/mm-desk-messages.md` for the counts, the 10,000-envelope runs and the timings.

## Unreleased - Durable runs' seam: call records, `tcall` keys, each tool's restart rule, and four restart defects fixed (`sub/mm-durable-seam`; desk-agent, deskd and the Agent app; Maia Mode Phase 0, Design 1's first item; in no release)
- **Fixed (desk-agent):** after a kill, a reply kept no text or chips, as both were written only at the end. Each step now writes them, before its calls run and after, and the reply ends `interrupted` at open with what it had. The run's marks (`U`, `S`, egress) are written first, so that text never reads unmarked to a later run (Security's DS-M1, fixed on the branch).
- **Fixed (desk-agent):** an unasked or `own` call in flight at a kill left no trace. Every call that runs now has a `run_calls` row in `agent.db` before it's sent (run, step, call id, tool, class, host, restart rule, approval or unasked id, state; ids and words only), settled after; at open a call still `started` ends `interrupted` (`failed` for a read) and is audited `tool.interrupt`, and a call that came back before its card settled settles the card from its record.
- **Fixed (desk-agent):** a lost deskd link settled an approved call `failed`, though it may have run. A call deskd got and didn't answer is asked again with its key on the next link, for up to 2 minutes, and takes deskd's real result; a key deskd no longer holds, no link, or an older deskd end it `interrupted`, and its card with it, never run twice.
- **Fixed (desk-agent):** a timer whose run was killed stayed `lastState: "running"`. It ends `interrupted` at open, audited `timer.fire` `interrupted`; the Agent app says "Didn't finish: the desk restarted. Its thread shows how far it got."
- **Added (deskd):** each `tcall` carries `key` (the call's record's UUIDv7), and deskd keeps the reply by it 10 minutes in RAM, as a client's `idem`: the same key runs once, a repeat waits for the first, `retry: true` for a key deskd doesn't hold is refused `key_unknown`, a first call with a key over 10 minutes old `key_expired`, another call with the same key `key_mismatch`; nothing refused runs. The hello says `keys: {ttlSec: 600}`, and desk-agent asks again only a deskd that says it (`API.md` §18, "Keys").
- **Added (both):** every tool declares what a restart does to a call in flight, `ToolDecl.restart` and `HostedDecls.Decl.restart`: `retry_by_key`, `interrupt` or `safe`, pinned tool by tool in `ToolRegistryTest`, deskd's to desk-agent's; a call whose class rose to one that asks is never `safe`, nor a declaration that names none.
- **Docs:** the draft ADR's §1 (`restart`), §3 ("Restarts") and §17; `API.md` §18 ("Keys"); desk-agent's README ("Restarts") and `docs/proposals.md` §5. Status: `docs/sub-status/mm-durable-seam.md`, with the seam the resume builds on.
- **Release order:** deskd and desk-agent in either order. An older deskd says no `keys`, so a lost call ends `interrupted` with no retry; an older desk-agent sends no `key`, and deskd runs it as before. `agent.db`'s new table is additive, so a rollback still opens the file.
- **Tests:** desk-agent `tool/DurableSeamKillTest` (11: killed mid-call and at settle, unasked and `own` calls in flight, the link dropped, a deskd without the key, an older deskd, a link that never comes back, a read, a timer), `run/RunCallsTest`, `tool/RestartTest`, `socket/ToolKeysSocketTest` (the link dropped over the real socket), `ToolRegistryTest`, `ToolCallSocketTest`; deskd `agent/AgentToolKeysTest` (over the real agent socket: a repeated key runs once, a repeat waits, an expired key refused, a dropped link's retry, a restarted deskd), `tools/ToolHostKeysTest`; web/agent `model/multiAgent.test.ts`.

## Unreleased - Courier's store in deskd, its `courier.*` methods and `courier.changed` (`sub/lsub-courier-store`, the Lead Subcontractor's Courier build, Waves 0.2 and 1.1; deskd; on, with a kill switch; inert until the image mounts `/data/courier` and a transport fills it)
- **Added (the owner, Nov 12: Courier is prelaunch, "VERY solid and capable... pretty much production ready, no MVP"):** `services/deskd/.../courier/`: `/data/courier/courier.db` (SQLite, WAL, `synchronous=FULL`, `secure_delete`, one writer thread and two readers) each raw message exactly as it came, gzip, in the database beside its row up to 64 KiB and in `blobs/` over it (staged and placed, so a crash leaves no torn or orphan file); the one idempotent sink every transport hands messages to, keyed on `(medium, externalId)`, which commits a transport's cursor with its messages; JWZ threading in any order of arrival with a subject fallback for replies, and **a thread never admits a stranger who only knows a Message-ID**; one channel per person or group, contacts that merge; lanes (People, Requests, Updates, Other, Spam, Trash) and standings (let in, not now, block by address or domain); FTS5 search by Notebook's rules with `from:`, `to:`, `has:attachment`, `before:`, `after:`, `in:`, `is:unread`, `secure-delete` on, read newest first from the index so no search sorts its matches; the outbound queue (10 s Undo, retries over 4 days, a stop mid-send put back and marked); retention (Trash and Spam 30 days, Requests 90); `CourierRead`, the agent read tools' seam, on the real store (§12's fields with the Courier plan's proposed amendments).
- **`courier.*` and the topic `courier.changed`** (`services/deskd/docs/API.md` §21): status, lists, threads, messages, HTML, search, contacts, drafts, the queue, accounts, blocks; read, move, Done, a Request's card, block, delete, empty Trash, drafts, send and undo, contacts. The owner's sessions on an adult's personal desk; a guild desk and a teen's are refused, and so is everything while `/data/desk/courier-switch` says `off`.
- **The ADR set (Wave 0.2):** `docs/decisions/draft-courier-store.md` (new), `draft-courier-channels.md` (amends 0016), `draft-courier-address.md` (amends 0079), `draft-courier-relay.md` (amends 0017). The owner's open decisions D1 to D5 are written as their recommended defaults, marked.
- **The image's part:** deskd now carries `sqlite-jdbc`; the Dockerfile takes its native library out of the jar (`/app/native`, `jvm.options`), the AppCDS training opens a store and calls `courier.*`, and `test-release-scripts.sh` checks both. `/data/courier` in `desk-datadirs` and deskd's container is the System Engineer's (`docs/sub-status/lsub-courier-store.md`).
- **Not built here:** `desk-parse`'s Courier mode, the vault, desk keys and envelopes, Platform's relay and mail door, the transports, the UI, attachments' Save to Files and downloads, the export's `courier` part (the plan's Waves 1.2 to 3).
- **Security (Nov 13, a stand-in read; fixed before the merge):** an email is the member's own only when its transport says so or it comes from their address with DMARC passing, never by its From alone (CS-M1); every delete for good empties the WAL (CS-L1); hidden, bidi, tag and separator characters are dropped from everything a transport or the sandbox hands over, and an address holding one isn't one (CS-L2); agent results carry `senderConfirmed` (CS-L3).
- **Tests:** see `docs/sub-status/lsub-courier-store.md` for the counts and the 100,000-message measurements.

## Unreleased - An agent's setup screen: what it can use, chosen before its first run (`sub/lsub-agent-tools-setup`; desk-agent, deskd and the Agent app; draft ADR "the Agent's tools and approvals" §14 to §16, work breakdown W1 and W2; in no release)
- **Added (the owner, Nov 12: the Agent's app tools are prelaunch, "pretty much production ready, no MVP"; desk-to-desk "must be an explicit grant the member chooses"):** before an agent's first run, one screen in plain words, "What can {name} use?": Notebook, Files, Browser and To-dos on, Courier off; other desks, each off until the member switches it on; "Without asking" for a Notebook tab, a Files folder and to-dos, all off. Save sets the agent up; Back leaves it waiting, with "Set up {name}" where the composer was. Settings' "What it can use" changes any of it later, and Stop takes back a grant to another desk.
- **The gate:** desk-agent starts no run of an agent the member hasn't set up (`precondition_failed`, `reason: "setup"`, after `no_key`): the member's, a timer's and another agent's. Its tools are only the apps that are on, read at every step and every call, so switching an app off reaches a run already going (`Refused: scope.`, no look and no card).
- **Two records:** desk-agent's `agent_access` and `desk_grants` in `agent.db` (each row with its SHA-256; a row that doesn't verify is nothing chosen, audited `access.tampered` once), and deskd's own `/data/desk/agent-access.json` (HMAC-SHA256 under its own key; a file that doesn't verify, or the last one when a write fails, is set aside and every agent waits), recorded only from the member's relayed `agent.access.set`. deskd refuses every hosted tool call, a look included, for an agent it holds no record of (`setup`) or an app that's off (`scope`), whatever desk-agent says; its hello names the agents it holds, and any other waits for the member to confirm.
- **Agents made before this** wait for the screen on their next run (`setup: "upgrade"`), which starts from what they could use (Notebook, Files, Browser).
- **New on the wire:** `agent.access.get`, `agent.access.set`, `agent.desks.revoke`; an agent's `setup`; the hello's `access`; audit `access.set`, `access.confirm`, `access.tampered`, `desks.offer`, `desks.revoke` (services/deskd/docs/API.md §11, "An agent's access").
- **Release order:** deskd with or before desk-agent (an older deskd relays none of the three methods, and the screen can't save).
- **Brand review:** "Without asking" says "one Notebook tab" and "one folder"; plainer words for settings' Apps note and three Activity rows ("Let it message another of your desks"); settings' Save matches its neighbours.
- **Tests:** desk-agent `access/AccessBookTest`, `tool/AccessToolsFlowTest`; deskd `agent/AccessRecordTest`, `agent/AccessRelayTest`, `tools/ToolHostAccessTest`, `agent/RealDeskAgentAccessIT` (the real jar); web/agent `model/access.test.ts`, `mock/mockAccess.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/agents.spec.ts`; web `src/agent/places.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/agent.setup.shell.spec.ts` (13, three themes, axe and the colour rule). Status: `docs/sub-status/lsub-agent-tools-setup.md`.
- **At the merge with the core and the Notebook tools:** desk-agent's gate takes each agent's switches from its access record (`ApprovalPolicy.perAgent(access::switchedOn, LowRisk.ADR)`), and deskd's `agent/AccessRecord` answers the core's `ToolHost.AccessRecord` (`host.access`), so both checks of an unasked call read the member's own choice. Nothing runs unasked yet: `LowRisk.ADR` and `HostedDecls.LOW_RISK` name no tool until the ADR's W3. The core's `RealDeskAgentToolsIT` saves the setup screen before its first message. **Fixed:** desk-agent's CDS training (the image's build step) sent a message before any setup was saved and failed on the gate; it saves the screen's defaults first now (7 audit rows; `CdsTrainingIT` and `test-release-scripts.sh` follow).

## Unreleased - The Agent's Notebook tools, production-ready (`sub/lsub-agent-tools-notebook`; desk-notebook, deskd, desk-agent and the Notebook app; the draft ADR "the Agent's tools and approvals" §2; in no release)
- **Changed (the owner, Nov 12: the desk Agent's app tools are prelaunch, "pretty much production ready, no MVP"):** `notebook_read` gives a page as Markdown that reads back as exactly that page: headings, lists and to-do lists (side by side, empty items, code inside), quotes, rules, code (fences longer than any backtick run inside), bold, italic, underline, strike and inline code, links with titles, hard breaks, highlights and text colours (`<mark data-color>`, `<span data-text-color>`), a block's colour (a `{color=…}` line), empty paragraphs (`<p></p>`) and spaces at a block's ends. desk-notebook's writer (`services/notebook/src/pageMarkdown.ts`) checks every inline run against its reader before it writes it. A table or an image the model writes is converted (to text, to a link) and the card and the result say so, rather than lost without a word.
- **Added:** `notebook_list` (read): the notebooks and their tabs, or one tab's pages, 100 at a time, the way to a `tabId` for `notebook_write`'s `create`. desk-notebook's `notebook.outline` serves it.
- **Added:** an agent's edit is done once under its approval's id (`key`), whatever retries; one page's agent edits take turns; a `replace_text` within one block edits in place, so the member typing in the same page loses nothing. **Undo:** `notebook.page.agentUndo` takes an append's blocks out, a created page to the Trash or a replaced text back, only while they're as the agent left them; otherwise it says `changed` with the `pre_agent` version to go back to. deskd's `tools/NotebookTools.undo(caller, approvalId)` is §15's undo's way in. `notebook.page.agentEdits` lists a page's agent edits (kept 30 days).
- **Added (the Notebook app):** a page's foot says "Edited by {agent}, {when}" with Undo after an agent's edit, and History names the version from before it ("Before {agent}'s edit") with Undo there too. Refusals are fixed words ("It's been changed since. History has the page from before."). The agent's name comes from the office's roster; "an agent" when it doesn't say.
- **Security (the stand-in Security read's ATN-M1 and ATN-I1, Nov 12):** an approved Notebook edit from a run that had read untrusted content (a web page, a file) is marked, and a page that still holds that text reads back untrusted, so the next run that reads it, searches to it or lists its tab holds `U` and must ask before it browses (the Rule of Two), as a marked workspace file does. deskd sends its own copy of the run's taint with every write; desk-notebook keeps each written block's words and links, hashed, in `agent_marks`, and says `untrusted` on `notebook.page.markdown`, a tab's `notebook.outline` and the new `notebook.page.marked`. A mark ends when the member changes that text's words or links; moving, recolouring or a version bringing it back keeps it; undo takes it out. An edit whose row and marks can't be kept is refused with nothing made, and an undo records who undid it.
- **Measured (the build box, x86_64, p50/p95):** a whole 100-page page read in 67/72 ms (one part 30 ms the first time, 3.5/3.9 ms after), a small page 0.3/0.9 ms; search over 10,000 pages 0.3/3.8 ms; an append shown in an open editor 4.8/8.4 ms, to a closed 100-page page 164/178 ms; through deskd, read 13/24 ms and append 59/93 ms.
- **Tests:** desk-notebook `roundtrip.test.ts` (1,000 random pages and a hand-written corpus, exactly), `agent-undo.test.ts`, `agent-concurrency.test.ts` (the member typing 1,000 keystrokes through agent edits; two agents at once), `agent-scale.test.ts`, `agent-taint.test.ts`; deskd `NotebookToolsTest`, `RealDeskNotebookToolsIT` (the real desk-notebook: exact round trip, the key and undo, two agents, latency); `web/notebook/tests/unit/agentEdits.test.ts`; `web/tests/e2e/notebook.agent.shell.spec.ts` (three themes, axe). Status: `docs/sub-status/lsub-agent-tools-notebook.md`.
- **The seam with the core (`sub/lsub-agent-tools-core`), at the merge:** a call desk-agent does without asking under the member's switch now carries its own approval id, minted by the gate (`Verdict.Allowed(group, approvalId)`), to deskd as `autoApprovalId` beside `auto`, required there (`bad_request` without it); `NotebookTools` sends it as the edit's `key`, as it does a member's approval id, so an unasked Notebook edit is made once and the ADR's `agent.approval.undo` can find it by that id (`NotebookTools.undo`). The `approval.auto` audit row names the id; the `tool.call` row still names none. Nothing runs unasked yet: the low-risk maps are empty until the ADR's W3. Tests: `NotebookToolsHostTest.aWriteDoneWithoutAskingIsKeyedByItsOwnApprovalIdAndRunsNothingWithoutOne`, `ToolHostAutoTest.aCallDoneWithoutAskingMustNameItsOwnApprovalId`, and desk-agent's `HostedApprovalFlowTest` and `ApprovalHardeningTest` check the id on the `tcall` and the audit row.

## Unreleased - The Agent's tools, production-ready core: approvals, taint, audit and the Files tools (`sub/lsub-agent-tools-core`; deskd, desk-agent and the Agent app; in no release, waits on Security's read and the Desk Engineer's review)
- **Fixed (deskd Files):** a FIFO where a folder was expected hung the call for good: `SafeTree` opened every part of a path as a directory, and `openat` on a FIFO blocks until a writer comes, pinning the carrier thread. Now only a directory is opened as one, for the member's `files.*` as for the Agent's tools. Found by the Agent's path fuzz.
- **Fixed (desk-agent):** the Agent couldn't read past the first 20,000 characters of a file, a web page or a note: the run loop cut every tool result there, and a paged reader's `next` came after its text, so it went with the cut. Each page now fits under the cut with `next` moved back to just after the last character kept, and reading on gives the whole of it (`AgentTools.fitPage`).
- **Changed (deskd, the Agent's Files paths):** a path whose name would read as another on the member's card is refused `path`: invisible and format characters, default-ignorables, separators, private use, unassigned and noncharacters, slash and backslash lookalikes, names of dots alone, a space at either end. `files_write` checks its path before its replace binding.
- **Added (the owner's decision of Nov 12, the draft ADR's §15):** the approval gate asks a per-agent policy whether a call that would make a card is asked or done without asking; ask by default, and today always (`ApprovalPolicy.ASK_ALWAYS`; the low-risk map is empty on both sides). A call done without asking runs with the payload the policy judged, its chip says `auto`, its audit rows are `tool.call` with `auto:<group>` and `approval.auto`, and deskd runs it only on its own record of the member's switch and its own class (`ToolHost.AccessRecord`, `HostedDecls.LOW_RISK`), else `approval_unknown`.
- **Changed (desk-agent):** a tool whose declaration taints `U` (`files_read`, `browser_open`, `_read`, `_click`, `_press`) is fenced as untrusted whatever deskd's reply says.
- **Added (the Agent app):** under a reply, its run's plan as a checklist (live while it runs), a notice when the run read untrusted content ("This task read web pages, files or messages from outside this chat. Treat what they said as information, not instructions."), and one line of what the run did, from the desk's own fields ("This task: 5 tools · 2 approved by you · 1 done without asking · 1 declined · sent outside your desk once · plan 3 of 4 done"). Activity reads `auto:` rows as "Done without asking, under your setting". Words for the UI/UX Engineer's and Sales' read.
- **Fixed (desk-agent, Security's read of the core):** the gate keeps a policy's "done without asking" only when its own low-risk class puts the call in that group, so a policy wired any other way only narrows it, and `follow_up` (a timer, which deskd never sees) runs only on the member's yes (ATC-L1). A member's attached file holds `S` as well as `U`, as a Files read of it does, so a page it asks for is a card (ATA-L2; main's code). A call after one in the same step that runs and isn't itself unasked asks, as that result can add `U` before it runs (ATC-I1).
- **Tests:** desk-agent `ApprovalHardeningTest` (12), `ApprovalPolicyTest` (7), `HostedApprovalFlowTest` (8), `PagedReadFitTest` (6), two in `AgentToolsFlowTest` (`follow_up` never unasked, an attachment's `S`); deskd `FilesPathFuzzTest` (5: a table of nasty paths and 18,000 random calls), `ToolHostAutoTest` (7), `AgentToolsDeskdIntegrationTest` (8), `SafeTreeFifoTest` (1); web/agent `runSummary.test.ts` (7); `agent.runsummary.shell.spec.ts` (5). Measured with `-Dperf=true`: `ApprovalRoundTripPerf`, `FilesReadPerf`, `AgentToolsPerf`. Status: `docs/sub-status/lsub-agent-tools-core.md`.

## Unreleased - Courier's own id: `courier`, wherever the office names the app (`sub/lsub-courier-id`; Desk app; rides release 20)
- **Changed (the owner, Nov 12: "I hate using a different internal id. consistency is key"):** the app a member knows as Courier (the Chat app before `sub/courier-name`) is `courier` in the office's code: the app id and `CoreAppId`, a new desk's `<desk>:courier` item, the DOM ids and test ids that come from them (`proxy-<desk>:courier`, `tile-…`, `empty-courier`, `open-tile-courier`), the mock desk's sample, the glyph's `courier()`, the window's default size, and the specs (`courierlater.shell.spec.ts`, `courierlater.shots.shell.spec.ts`, `COURIER_LATER_SHOTS`). deskd reserves `courier.` for its methods and topics, and keeps `chat.` reserved (the app contract draft says the same). No fleet-map key named the app (its `chat` keys are Maia's and Commons'), so the map is unchanged. "Chat" stays where it means something else: Maia's chat, Commons' channel chat, the Agent's conversation, and the Guild's "a board and chat".
- **Stored layouts (`src/canvas/storedIds.ts`):** members' saved layouts on Platform and in the mirror say `chat`, in an app's `app`, its `<desk>:chat` id, the desk's `stack` and its `carousel`; so do this browser's window places (`ma.windows.v1`), app zoom (`ma.appZoom.v1`) and a phone's carousel (`ma.phoneCarousel.v1`). Every reader takes `chat` and `courier` alike and holds `courier`. A version-1 layout migrates the same way. No new schema version: a newer one would stop every older build from saving.
- **Rollback safety, a transition:** release 19 and every build before it drop an app id they don't know when they read a layout, and their next save takes the tile from Platform's document for good. So this build still **writes** `chat` (`STORED_COURIER`), which every build reads: a document saved here is byte for byte what release 19 saves, and a rollback finds every tile. The release after this one has gone out everywhere flips `STORED_COURIER` to `courier`; reading `chat` stays, since Platform keeps a document as it came. Removal step and proof: `docs/sub-status/courier-id.md`.
- **Bytes:** first load +507 B gzipped against main `963b808a` (248,981 to 249,488 B); with release 19's main `f454e086` merged, 254,236 B (248.3 KB of 250; main 253,693 B, +543 B).
- **Tests:** `src/canvas/storedIds.test.ts`, `courierId.test.ts` (real-shaped documents as release 19 saves them: Courier in the items, stack and carousel, an agent's own app, the Bible, Files, a style, `prefs`; the schema version; the 64 KiB cap and a 413's smaller one; two devices, one on release 19; a document with both ids), `courierId.flip.test.ts` (the release that writes `courier`, the cap with the longer id), the zoom store's; and release 18's (`09cbc49d`) and main's own code reading what this build stores (every tile kept) and what writing `courier` now would have stored (Courier dropped).

## Unreleased - The final app icons: the MA mark's 3D finish on all seven apps, and Files in Silver (`sub/final-icons-3d`; Desk app only, in no release)
- **Changed:** every app's icon takes the MA mark's 3D finish (variant B, the owner's pick, Nov 12): lit tops, darker ends, folds where one part tucks behind another and a lit line on the light side, each stop a theme token moved in OKLCH lightness. Courier (was Chat; its id stays `chat`) is the raven on its letter (raven 4, variation 1), still with its "Coming later" chip and never a count; Commons is the three people of round 3 (no board); the Bible keeps its own Lapis ink, not the office blue. The 24 px cut still draws at 30 px and under.
- **Changed:** Files' icon is Silver, not teal (the owner: "an on-brand silver"): `glyph.filesFront`, `filesBody` and `filesSheet`, with the window's own Silver colors (`APP_INKS.files`, already on `main` from `00029b59`).
- **Changed:** the icons in window heads, the carousel, the phone's thumb row, open tiles and the Apps sheet are the canvas's own drawing on a small canvas (one copy of the shapes, no SVG copies), drawn again on a theme change and when the screen's pixel density changes. Still hidden from assistive tech.
- **Where a browser's canvas can't read `oklch(from ...)`** (decided by the canvas itself, not `CSS.supports`), the icons use precomputed colours, within 1 of 255 per channel. Chromium 156, Firefox 157 and WebKit 27.2 all read it.
- **First load:** see `docs/sub-status/final-icons-3d.md` (under the 250 KB budget); the Bible's drawing is in the desk-app host's chunk.
- **Tests:** `glyphs.test.ts` rewritten (every app, theme and size, the cut, Commons, the raven, the Bible's ink, the fallback hexes against the tokens, the canvas's LIVE decision); `glyphs3d.shell.spec.ts` (a window's glyph redrawn on a theme and a density change); shots `icons3d.shots.shell.spec.ts`; `tests/glyphs/xbrowser.mjs` (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit); `tilefaces.perf` with `TILE_CARD_CPU`. `docs/sub-status/final-icons-3d.md`.
- **Rerun on `sub/lsub-icons3d` (the Lead Subcontractor; `main` `e16b4655` merged, no code changed):** Vitest 3,233 passed; `release` 142 passed; the full `shell` project 2,013 passed, 59 skipped, 0 failed of 2,072; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit renders the same as before; first load 250,406 B gzip (+3,531 B on `main`, 5,594 B under the budget), the host chunk +855 B. Paint times and the rest in `docs/sub-status/final-icons-3d.md`.
- **After review:** a DOM glyph's canvas is its own box in device pixels, each axis, its edges snapped where it sits (`chrome/glyphBox.ts`), so at 1.25x and 1.5x it's never resampled and doesn't blur; the hidden list's icons are the same `GlyphCanvas`, so they redraw when the screen's density changes. Tests: `chrome/glyphBox.test.ts`, `glyphs3d.shell.spec.ts` at 1.25x and 1.5x. `notebook.shell.spec.ts`' full-page test waits for the new page's focus before typing, as its sibling does, and `opentiles.shell.spec.ts`' axe test waits for the window to land before axe (both raced on `main` too).

## Unreleased - No member's id is left in the kept camera or view after a sign-out (`sub/lsub-first-office-wipe`; Desk app only, in no release)
- **Fixed (on `main` since the first office's merge, `790d950c`, in no release):** the camera (`ma.camera.v1`) and the view (`ma.view.v1`) this browser keeps were stamped with the member's id so the next member wouldn't start on them, and that id survived a sign-out, a dead session and another account's sign-in (ISS-67, ISS-89). Now the id is written only while the layout mirror's guard allows it, and every signed-out state and another account's sign-in leave both keys stamped as left at a sign-out, with no id. They stay as the device's own, the scope included, and nobody starts on them: the next member's first office is framed by the owner's rule, and so is the same member's after a sign-out. A reload keeps a member's own camera as before.
- **Tests:** `memberCaches.shell.spec.ts` passes again (5 of its 7 failed on `main`); `firstOffice.shell.spec.ts`'s next member checks that nothing in this browser names A after A's sign-out; `state/deviceOwner.test.ts` and `state/memberCaches.test.ts` hold the stamp, the wipe, another account's sign-in, a stale tab and a page that has signed out.

## Unreleased - Typing in a page opened just after a version save is stored (`sub/lsub-nb-lost-typing`; desk-notebook only, in no release)
- **Fixed (possible loss of a member's typing, on `main` and in every desk-notebook release with direct connections, prod's `2026.11.11-90528b5` included):** after `notebook.version.save`, `notebook.version.restore` or an agent's Notebook edit on a page nobody had open, an editor that opened the page before Hocuspocus's 0 ms post-store timer fired had its page unloaded by that timer, which goes by the page's id: out of Hocuspocus and out of `Pages`. What it then typed was acknowledged and never stored, and was gone on the next load. Hocuspocus's unload now does nothing for a document it no longer holds under that id (`Collab` in `services/notebook/src/collab.ts`); no timing, retry or wait was added.
- **Tests:** `services/notebook/tests/reopen.test.ts` (4): the save, the restore and the agent's edit with that timer held until the editor's page has loaded (each fails every time on `main`: 30 of 30 in 10 runs), and save, open, type 16 times as it falls (on `main` 46 of 160 lost in 10 runs, 2 to 6 a run). 50 runs of the file on this branch: 50 passed, nothing lost (150 held, 800 as they fell).
- **For the release:** desk-notebook alone; deskd, desk-agent and the web are unchanged. `docs/sub-status/lsub-nb-lost-typing.md`.

## Unreleased - Maia's web search card, the auto line and her sources (`sub/lsub-maia-websearch`, the Lead Subcontractor's item 15; Desk app; shows for an adult Officer, Founder or the owner once ma-platform's Maia with web search on by default, asking first, is rolled out; its `webSearch.enabled: false` is the kill switch)
- **Added (the owner, Nov 12: "the user can auto approve all web search requests or opt to approve each individual batch (don't want a user to approve 10 web searches in one "turn")"; ma-platform ADR 0137):** `src/maia/web.ts` (words, checks, the answer's body), `WebSearch.tsx` (the card, the line, the sources), `frames.ts` (`web_search`, `sources`), `gate.ts` (`webSearch` in the state), `chat.ts` (`answerWeb`, `setWebMode`, a reloaded chat's waiting card and sources), `maia.css`.
- **The card:** one for a reply's whole batch, "Search the web?", the queries as a plain list, Search, Not now and a quiet Always allow (described as "Maia searches without asking from now on"). An answer is one turn with no message (`{conversationId, clientContext, webSearch: {batchId, decision}}`, held key for key by the tests); an expired card (or a `410`) offers nothing and says so. **Auto:** no card; a quiet line "Searched the web for …" with "Ask me first", which saves `ask` (`PUT /v1/maia/preferences`, so it follows the member). **Sources:** under her reply, numbered as her `[n]`, each opening the search API's own http(s) address in its own tab with `noopener noreferrer` and no referrer: the one place a link off our hosts shows, never from her text. Tokens only, no new colors.
- **Not changed:** the initial JS (243.9 KB of 250 after merging main; the code is in the lazy widget chunk but for `gate.ts`'s few lines), the fleet map (no key changes), `web/CLAUDE.md` (UI/UX's; the lines for it are in `docs/sub-status/lsub-maia-websearch.md`).

## Unreleased - A new member's first office opens naturally (`sub/lsub-first-office`, from `sub/first-office`; ADR 0057; Desk app only, in no release)
- **Fixed (the owner, Nov 12):** a first visit's desk was 584 x 236 (2.47:1), framed at 175 % on most screens, so it looked squat and everything was oversized. A new desk is now 860 x 480 (about 16:9), its apps in a row along the top as before.
- **The owner's rule, for a desk as it's first introduced:** "By default when a desk is first introduced I want it to be only 50% of a browser's width in a 16:9 layout. 4:3 and others are obviously different". A new desk is framed at half the width of a 16:9 screen, and at the same share of the area on any other shape (4/(9a) for a desk of aspect a), so (2/3)·√(W·H) wide, centred between the bars, inside the fit's margins.
  - Its zoom: 112 % at 1920 x 1080, 88 % at 1440 x 900, 69 % at 1024 x 768, 173 % at 3440 x 1440, 176 % at 2880 x 1800.
  - "First introduced" is a desk at the size the office gives a new one (`asIntroduced`): a new member's, a desk added later, the next member's in the same browser. While it keeps that size, arriving, a reload, a focus, Fit and `0` all frame it so; the overview comes no closer.
  - Phones in portrait (under 600 px wide) keep their own fit: the cell grid across the width, 90 % at 390 x 844. A short screen (under 400 px high: a phone on its side) takes the phone's fit by height: the desk fills the room between the one-row bars, 54 % and 41 px tiles at 844 x 390 (33 under the rule).
  - It's in CSS pixels, "50% of a browser's width": the browser's own zoom doesn't enlarge a new desk; the canvas zoom does, and is kept.
- **The next member in the same browser** is framed by the rule too: the camera and the view kept per device (`ma.camera.v1`, `ma.view.v1`) are stamped with their member (`state/deviceOwner.ts`), and another member's are dropped as the next one arrives, in a new page or the same tab. The same member keeps theirs.
- **An open tile on a new desk goes live:** at 1440 x 900 the rule's 88 % left a 536-wide tile at 473 px, under the 480 it needs; once the member lets go of a tile made open, the office frames the grown desk as any arranged desk (`watchOpenTiles`), with no Fit.
- **Kept:** a saved desk keeps its size and place, and its framing (filling the screen, up to 175 %), and so does a new desk once the member resizes it; open tiles stay live as before. An untouched desk from an earlier first visit (584 x 236 or 584 x 144) still counts as untouched, so its browser uploads nothing. The member's own zoom is kept as before.
- **Tests:** `firstOffice.shell.spec.ts`: a fresh account at 1920x1080, 1440x900, 1024x768, 3440x1440, 2880x1800, 1440x900 at DPR 2, 390x844 and 844x390; the next member after a sign-out; an open tile on a new desk; the reload, the click, Fit and the member's zoom; a desk added later; the test account's saved desk and a saved 584 x 236 one framed as before and not saved again; a resized new desk. Its six desktop first visits and the added desk fail on `main`. Unit tests: `shell/framing.test.ts` and `state/deviceOwner.test.ts` (new), `layout`, `layoutDoc`, `agentTiles` and `camera`. `shell.spec.ts` and `layoutSync.shell.spec.ts` read a new desk's 860 x 480 and the framing's zooms (one desk's zoom out at 1920 x 1080, 112 % to 100 %), `a11y.shell.spec.ts`'s focus ring and the etched-name test run where a new desk is framed over 100 %, and `notifications.shell.spec.ts`'s axe hides the canvas proxies it can't place under the panel. ADR 0062's default desk points at ADR 0057. ma-platform `sub/lsub-first-office-fleet`: fleet-mcp's office test reads the new default. Also fixed: the office tour's mount rendered at every read of the layout document, which broke two of `shell.spec.ts`'s layout rules on `main` (the same document renders nothing; Platform has no 304); now a read renders it only while a first visit is still to be decided. With `main` at `963b808a`: vitest 3,271 passed; 39 shell spec files (with `appzoom`, `windowlift`, `loadingOverlay` and `tour`) green after that fix; fleet-mcp 449 of 449 and 17 journeys; initial JS 243.9 KB of 250.

## Unreleased - The office tour: a short look around on a member's first visit (`sub/lsub-walkthrough`; Desk app, ships on; ADR 0056)
- **Added (the owner: "Before launch we need a tooltip guiding system or walkthrough... They should be able to disable and reenable the walk through as they so choose."; the Product Designer's spec, Sales' words):** on a member's first visit, once the office is drawn and quiet, a spotlight and a small card walk through five things at most: Your office, Your desk (or Your desks), Open an app, Ask Maia (where her button shows) and Help and this tour (the mark). A step whose target isn't on screen is left out, and "{n} of {total}" counts only those shown. Next, Back, Skip, Close the tour (×), Esc and "Don't show this again". It never starts over a window, a sheet, a menu, a notice or a desk being set up, and a new notice ends it.
- **Turning it off and on:** Done and "Don't show this again" turn it off (the off link says once "Turned off. You can turn it back on in the account menu."); Skip keeps nothing, so it starts again next time. "Show the office tour" in the account menu, under "Show setup and what's new"; "Take the tour" in the mark's menu, after All desks, starts it any time.
- **Remembered:** the layout document's `prefs.tourOff`, so it follows the member to every device; this browser's `ma.tour.v1` (a layout mirror key, wiped on sign-out and account change); and Platform's `preferences.officeTour` if Platform ever sends it (proposed, `docs/sub-status/walkthrough.md`; nothing waits on it).
- **Accessible:** a modal dialog named by its step, the office inert under it, focus kept in it and returned to where it was, each step said once; reduced motion moves nothing; a phone docks the card to an edge with 44 px targets.
- **Kill switch:** client-config `walkthrough: {"enabled": false}` (absent is on).
- **Bytes:** first load +695 B gzipped (248,292 to 248,987 B against main `5a19afa9`, 243.2 KB of 250 KB); the tour is its own chunk (5,522 B, plus 351 B shared with the account menu) with its own stylesheet (1,277 B), fetched only when it may start, on Take the tour, or as the mark menu opens. The app's stylesheet gains the spotlight token, +50 B.
- **Fleet map:** `office.tour`, `office.tour.next`, `office.tour.skip`, `office.tour.close`, `office.tour.off`, `office.mark.tour`. The fleet's office tools turn the tour off on a fresh account (ma-platform `sub/lsub-walkthrough-fleet`).
- **Review round 1:** the step announcements are inside the dialog, which isn't modal while it leaves; it never starts under the loading overlay (`#ma-ld`, ADR 0058: not until the office has taken it away); a change of member in a tab closes the open tour and decides afresh for the new member on their own document (`layoutSync.ts` `layoutDocMember`, `readLayoutNow`).
- **Tests:** `src/tour/engine.test.ts`, `door.test.ts`; `tour.shell.spec.ts`; additions to `bars`, `onDemand`, `a11y.sweep` and `fleet-map` shell specs. `docs/sub-status/walkthrough.md`.

## Unreleased - The office's loading overlay: the mark and a calm glint until the office is really ready (`sub/loading-overlay`, `sub/lsub-loading-overlay`; ADR 0058; with ma-platform's loader page of the same branch; in no release)
- **Added (the owner, approved Nov 12: "While an office is being loaded for any tier we need an overlay with a loading animation combined with the logo that fades away when the office is actually ready."):** Platform's loader page draws the Product Designer's overlay from the first paint: the theme's own ground, the theme's mark (the kit's file, whole), and a blue glint crossing a 2 px track every 1.8 s, with "Still loading your office…" after 10 s. Every tier, band and theme (Light, Zen, Dark), and the phone's sizes under 600 px. Reduced motion is a still.
- **When it goes:** on the first frame of the office the member will use: the desk list's answer in (never the mirror), `/v1/me`'s answer or failure (never the last visit's record: `meAnswered` in `platform/session.ts`), a free member's chunk, fonts, then the canvas drawn after them (`chrome/loadingOverlay.ts`, `useOfficeReady`); or a card standing in for desks; or the sign-in, refusal, export or OpenRouter page; or React failing to render (`onUncaughtError`). The Designer's ending: the mark first, then the ground, 600 ms (200 ms of bare ground if the mark hadn't shown; at once in a hidden tab). It takes no input at any time. The loader ends it on its kill switch (client-config `loadingOverlay: {enabled: false}`), a failure, a release from before it (`ma-release.json` `loadingOverlay: 1`), and after 15 s whatever happens.
- **Harness:** `harness/loader/` holds byte-for-byte copies of the loader's `index.html` and `loading.css`, which every shell and release spec now loads; `desksDelayMs`, `meDelayMs`, `loadingMaxMs` and `releaseManifest` steer it.
- **First load:** 242.2 KB gzipped of 250 KB (the ready signal and the ending only).
- **Tests:** `tests/e2e/loadingOverlay.shell.spec.ts` (20; shots and a clip with `LOADING_SHOTS`), `src/chrome/loadingOverlay.test.ts`, `src/chrome/loadingOverlayMark.test.ts`. Status: `docs/sub-status/loading-overlay.md`.

## Unreleased - Window lift: app windows lift off the desk (`sub/lsub-window-lift`; Desk app, ships on)
- **Changed (the owner: lift the app windows off the desk a little, "Maybe a bevel or something?"; on Nov 12 he picked option 5 of the Product Designer's sheet, "the top-edge highlight plus the contact shadow"):** an app window sits on a tight contact shadow at its base under a short soft one, deeper when it's the active or a full window, in place of the menu's and the window's shadows; and every window surface (a window, a desk's carousel stage, an open tile) has a 1 px edge inside it, lit from the top left as the app icons are: the top strongest, the left a third of it, in the theme's own near-white. Light, Zen and Dark each have their own values, the Designer's `options.py` exactly (ADR 0029's addendum lists them). Zen and Dark keep the hairline round the active window. No edge where a surface fills the screen (a phone's stage, a full window on a phone or a short screen), and none under forced colours, where the browser drops every shadow.
- **How:** `WINDOW_LIFT` in `theme/tokens.ts`, as `--ma-window-lift-rest`, `-active` and `-edge` in themes.css, CSS only and out of the bundle (as `VIEW`); the edge is a `::after` at z-index 5 on `.ma-window`, `.carousel-stage` and `.open-tile` (`styles/app.css`, `opentile/opentile.css`), which takes no press; the stage and the tile are their own stacking contexts (`isolation: isolate`), and the Files dialog's scrim is at z-index 6, over the edge. `elevation.window` and `elevation.menu` are unchanged for the modals, menus and panels that use them.
- **Bytes:** initial JS unchanged by the lift (241.1 KB gzip of 250 against main `e16b4655`, all JS +3 B gzip for the version string; 241.7 KB after merging main's ended-membership card); CSS +268 B gzip (48,980 to 49,248 B).
- **Tests:** `src/theme/windowLift.test.ts` (14: the tokens against the Designer's option 5 layer by layer, the exact custom properties, every theme's block and the dark fallback); `tests/e2e/windowlift.shell.spec.ts` (10: what Chromium computes for each surface in each theme, the stage's and the tile's own shadows, a press on Files bringing it forward, a Files dialog's scrim over the edge, a full window, axe on the windows and an open tile, forced colours, a short screen, a tablet, a window narrowed to a phone's and a phone's stage). Shots for the Designer's read: `WINDOW_LIFT_SHOTS=<dir> npx playwright test --project=shell windowlift.shots`. `docs/sub-status/lsub-window-lift.md`.

## Unreleased - Each app zooms on its own (`sub/app-zoom`, finished on `sub/lsub-app-zoom`, ADR 0055; Desk app only, in no release)
- **Added (the owner's ask, Nov 12; the Lead's spec):** every app's head has a zoom button beside its expand button (a window's, an open tile's, the carousel's, each app's head on a phone): a circle holding two corner brackets. It opens a popout with a slider from 50 to 200 % in 10 % steps and the level, which puts the app back to 100 %. Ctrl and the wheel, Cmd and the wheel, and a trackpad pinch over an app zoom that app in 10 % steps; the desk and the page don't move. **Not yet over a desk app's own view** (the Bible's sandboxed frame): the wheel and the pinch go to the frame, never the office, so there the level stays and a pinch still magnifies the whole office, as on `main`; its head (the button, the slider) and its title bar zoom it. Off the apps, nothing changed: a pinch on the canvas is the camera's, Ctrl and the wheel off the canvas the browser's own.
- **Office apps** (Notebook, Files, the Agent, Courier, desk apps such as the Bible) re-lay out at the new size with CSS `zoom`, never a scaled picture; a desk app's sandboxed view re-lays out with its window (Chromium and Firefox checked). Commons has no zoom yet: its board sizes its canvas on screen and would draw at twice its size. Remembered per app for the member in this browser (`ma.appZoom.v1`, wiped with the member's other caches at every sign-out and change of account); every agent shares the Agent's level.
- **The desk Browser** takes its desk's own zoom (deskd `browser.zoom.get`, `browser.zoom.set`, `browser.view`'s `zoom`, the Desk Engineer's interface, not built on any desk yet), so the page stays sharp; until a desk answers `browser.zoom.get` (every deskd today answers `not_found`), and while the desk's phone view is in play (Chromium suspends page zoom there), it has no zoom button and Ctrl with the wheel over its picture goes to the desk as before. Nothing about the zoom moves, reduced motion or not: the popout and a new level are there at once.
- **Fleet map:** `office.app.zoom`, `office.app.zoom.slider`, `office.app.zoom.reset`.
- **First load:** +220 B gzipped (247,550 to 247,770 B against `main` `de9685a0`, 242.0 KB of 250 KB); the button and the store are lazy chunks with the frames, the popout its own.
- **Tests:** `zoom/level.test.ts`, `zoom/store.test.ts`, `canvas/pinch.test.ts`, `browser/driver.test.ts`, `state/memberCaches.test.ts`; `appzoom.shell.spec.ts` (18: reduced motion and the Browser on a phone added), `fleet-map.shell.spec.ts`; shots with `APP_ZOOM_SHOTS` (`appzoom.shots.shell.spec.ts`). `docs/sub-status/app-zoom.md`.

## Unreleased - Chat is now Courier (`sub/courier-name`; Desk app only, in no release)

Its blurb reads "Messages and email for this desk, kept on this desk." Every internal id stays `chat`, so no stored layout or desk changes.

## Unreleased - Agents read attached files and Files documents to the 20 MiB ceiling (`sub/parse-memory`; desk-agent only, in no release)
- **Fixed:** a file over about 16 MiB, attached to an agent message or opened by the Agent from Files (`files_read`), was answered "too complex": the reader ran out of its 72 MiB heap while reading the file in, through the parse sidecar as through the in-container child. It now holds the file once, in 64 KiB chunks (`worker/Input`), never in a doubling buffer or a copy; PDFBox reads the chunks in place, the zip formats stream them, and text is decoded as it arrives. Every kind (text, PDF, docx, xlsx, pptx) reads at 20 MiB, the most a desk owner may allow a file; before, the largest was 16 MiB. Peak memory at 8 MiB is the same or lower (text 105.5 to 56.3 MiB). The heap, the sandbox, the parse sidecar's wire format, the security manager and every cap (40 MiB guard, characters, pages, inflate budget, deadline) are unchanged.
- **Tests (the x86_64 build box):** `ParseWorkerStreamTest` (all six at 20 MiB, and past the 40 MiB guard), `ParsersTest` (20 MiB text and PDF through the child), `worker/InputTest` (against PDFBox's own buffer), `worker/PlainTextTest` (against the old reader on 3,000 random files). `scripts/measure-attach.sh --sizes` measures the worker by kind and size. Numbers: `docs/sub-status/desk-parse-sidecar.md` item 7.

## Unreleased - Sign-out's shared sign-in: a refused end isn't tried again (`sub/sso-desk-503`; Desk app only, in no release)
- **Fixed:** `id.` clears its cookie when `POST /v1/sso/end` answers `503`, so the signed-out page's one more try got a 204 and the "may still be signed in" line never showed, though the other apps weren't signed out. Now step 3 says how it went (`EndOutcome` in `sso.ts`): any answer but its 204 (`refused`, a 503 above all) shows the line at once with no second try (note `end-refused`); no answer (`unanswered`, a network failure or 3 s) gets one more try on `/signed-out`, and the line if that fails or gets no answer (note `end`). Sign out everywhere's note carries the same.
- **Tests (.47):** `src/platform` Vitest 397 of 397 (`sso.test.ts`: each outcome, the page's line per note and retry; `session.signedOut.test.ts`: Sign out everywhere with each outcome); `sso.shell.spec.ts` 19 of 19 (a 503 once with no retry; no answer then 503; no answer twice; no answer then 204; Sign out everywhere failing with a refused step 3). Typecheck clean, lint 0 errors; first load 240.4 KB.

## Unreleased - One sign-in for every app, the office's part (`sub/sso-desk`, ma-platform contract auth.md 2.8, 0.3.118 draft; Desk app only, in no release)
- **Added (only where client-config's `auth.sso` is an object with an https origin at exactly `id.` on this site, as ma-platform's member service sends it, `{"origin": "https://id.<suffix>"}`; the label pinned by Security's SSO-L4; a boolean, another host, absent or anything else is off, nothing changes and nothing goes there):** step 1 (`POST <origin>/v1/sso/session`, the app's access token, credentialed) after `/auth/callback` with the sign-in's own token, before the office is drawn (4 s at most), and once a page load after the first good call, not waited for; step 3 (`POST /v1/sso/end`, credentialed, `keepalive`, no token) first of Sign out's calls, after the layout's last words, for Sign out and Sign out everywhere, with `ma-sso-out` (epoch ms) in this tab's sessionStorage; a step 3 without its 204 is tried once more on `/signed-out`, which says other apps may still be signed in if it fails again; a Sign out everywhere that doesn't hear yes after step 3 signs this browser out anyway and the signed-out page says other devices may still be signed in (step 3 stays first, Security's ruling); `/signed-out` waits for nothing when Sign out noted nothing; a `401 session_revoked` that outlasts the refresh revokes and clears the refresh token behind it and goes to sign in (`notice=session`), or the card's "You were signed out. Please sign in again."; every session ends at `signedInAt` plus 30 days (`pastThirtyDays`, plain line). `sso.ts` is a chunk of its own, loaded only where it's on.

## Unreleased - The Agent reads attached documents in a sandbox of its own (`sub/desk-parse`, ISS20-P1, ADR 0097; desk-agent only; in no release)
- **Security:** on an image with ADR 0097's parse sidecar, desk-agent no longer reads a PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint or text attachment in a child of its own process, which had its uid, its `/data/agent` (where the sealed provider keys are) and the network. It sends the file to the sidecar, a sandbox with uid 1113, no network, no `/data` and no capabilities, over `/run/desk-parse/sock/parse.sock`, and reads the same answer. Once desk-agent has seen the sidecar's marker or socket it never reads in its own child again, and a missing or broken sidecar fails the file as `unavailable` (a new reason; the Agent app needs its sentence). Health gains `parse`: `sidecar` or `child`.
- **Unchanged on today's images:** with no marker and no socket, desk-agent reads in its child as before, until the move to JDK 24.
- **Changed:** a document from Files that an agent reads through the sidecar is read to 2,000,000 characters and 500 pages, as it is in the child: the parse header's ceilings are ADR 0097 section 2's, so nothing is cut (`sub/parse-limits`). The reader's JVM now writes its own messages (an out-of-memory exit among them) to standard error, so an answer is never mistaken for one.
- **Tests:** `ParseRequestTest` (41), `ParseWorkerStreamTest` (7), `ParsersSidecarTest` (17), `ParseOptionsTest` (2), a health case in `DeskAgentApplicationTests`, 13 new checks in `scripts/test-release-scripts.sh`; `scripts/test-parse-sidecar.sh` (41 checks against a real sandbox on Docker); `scripts/measure-attach.sh --timing-only`. Status: `docs/sub-status/desk-parse-sidecar.md`.

## Unreleased - A desk app is paused only for its own long frames (`sub/frame-budget-attribution`, ADR 0054's addendum; for Desk release 16; no words change)
- **Fixed:** an app's window (the Bible reading Genesis 1, say) showed "Paused to keep your desk smooth" when the office itself was busy: a live Browser tile, a redraw, the desk link, another app or a slow machine. The frame budget counted every long frame in the page. Now only the view's own time counts.
- **Changed (how):** the view's boot script answers a probe of which thread it runs on, and a view whose frame runs in its own process (Chrome on a computer) is never paused, since it can't slow the office. On the office's thread (Chrome's headless shell, Chrome on Android, Firefox, Safari), with Long Animation Frames a frame counts only when the time before style and layout that none of the office's own scripts explains is over 50 ms; three such frames in 10 s while the office is idle, after the view's first 2 s, pause it, with Resume as before. Without Long Animation Frames only hangs count: two gaps over 1 s in 30 s. Non-production builds log why a pause happened.
- **Tests:** `web/src/deskapps/host/budget.test.ts` (23); `web/tests/e2e/deskapps.budget.shell.spec.ts` (4: a busy office for 20 s, which paused on `main`; a busy view paused and resumed; the real Bible view at 4x CPU for 20 s with a pan; no Long Animation Frames) and `deskapps.budget.chrome.shell.spec.ts` (1, Chrome itself). The office-made budget test left `deskapps.shell.spec.ts`.

## Unreleased - A change of account closes the last member's windows (`sub/account-change-windows`, ISS-89's follow-up, the office half; privacy; no words change)
- **Fixed (privacy):** a tab whose tokens became another member's (a sign-in in another tab of the same browser) kept the last member's open windows: a full-screen Browser with its picture and tab titles, a Notebook page, the Agent, Files, an installed app. Every window now closes at once, with no animation and nothing asked; focus leaves it, and the office's tab stop starts over on the new member's first desk.
- **Fixed (privacy, in memory):** every desk's session in the tab ends with what it said (the panels' words, tab and page titles among them, the agents' names and looks, the installed apps' records), and the new member's list opens their own. The Notebook's pages (text the desk hadn't confirmed included) and its close-guard hold, Files' transfers, the Browser's put-away protected-playback notes, Send to my desk's lines, the desk menu, the hidden list and a rename's field go too.
- **Fixed (privacy, storage):** the pictures kept of the last member's desks go when the account changes, as on every signed-out state; and the Browser keeps a picture of a desk's screen only while the office lists that desk, so the last member's Browser closing, or their desk's session ending, no longer keeps one beside the new member's tokens. The view's own key (`ma.view.v1`) is written only when what it keeps changes.
- **Tests:** `web/tests/e2e/accountChange.shell.spec.ts` (6, the bell's count among them; the window checks fail on `main` with 3 windows left), `web/src/state/accountChange.test.ts` (6), `web/src/apps/files/engines.test.ts` (2), 2 new in `web/src/notebook/deskBackend.test.ts`, 1 each in `browser/protectedPlayback.test.ts` and `browser/lastScreen.store.test.ts`, and the session's listener in `platform/session.signedOut.test.ts`. Status: `docs/sub-status/account-change-windows.md`.

## Unreleased - No blank-page tiles: a tile with nothing to read shows its icon, large and centred (`sub/tile-icon-fallback`; the Designer's brand review and re-read passed; rides Desk release 16)
- **Fixed (the owner's report, Nov 11):** a tile past icon size that wasn't live drew its widget card even with nothing on it: no summary, its words hidden at a far zoom, or the privacy shield on. It read as a blank page with a tiny glyph top left. Now a tile never draws a card with nothing readable on it (`tileFace` in `web/src/canvas/layout.ts`): from medium up to open, when it isn't live, it's its widget only with a title or text to read at the zoom that shows text (`lod` `full`); otherwise it wears the icon's look, its glyph centred at min(63% of the shorter side, max(128, 45% of it), 256) units (`faceGlyphSize`, the Designer's rule): an icon's and a medium tile's 63%, about 45% on a large or an open tile (158 and 200), never past 256. No head glyph, no label row. Radius, stacked look, badge, shield and marks rules are unchanged. An icon at the farthest zoom (`card`) stays a plain card (design 3.10).
- **Changed:** at the farthest zoom (`card`, under 20 px a cell) a larger tile now draws its glyph where it drew a plain card. An open tile whose app is open in its window says "Open in its window" under its glyph when it has no words. A corner drag draws the glyph at the size it lands at, from one 256 bitmap. The face is chosen at draw time with the text's visibility, so a zoom past a threshold or a tile going live and back swaps at once. A phone's grid follows the same rule with no change of its own.
- **Added (mock only):** `?widgets=none` on the mock desk (`MockDeskLink`'s summaries and the mock desk apps' rows): badges and marks with no widget words.
- **Performance:** a larger tile's glyph is a small sprite of its own over the plain card its size shares, not baked into a bitmap per app. At the farthest zoom, in the worst office measured, a zoom's settle draw is about 9 ms against `main`'s 6 (baked, it was twice `main`'s); a pan's draws are unchanged (0.5 to 0.6 ms). `spriteBytes` joins the dev-only test hook.
- **Tests:** `web/src/canvas/layout.test.ts` (9 new: the face for each level, LOD and words; what's readable; the glyph's size, one case per regime, and that it never shrinks); `web/tests/e2e/tilefaces.shell.spec.ts` (6, each failing on `main`: no words at medium, wide, large and open not live, at 75, 50 and 25 %, read from the nodes and the canvas's pixels; with words the widget, and the glyph once they're hidden; an open tile live and back, every frame; the window note; a phone's grid, with and without words); `shell.spec.ts`'s no-data widget now expects the glyph, not a name row. Shots: `tilefaces.shots.shell.spec.ts` (`TILE_ICON_SHOTS`); the `card` cost: `tilefaces.perf.shell.spec.ts` (`TILE_CARD_PERF`). Initial JS 249.0 KB (about 248.9 on `main`). Status: `docs/sub-status/tile-icon-fallback.md`.

## Unreleased - The office in a desk's Browser lands on the portal (`sub/portal-redirect`; deskd only; in no release)
- **Added (deskd, `browser/PortalRedirect`):** a top-level visit to the member's own office (`office.` or `desk.`, prod or staging, and any name under them) in the desk's Browser lands on `https://portal.minimalistalliance.com/` (`portal.staging.` on a staging desk) instead of Chromium's "This page is blocked". deskd's own `browser.tab.navigate` and `browser.tab.open` go straight there; a link, a redirect or `window.open` has its blocked page replaced (`location.replace` from deskd's isolated world), so the office never stays in the tab's history and Back from the portal leaves at once. `api.`, `auth.` and `gw.`, frames, lookalikes and the Agent's tabs stay as they were. Every band, cloud and self-hosted.
- **Fails closed:** the image's policy still blocks the office; with deskd down or `DESKD_PORTAL_URL=off` (new, in `deskd.env`) the member sees Chromium's page as before. The portal page itself is another branch's.
- **Tests:** `PortalRedirectMatchTest` (10), `PortalRedirectTest` (23), `PortalRedirectChromiumIT` (10, on real Chromium 156 and 158 with the image's URLBlocklist). `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §10, "The office in the Browser"; `docs/sub-status/portal-redirect.md`.

- **Fixed:** `selfhost/install.sh` pairs with prod, your office, unless given `--env staging`, which is for testers. ## Unreleased - The member's other caches go on every signed-out state too (`sub/lsub-iss89-caches`, ISS-89; privacy; no words change)
- **Fixed (privacy):** beside the saved layout's mirror (ISS-67), this browser kept more of the last member after every signed-out state: their id and stream quality choices by desk (`ma.streamQuality.v1`, `ma.streamQuality.desks.v1`), the boots told of their own desks (`ma.boots.seen.v1`), the bandwidth heads-up seen (`ma.bandwidth.notice.v1`), the Guild Desk's privacy line (`ma.guild.privacy.v1`), the ids of up to 20 members shown the touch hint (`ma.browser.touchHint.v1`), the connect-time report's cap (`ma.rttCapped.v1`), the owner's About (`ma.menu.about.v1`), and in the tab Maia's conversation and open panel (`ma.maia.conversation.v1`, `ma.maia.open.v1`). All go now, on ISS-67's own hooks (`MEMBER_CACHE_KEYS` and `MEMBER_TAB_KEYS` in `web/src/state/layoutStore.ts`), per account by its owner key (in the tab's sessionStorage too, so a reload under another account drops the tab's), and none is written while `layoutMirrorWritable()` says no.
- **Fixed (per account, in the tab):** a tab whose tokens become someone else's drops the desk list at once and shows it loading while it asks for the new member's own (an answer asked for the last member lands nowhere; any desk the new list names again draws at once, at the places the teens fix gives it), clears the live region's last line, drops Maia's chat and state and asks again for the new member, drops the boots told, and starts stream quality over, rather than keeping the last member's until a reload or the next call.
- **Fixed (a stale tab):** a tab another tab signed out, still drawing its member on the access token it holds, wrote that member's layout mirror, desk names (`ma.desks.v1`), last-known record, kept pictures and Maia keys back once anyone signed in there. The shared guard now also asks that what's stored is this tab's account's, a sign-in names the mirror at its first token, and the desk list's mirror, the last-known record and the kept pictures take the guard too.
- **Fixed (cross-tab):** another tab's sign-out wipes this tab's own sessionStorage keys even when its message arrives before the other tab's removal of the token from localStorage does (`onTokenGoneElsewhere` says whether the token gone was this tab's).
- **Changed:** after a signed-out state, a boot of the member's own desk from the last day, this week's bandwidth heads-up, the touch hint and the Guild Desk's privacy line may each show once more; the owner's About starts shut.
- **Tests:** `web/src/state/memberCaches.test.ts` (18), `web/src/platform/session.signedOut.test.ts` (every path, the caches seeded), `web/src/quality/store.test.ts` (4 new), `web/src/browser/lastScreen.store.test.ts` (1 new); `web/tests/e2e/memberCaches.shell.spec.ts` (7, each failing on `main`). Initial JS 248.6 KB (249.1 on `main`). Status: `docs/sub-status/lsub-iss89-caches.md`.

- **Changed (docs):** design section 10.5 and its rows (lines near 107, 169, 801, 948), ADR 0026's amendment note, and the ISS-64 status doc say a visible tab with no input on a free or an Alliance desk lets go after 2 hours (24 hours on every other desk; sound holds the 2 hours, not the 24). - **Changed, `web/src/desk/idle.ts`, `desk/limited.ts`, `desk/liveStore.ts`:** on a free or an Alliance desk (the tiers with an idle limit) a visible tab with no input on that desk closes its sessions after 2 hours, not 24, so the desk's own stop (30 minutes by default on a free desk, 20 on an Alliance desk) follows. Officer, Founder (role lead), Guild and self-hosted desks keep 24 hours; the hidden tab's 60 minutes is unchanged. Sound in the desk exempts the 2 hours only (the same signal as ADR 0038); a running file transfer still counts as input. ## Unreleased - A change of account in a tab keeps the listed desks drawn (`sub/lsub-iss67-teens-fix`; fixes a regression from ISS-67's `371b774d`; Desk release 15)
- **Fixed:** when another tab's sign-in made this tab's tokens another account's, ISS-67 emptied the tab's layout (rightly: it was the last account's arrangement) and nothing gave the desks the list still showed a layout until the list answered again, up to a minute later, so the office drew no desk at all. Those desks now get a new desk's places at once (nothing of the last account's arrangement), and the age rules fit them as before once `/v1/me` answers: `teens.shell.spec.ts`, "a teen's sign-in in another tab takes the Agent from this one…", passes again. Nothing of it is written to the mirror until the new account's own change or list.
- **Tests:** `web/src/state/layoutMirror.test.ts` and `web/src/platform/session.signedOut.test.ts` each gain the case, and fail on `main` `ddd27e8b`; `layoutSync.shell.spec.ts`'s change-of-member test now checks the desk is drawn at a new desk's place (not the last member's, not the dropped answer's). Status: `docs/sub-status/lsub-iss67-teens-fix.md`. Rides Desk release 15.

## Unreleased - Fewer copies of an LLM key in deskd's and desk-agent's memory (`sub/lsub-iss20-key-zero`, ISS-20; deskd and desk-agent, nothing a member sees changes)
- **Fixed (security):** deskd's link to desk-agent wrote each frame from a heap buffer, so NIO copied it into a temporary direct buffer that it caches per carrier thread and never zeroes. A KEY frame's key, at the frame's end, stayed there past later, shorter writes. `AgentLink` now writes every frame through a direct buffer of its own and zeroes it after each piece (`writeThrough`).
- **Fixed (security):** deskd parsed a client's RPC request with jackson's shared buffer pool, which hands out the same `char[]` again without zeroing it, so an `agent.key.add` key stayed reachable until a longer string covered it. Requests now parse through `Json.parseRequest`, whose buffers aren't pooled and go with their parser: about 0.5 KiB more allocated per request. Every other parse keeps the pool.
- **Fixed (security, the Desk Engineer's review):** deskd's `rtc.sock` read buffer, which lives as long as the connection, kept the bytes of every envelope it had handled past the start a later, shorter read covers, an `agent.key.add` request among them. `RtcClient.drain` now zeroes what each read brought once its envelopes are handled, and the buffer is zeroed when the connection ends.
- **Changed:** desk-agent's `KeyLease.headerValue` makes one `String` of the prefix and the key, joined in an array it zeroes, rather than two.
- **Added:** desk-agent's `test-release-scripts.sh` fails if anything a desk runs names `jdk.httpclient.HttpClient.log`, `jdk.internal.httpclient.debug` or `javax.net.debug`, which would log a provider key's header or TLS plaintext.
- **Tests:** `AgentKeyTest` 2 new, `JsonTest` 2 new, `KeyRingTest` 1 new, `RtcClientReadTest` 2 new (both fail without the `rtc.sock` fix), and the release script's 3 "http logging" checks. The finding, path by path, with the uids, the risk and the options not taken: `docs/sub-status/iss20-key-zeroing-finding.md`. Security cleared it at `974b2db2` with no code findings (ISS20-P1 Low and ISS20-I1 Info, both outside this branch's code). Ships with the next deskd and desk-agent releases; release 3 is unaffected. Status: `docs/sub-status/lsub-iss20-key-zero.md`.

- **Fixed:** dismissing the Guild Desk's privacy line writes `ma.guild.privacy.v1` only while `layoutMirrorWritable()` (`web/src/guild/GuildLayer.tsx`), as `theme/backdrop.ts` does. ISS-89 wipes the key on sign-out; without this guard a tab that another tab had signed out could write it back. After such a sign-out the dismissal lasts for that page only.

## Unreleased - The AI tester fleet's map of the office (`web/public/fleet-map.json`), checked in the shell suite (ma-platform ADR 0134 addendum, build group G4; `sub/lsub-fleet-office-map`, Lead Subcontractor for the UI/UX Engineer; status `docs/sub-status/fleet-office-map.md`)
- **Added:** `web/public/fleet-map.json`, the fleet's selector map for the office (`office.*`: the shell, the desk and tile buttons of the proxy layer, the home mark and desk switch, windows and their Close, open tiles, the desk's menu and Apps sheet, the account menu's Theme radios, the Browser's bar and picture; `bible.*`: the Bible's frame and, inside its real view, Go to, the translations, Search, verses and highlights). Vite copies `public/` as is, so every build serves its map beside `ma-release.json` (`/v/<version>/fleet-map.json`). Every selector is the app's own: a `data-testid`, a role and name, or its own class or id; no app file is changed and no test id added. The file's `_doc` gives each build group its block: G4's `bible.*` and `office.*` (route `office`), and G5's `apps.*`, `maia.*`, `notebook.*`, `files.*` and `commons.*` (route `apps`), merged as two blocks with no key in common.
- **The rule for anyone changing `web/`:** a change to an element a map key names updates the map in the same commit; the map checks below fail until it does.
- **Tests:** `web/tests/e2e/fleet-map.shell.spec.ts` (shell project, 9): every `office.*` key and `bible.frame` resolves on the harness office in the state it names, and a coverage test fails on a key in no state that isn't `"check": false` with a reason. `apps/bible/view/tests/fleet-map.bible.spec.ts` (the Bible view's suite, 3): every other `bible.*` key resolves on the real view against the real `bible-app`, the error state included.

## Unreleased - The tileSync unit test waits for the look's chunk itself (`sub/lsub-tilesync-flake`; test only, nothing a member sees; UI/UX approved `2abb897d`; rides Desk release 16)
- **Tests:** `web/src/agent/tileSync.test.ts`, "a new agent gets a tile…", failed now and then in full runs on a busy box, at about 1 s. It gave the agent look's lazy chunk (`src/agent/lookChunk.ts`) `vi.waitFor`'s default 1 s to arrive, and in a full run with more workers than free CPUs, Vite's first transform of that chunk can take longer. The product promises no time: a tile is blank until the chunk lands. The test now loads the chunk with its other imports, waits for `lookReady` through the roster store's own change with no deadline of its own, and checks that the tile is blank first. With 16 workers on 1 CPU it failed 3 of 6 full runs before and 0 of 6 after; on 0.5 CPU, 2 of 2 before and 0 of 2 after. No product code changed. Status: `docs/sub-status/lsub-tilesync-flake.md`.

## Unreleased - Moving and resizing with no drag: the edit's own row in the bottom bar (WCAG 2.5.7, the Product Designer's pick; `sub/lsub-desk-tile-keys`, Lead Subcontractor for the UI/UX Engineer)
- **Added:** while a desk or an app is moved or resized (`m`, `r`, or Move and Resize in its menu), the bottom bar's row is a quiet label naming it ("Moving Personal", "Resizing Notebook"; the group's name too), then six buttons: ← ↑ ↓ →, Keep and Restore. Each arrow steps it one grid cell (92 units); moving, they're "Move left", "Move up", "Move down" and "Move right", and resizing they move its bottom-right corner, "Narrower", "Shorter", "Taller" and "Wider". Keep and Restore do what Enter and Esc do. An open tile being moved or resized keeps its frame's dashed ring while the row has the keyboard. 36 px buttons, a whole 44 px target each with a finger, the office's chevrons and chips; one row in short-viewport mode too. So a mouse or a finger moves and resizes with presses, no drag (`chrome/EditBar.tsx`, in the popovers chunk, so the first load grows only by the bar's switch to it, 16 B; the arrows call `step` in `shell/arrange.ts` with `G.pitch`).
- **The keyboard** goes to the first arrow as the edit starts and back to the tile as it ends, whichever way. The keys are as they were: the arrows step 8, Shift 40, Enter keeps, Esc restores, Tab keeps and goes on; Space presses the button it's on. A press on the row goes on with the edit; a press anywhere else still keeps it. The tile keeps its dashed ring while the row has the keyboard. Dragging is unchanged.
- **Changed:** the hints' edit line ("arrows move · shift 40 · enter keeps · esc restores") gives way to the row while it's up.
- **Tests:** `editbar.shell.spec.ts` (both modes, every arrow, Keep, Restore, focus in and out, the keys, the menu by mouse, a drag, 640 x 360 in three themes, 1440 x 900, a finger's 44 px); `bars`, `a11y` and `shell.spec.ts` updated where they read the hints or the focus during an edit. Status: `STATUS.md`.
