## Unreleased - The connect-time round trip respects its 429 (`sub/rtt-429`; not shipped)
- **Fixed:** a member past the contract's 24 reports a UTC day no longer sends a refused `POST /v1/desks/{id}/rtt` (and logs a console error) on every connection. After a `429` the office remembers, in `localStorage` with the member's `sub`, to send nothing until `Retry-After` (or the next 00:00 UTC if it is missing); until then it doesn't even measure. The `e2e/` console check allows only that one 429 on `/rtt`.

## Unreleased - The notification bell in the top bar (`sub/notifications-office`; ma-platform ADR 0125 §9, contract notifications.md 0.3.72; not shipped)
- **A bell between Full screen and the account menu** (`web/src/chrome/Bell.tsx`): the member's notifications, one list across the office, the account site and Community Forums, read at `/v1/me/notifications…` with the office's own token. A small count in the accent tint, never red, nothing at 0 and "9+" past nine; the button's name says the real number. Hidden until `/v1/me` answers, and while the kill switch answers `503 notifications_paused`.
- **The list** opens as the account menu does (a dialog; Esc, a click outside, the keyboard back to the bell, Tab ringing inside): newest first, one line an item with a family glyph, the title, the body, how long ago, and an accent dot while unread. A click marks the item read and opens its link in this tab. "Mark all read" while anything is unread, "All notifications" to the account site, "Nothing new." when the list is empty. Opening says `seen`; it marks nothing read.
- **While the service is absent (404) or paused, the bell asks about every 10 minutes** instead of once a minute (`UNREAD_SLOW_POLL_MS`), and goes back to once a minute as soon as a count or a 304 arrives; Vitest for the interval, Playwright for the 404 (no bell, no "Notifications" Tab stop, no second ask).
- **The count** (`web/src/platform/notifications.ts`) is asked at most once a minute while the tab is visible, with the ETag (a 304 keeps the last value), again when the tab comes back, never while hidden, and not at all once signed out.
- **The panel renders the shared spec** (`sub/notifications-office-kit`; `packages/bell-kit`, a vendored copy of ma-platform's `packages/bell-kit` 1.0.0, ADR 0125): the Product Designer found the three bells (Community Forums, the account site, the office) had drifted into three layouts, so all three now render one `SPEC.md` from one row model. `Bell.tsx` imports `rowsOf`, `countLabel` and `bellLabel` from the kit and has no words of its own any more (the local relative time, count text, family map and " · and N more" are gone). The layout as the spec: a header only while something is unread ("Notifications" 14/600, "Mark all read" as a text link right); rows with a 28 px round sunken chip holding the 16 px kind icon by family (`thread`, `card`, `desk`, `account`, `note`; `note` is new in `icons.tsx`), the title 14/500 to two lines ("Ada and 2 others replied in Quiet mornings"), the second line 13 px on one line and cut ("Community Forums"), the time ("4 min", "1 h", "2 d", then "Nov 1") with an 8 px accent dot under it while unread; "All notifications" alone, left-aligned; "Nothing new." with no header; `system.quiet` rows not shown; the header's and the footer's links 44 px tall on a touch screen. The 404/paused and 10-minute poll behaviour is unchanged. `vite.config.ts` `fs.allow` lists the kit.
- `web/src/chrome/icons.tsx`: `bell`, `thread`, `card`, `screen`, `note`. `web/src/styles/app.css`: the `.bell-*` rules, from existing tokens only. The `TopBar.tsx` comment names the bell as the bar's one exception. `web/harness/notifications.mjs` answers the routes for the specs; the fixtures (the dev server's and the spec's) carry `actor`, `count` and `body`.
- Tests: Vitest `notifications.test.ts` (12, the poll and the count) and `chrome/bell.test.ts` (4, the rows against the kit's `rowsOf`), the kit's own `packages/bell-kit/tests`; `membership.shell.spec.ts`'s list of the top bar's Tab stops gains "Notifications"; Playwright `notifications.shell.spec.ts` (12: the count and 9+, the list in the kit's words, the header and the footer, a click marks read, Mark all read, the empty state, the kill switch, the 404, Esc and focus, the Tab ring, 375 px, axe in Light, Dusk and Dark).

## Unreleased - Picture quality in layers, kept on Platform: each desk's own level and each app's on it (S-83; `sub/stream-quality-rest`; not shipped)
- **The owner's rule:** "each desk is its own space so configs should not be shared across desks unless they are set at the account level. configs at the desk level defer to account if not specified or override if set". An app's level on a desk is the first that is set of the app on that desk, the desk, the app on every desk, the account's default, then High (`levelOnDesk`, `web/src/quality/levels.ts`); a default never chosen reads as not set, not as High.
- **The Browser's Picture quality menu** sets the Browser's level on this desk, with "Use this desk's level (…)" or "Use my default (…)" to put it back, and a "This desk" row that turns the menu to the desk's own level for every app on it (Right and Left, or Enter and Esc, in and out). Both save to Platform (`ma-platform` contract 0.3.70, `PUT /v1/me/stream-quality` `desks`), so they follow the member to any browser and reach no other desk; a failed save is put back with the usual line.
- **Before:** a desk's level lived only in the browser that chose it (`ma.streamQuality.desks.v1`). It moves to Platform once, quietly, under anything Platform already has. Against a Platform without the desk layer the menu works as before and "This desk" isn't shown.
- **Unchanged:** the four levels, their words, `QUALITY` on `keys` (stream 0), `desk-rtc`, `desk-stream` and `deskd`.
- **Tests:** Vitest `levels.test.ts` (every combination of set and not set, overlay and merge, what moves) and `store.test.ts` (the desk layer on Platform, an older Platform, moving a browser's copy, a failed move, another member); Playwright `browser.quality.shell.spec.ts` (This desk and its page, the layers end to end against the harness, keyboard, a failed save, an older Platform, the move, a reload, phones and desktop in Light, Dusk and Dark). `harness/streamQuality.mjs` answers as contract 0.3.70 does, and `streamQualityBefore` as before it. Docs: ADR 0039 "Changed 2026-11-10", `docs/interfaces/stream-quality.md` section 2.

## Unreleased - Refreshed TURN servers reach the connection (`sub/turn-refresh`; ma-platform ADR 0120, contract gateway.md 2.4, 0.3.58 draft; not shipped)
- A desk session's TURN credentials expire with the token they came with (about 5 minutes), and every `session.refreshed`, and a resume's `session.opened`, carry new ones. The Desk app used only the first set, so after they expired an ICE restart over the relay couldn't allocate and a new session was opened instead. Now the session's connection takes each new set with `setConfiguration({...getConfiguration(), iceServers})`, so the next ICE restart allocates with live credentials. `[]` (the relay paused) is used as given; a set the browser refuses leaves the last one, and is said nowhere. A new session uses only its own `session.opened` servers, never an earlier session's. Credentials are kept only in the connection and never logged.
- `web/src/desk/rtc/peer.ts`: `PeerSession.useIceServers`. `web/src/desk/rtc/gateway.ts`: a reply that carries `iceServers` also goes on to the links. `web/src/desk/rtc/rtcDeskLink.ts`: one line hands it to the session's own connection, so a late reply for an earlier session changes nothing.
- Tests: `peer.test.ts` (+3), `gateway.test.ts` (5, new), `rtcDeskLink.turn.test.ts` (5, new: the real gateway and link on a fake WebSocket; a refresh, an ICE restart, a reconnect, a resume, a refused set, and no credential in any log line).

- `web/tests/e2e/notebookDesk.ts`: `fake-indexeddb/auto` installs on `window` when there is one, and `filesDesk.ts` puts a stand-in `window` up first when `files.rtc.spec.ts` shares the worker, so `globalThis.indexedDB` was never set and the Notebook `rtc` specs failed with "indexedDB is not defined" in a full run (they passed alone). The setup now puts the fake database's globals on `globalThis` whichever object got them.
- `web/tests/e2e/browser.rtc.spec.ts`: the Picture quality menu spec (S-83) read the desk layer under the fixtures' desk id, but the `rtc` stack lists `DESK_ID` (`rtcStack.ts`), so the PUT it waited for was under another key and it timed out. It now uses `DESK_ID`.
- No app code changed.

The address now keeps at least 160 px, and the line gives way: its sentence shortens with an ellipsis, and its icon and Undo stay. The office's welcome card in that view balances its body text, so the last line isn't one word ("home."). No mouse or desktop change.

## Unreleased - One connect-time round trip a session, where the Platform asks (`sub/region-rtt`; ma-platform ADR 0112 P4, contract 0.3.69 draft; not shipped)
- `web/src/desk/rtc/connectRtt.ts`: 3 s after a session to the member's own desk first connects (never a Guild Desk's), it reads the selected candidate pair's `currentRoundTripTime` 5 times, 1 s apart. It sends the median of the samples on the pair's latest path (at least 3) and whether the pair is relayed (TURN), once, to `POST /v1/desks/{deskId}/rtt` with a fresh `Idempotency-Key`. That happens only where desk-web client-config has `connectRtt.enabled`, which no env sends until the Platform's part is applied. No location, time or id is sent: the Platform adds the day, the desk's region and the viewer's US state, and keeps counts only. It never throws, retries or logs, and a failure changes nothing in the session. Client-config and the API are loaded only when a report is due.
- `web/src/desk/rtc/peer.ts`: one line calls it as the `RTCPeerConnection` is made.
- Tests: `connectRtt.test.ts` (15): the median, relayed detection (either candidate), Firefox's selected pair, too few samples, off by client-config, a closed or failed session, a moment disconnected, send once per connection (an ICE restart starts nothing), and every failure staying silent.

## [Unreleased]

- deskd, the simplified view reads listings and engines as their results (task 47 round 6, `docs/sub-status/simple-view-quality.md`; distiller v14; in no release): an engine's snippets are whole, their bold query words included, without address lines or crumbs, and a search with a long AI answer stays a list of results; eBay's sponsored carousels and promoted store, a shop's promotion strips and tiles, deals carousels, feedback asks and newsletter sign-ups leave the reading; a store's header lines and filter links join the menu; Best Buy's grid drawn as rows is one grid, and its view stays while the grid redraws instead of falling back; a product's facts are its meta and a card's carousel buttons and tile noise go; GitHub's issue tabs and dates read as one line, a post's byline is one line, swatches are named, and a footer without a landmark, a pronunciation button and a reference's Jump up leave the reading. Measured on 58 real pages with three new raters before and after, no scroll, and the sites' real pages in place of their bot checks: better or clean fallback 45 to 47 (78% to 81%); two fresh raters on the member candidate: 38 and 45 of 58.
- deskd, the simplified view reads the pages it read worse or the same (task 47 round 5, `docs/sub-status/simple-view-quality.md`; distiller v13; in no release): a forecast keeps its current conditions first and lists its days with their pictures and highs; a product page shows its price once, its first photo, its swatches by colour and its rating as stars, with no read time; a full-width player in a short window is the page's video, and comments in a read-only text box are text; a front page reads each story once, its headline the link and its kicker a quiet line; a link aggregator keeps each story's score and comment count and its next page; Hacker News' header and the Internet Archive's section links join the menu, and a comment's ▲ is the vote control; an issue list's menu buttons are Filters and its density switches go; an engine's own tools, chips, ad searches and address lines leave its results, and its answer is one block; a fundraiser's ask in a live blog is hidden; a docs page's contents are its Contents and its callouts read "Tip: …"; a recipe's rating and times stay under its title; a shop tile's leftovers and pictures that will never load stay out. Measured on 58 real pages with three new raters before and after, no scroll: better or clean fallback 37 to 44 (64% to 76%), worse 9 to 2.
- deskd, the simplified view reads forums, repos and listings better (task 47 round 4, `docs/sub-status/simple-view-quality.md`; distiller v12; in no release): a Discourse thread's replies are there in a member's tab, read from the topic the page already loaded (its own JSON, parsed inert, nothing fetched), and its announcement banner is one line; a GitHub repo's About is its facts and its file list shows ten rows, so the README follows close under the title; a store's long filter rail and its filter chips fold into Filters, product tiles are cards before their pictures draw, and ads known by their own link's click id drop wherever they sit; an engine's results are cards by their own titles, and its tabs, related searches and feedback line leave the reading; a docs page's breadcrumbs are one line, and its contents list, heading anchors and Copy page stay out; a player that draws its own poster gives the view that still; a bot check drawn over a page, and a long page of which the view keeps almost nothing, open as the real page. Measured on 58 real pages with the same three raters, no scroll: better or clean fallback 32 to 39 (55% to 67%).
- deskd, the simplified view has what loads as you scroll (task 47 round 3, `sub/simple-view-quality`, `docs/sub-status/simple-view-quality.md`; distiller v11; in no release): once per page, as the view first shows it, deskd scrolls the real page down to its end or 12 screens and back to exactly where it was, so lazy pictures, a thread's later replies and a list's next items are in the view; never on a sensitive page, with focus in a field, or after the member acts, and an action stops it at once (`services/deskd/docs/API.md` §14, "The pre-scroll"; `prescroll-off` turns it off). A store's product grid is cards even when a promo tile in it holds the page's title (Target); an engine's shopping ads drop when their click id sits inside its own redirect (Yahoo), and so do Amazon's sponsored-brand rows; a page's own display settings (Wikipedia's text size, width and colour) drop rather than showing as filters above the title; a docs page's definition reads as one item with its term (Python's signatures).
- desk-notebook, a mass path change no longer stalls the service: the readable Markdown copy (`readable/`) lets go of the event loop after each page it moves or writes, as the startup rebuild does, so renaming a notebook of 4,000 pages no longer blocks every member's editing for about 3 s on a loaded box. Order and end state are unchanged; SIGTERM's final export is still synchronous and cuts an in-flight run short. `src/readable.ts`; test `tests/readable.test.ts` (4,000 moves: event-loop lag under 100 ms, every file in place; 2.8 s before).
- deskd, the simplified view reads better (task 47, `sub/simple-view-quality`, `docs/sub-status/simple-view-quality.md`; distiller v10; in no release): a video deskd found plays in the view rather than saying it plays only on the real page (TED, Wikimedia Commons, the Internet Archive); an encyclopedia's infobox and thumbnails follow the lead rather than coming before it; a story's opening, standfirst and photo stay when a hidden consent box or a small promo player sat elsewhere on the page (Ars Technica); a shop's filter rail folds into Filters even when nothing names it a filter (Best Buy), and a rating's link never names a product; shopping ads known by an ad network's click id drop with their row, and an ad module's leftover switch with them; a cookie banner in the page's own flow is hidden and counted, never pressed (GOV.UK); a Q&A, forum or listing shows no read time; an issue list's "Filter by author" is the issue's author, not a button. The real-site harness saves the view's images and the original's first screens for a rater, and can leave the page unscrolled.
- Desk app, the Dusk theme is **Zen** (the owner, Nov 10; `sub/zen-theme`, ADR 0093; in no release yet): the name members read in the account menu ("Zen", "Use Zen when the system is dark"), a natural palette: a cool slate office in MA's own blue-grey with cool near-white text (the owner, the same evening: "I hate brown"; a warm stone and linen version came first), MA's blue softened as the accent, a soft green for online and a cool gold for warn, the bars' pills a deep slate, the mark all white, and the four desk textures as the one slightly warm thing: wood at low chroma (a greyed light oak, a pale greyed ash, a dark greyed walnut) and a cool grey linoleum. The theme's id stays `dusk`, so every saved choice keeps working; Light and Dark are unchanged.
- Desk app, office backgrounds (the Zen theme's look; `sub/zen-backgrounds`, ADR 0094; in no release yet): a calm nature scene behind the desks, ten to choose from in the account menu under Theme ("Background": None, then a swatch per scene, the chosen one named under the row), saved per member in the layout document's `prefs.background` so it follows them to every browser. Zen shows the first scene with nothing chosen; Light and Dark show none until a member picks one. A light theme-aware scrim (`surface.backdrop`) lies over the picture; text never sits on bare ground (the bars' words are on their pills), and while a scene shows the active desk's ring and every focus ring over the ground carry a 1 px dark halo (`ring.halo`) so they read on any picture, measured in a test over each scene's average colour; the picture fades in over 300 ms after the canvas's first paint (no fade under reduced motion), from AVIF with WebP behind it at 960 to 3840 wide, 1920 at most for a browser saving data. "Plain background" hides it with the gradient. The scenes ship in `web/public/backgrounds/` (the Designer's graded set, ocean-cliff first: ocean-cliff, mountain-lake, misty-mountains, snow-ridge, pebble-shore, glacier-lagoon, snowy-forest, fjord, moonlit-sea, peaceful-city). Initial JS 241.7 to 242.2 KB gzipped.
- Desk app, regression specs (`sub/regression-specs`, `docs/sub-status/regression-specs.md`; tests only, nothing shipped): `apps.shell.spec.ts` opens and closes every app a member has, both ways, in all three themes, Commons on a Guild Desk included; `theme.shell.spec.ts` sets themes through the account menu's radios and checks the document, the canvas, theme-color, a reload, System's OS flips and the loader's boot color; `a11y.sweep.shell.spec.ts` runs axe over Maia's panel, `/export`, the signed-out card, the new-version notice and the account menu on desktop and phone; `signOutEverywhere.shell.spec.ts` adds Platform not answering (Sign out still ends here after 3 s; Sign out everywhere ends nothing after 15 s and says so) and a second tab ending up signed out; `release.spec.ts` adds Sign out everywhere under the real CSP.
- Desk app, two word calls (`sub/word-calls`): the Notebook's view switch reads "By tab" and "By day" (labels only), and the asleep button reads "Wake this desk", or "Wake the Guild Desk" on a Guild Desk, in the Browser, Notebook, Agent and Files.
- desk-agent, a conversation's lock is fair (main `0792de25`; in no release): while a reply streamed, something reading that conversation over and over, such as thread reads or a subscribe-and-snapshot loop, could keep taking its lock ahead of the reply's updates, so the reply barely moved. A thread dump showed the reply parked in `TopicHub.publish`; it was starvation, not a deadlock. `TopicHub`'s per-topic `ReentrantLock` is now fair. `AgentServiceConcurrencyTest` stops its loops in a `finally`. With 2 CPUs it failed 7 of 10 runs before and 0 of 10 after.
- desk-agent, test only (`sub/timers-flake`): `TimersTest.aOneShotTimerMissedByLessThanADayStillRunsAtStart` (and any test that restarts with a due timer) failed at random. The test desk started the timers' thread and stopped it again, and on a busy machine the thread fired the missed timer first. The timer code was right: a one-shot under a day late runs once at start, and an older one ends missed. `AgentService.restore(boolean startTimers)` lets the test desk never start the thread; `restore()` is unchanged. No change to what members get.
- deskd, reliability (audit D1 to D5 and D7, `sub/deskd-reliability`, `docs/sub-status/deskd-reliability.md`; in no release): desks rate-limited at the gateway come back spread over 30 to 60 s (or the handshake's `Retry-After`, up to 5 minutes, plus as much again) rather than all at +30 s; after a gateway deploy drops healthy links they come back over 0 to 5 s rather than inside one second; a failed heartbeat goes again at 16 s, backing off to the period, not a whole minute later, and boot's retries are spread; a desk whose desk-rtc doesn't come up says hello after 30 s with the identity desk-rtc had before, so the Platform sees it with `rtc: failing` (sessions still get `desk_not_ready` until desk-rtc is up); the heartbeat's `dataVolume` is `failing` under 64 MiB free, which a full disk used to pass as `ok`; `nextHeartbeatSeconds` is held to 2 minutes and any `Retry-After` to 5; the links to desk-agent and desk-notebook back off from a peer that accepts and drops at once. And desk-notebook's storage state (main `6216d1e8`) shows in `/health` as `notebookStorage` and in the heartbeat as `health.checks.notebook` (`degraded` while its writes fail, `failing` once it refuses edits). Tests: `GatewayLinkTest`, `HeartbeatLoopTest`, `BootSequenceTest`, `AgentLinkBackoffTest`, `NotebookFailureTest`, `NotebookStorageTest`.
- Desk app, small UX (`sub/apps-ux-office`): Chat's empty state says "Chat is on its way" and "Nothing's kept here yet." whether or not the desk is connected; the keyboard shortcuts list adds a second list, "In an app" (Notebook search, Files rename and trash, sending in Agent and Maia, the Browser's address bar, the menu key), with ⌘ for Ctrl on a Mac; on a phone in portrait the Browser's picture sits at the top of its surface, not mid-screen (`.br-video` height auto, 16:9).
- Desk app, the office's membership cards C, D and E (S-82, the membership card proposal; the owner's OK, Nov 6; `sub/membership-cards-ce`, `docs/sub-status/membership-cards.md`; in no release yet; ma-platform contract `desks.md` 0.3.69, draft): with no desks listed, `/v1/me`'s `stoppedBy`, `stoppedAt` and `dataDeleteAt` pick **C, "Your desks are paused"** (`suspended` with `payment`, the deletion date ahead: both dates, Update payment to the billing page, Write to us), **D, "Your membership has ended"** (`ended` with `member`, the date ahead: Rejoin, and Ask for an export, which goes to the office's own `/export` where client-config turns it on and by email elsewhere) and **E, "Welcome back"** (the same after the date: A's picture, See membership and A's Alliance line while Pay is open; a teen's has one desk, no Alliance line, and a button only while Alliance is on sale). Words exactly as the proposal's, without the restore promise until TD-110 passes; dates in the member's own time zone, in bold, the one emphasis. `other`, an end after a failed payment, a missing field or a date that doesn't read stay F, with no date or reason, so a safety suspension or a hold is never named. `shell/deskNotice.ts` `stopCard`, `chrome/membershipCards.tsx`, `platform/clientConfig.ts` `billingUrl` and `exportPageUrl`; tile G stays a draft. Unit `deskNotice.test.ts`, `membershipCards.test.ts`; `membership.shell.spec.ts` (C, D and E in Light, Dusk and Dark, desktop and phone, with axe; the export page; F for everything else).
- Desk app, Maia's first asks (`sub/apps-ux-maia`): while a chat is empty, three quiet buttons sit at the foot of her panel, "Show me around my office", "Open my Work desk" (where there is a Work desk) and "How do I give my Agent a key?" (adults); a press sends it as a normal turn, and they go once the chat has a message. `maia.shell.spec.ts` covers them.
- Desk app, the Agent's key screen (`sub/apps-ux-agent`): the form reads provider, key, Add key at a glance: the where-the-key-goes note shows its first sentence with "How the key travels" to open the rest (the same words), and the Add key row stays in view while the form scrolls under it, so on an 800 px screen the button is no longer below the fold. The failed-wake button says "Try again" in the Agent and the Browser, as everywhere else.
- `deskd` and `desk-agent`, a Guild Desk: leaving deletes the member's agent conversations (audit Z28, ADR 0080 decision 12 and its addendum of 2026-11-10; `sub/guild-member-purge`, `docs/sub-status/guild-member-purge.md`; held for Security's read, in no release yet). A newer roster that leaves a member out owes their purge, sent to desk-agent 10 minutes later; a roster that lists no Founder, has an unreadable entry or drops more than half the members at once is held until a later sound roster confirms, and a member listed again keeps everything. desk-agent deletes their agent, thread, files' notes, limits and staged files, keeps what they spent only as the guild's, and leaves no byte of them in its SQLite files or WALs; the audit log keeps its rows, which hold no content, and Notebook pages and Commons stay. Also: before any roster a Guild Desk tells members `desk_not_ready` (Z30), a SUB topic with a control character is `bad_request` (Z29), and `desk-runtime.md` says `/data/selfhost` is user `selfhost`'s (Z31).
- desk-notebook, a full disk no longer loses edits quietly (reliability audit D6, `sub/notebook-disk-full`, `docs/sub-status/notebook-disk-full.md`; not shipped): while writes fail, edits stay in memory and are retried with backoff (a page that closes keeps its unwritten edits too), the health frame says `storage.state: degraded` with the unsaved size, and an update is refused with the close reason `storage_full` once a page's waiting edits pass 1 MiB, all pages' pass 8 MiB or 60 s have gone by; recovery writes it all and clears the state. One error line per run, no page text. Tests: `tests/durability.test.ts` (`the disk is full`, 4, with SQLite's real `SQLITE_FULL`).

- Guild Desk, Notebook notebooks and tabs need `manage_spaces` (Security's `Z26`/`R4`, the Guild Engineer's call; `sub/guild-authz-r4`, `docs/sub-status/guild-authz-r4.md`; in no release yet): creating, renaming and recoloring notebooks and tabs (and adding a tab at a page) now needs the Founder or an Officer with `manage_spaces`, in desk-notebook and in deskd (`forbidden`, `{reason: "grant"}`); pages stay open to every member. The Desk app leaves out New notebook, New tab, rename, tab colors and "Add tab at this page" for a member without it, and shows one plain line if a change is refused anyway. A member's own desk is unchanged. Tests: notebook `guild.test.ts`, deskd `GuildRelaysTest`, `panel.test.ts`, `guild.shell.spec.ts`.

- desk-notebook, Trash holds against plain members on a guild desk (access-control audit, Medium; `sub/authz-5`; not shipped): `notebook.page.restore` needs `moderate` or being the one who trashed the page (`pages.deleted_by`, a column added without a schema bump, set at trash and cleared at restore), so a member can no longer undo a moderator's or the lead's trash; and until the 30-day purge a trashed page's `notebook.page.content`, `version.*` and collab open are `forbidden` (`reason: grant`) to a member without `moderate`, where they served and let them edit the text before. Personal and Work desks are unchanged. `src/rpc.ts`, `src/collab.ts`, `src/notebook.ts`, `src/db.ts`; tests `tests/guild.test.ts` (`authz-5 …`, 2); `docs/sub-status/notebook-guild.md`, `services/deskd/docs/API.md`.
- Desk app, a suspended member gets the export and the move as an ended one does (money audit R1, `sub/suspended-export`, `docs/sub-status/suspended-export.md`; contract `desks.md` 0.3.64, draft): `/export` treats `suspended` like `ended` (the export-only session per desk, the window's waking and retries, and the move panel, which an ended member didn't see before), and the line under the title is neutral for both: "Your membership isn't active. Your desks are kept until {date}, so you can take everything with you.", or "...kept for now..." with no date. A safety suspension is whatever the server answers: its refused export `connect` shows the desk's calm "can't be opened" line once, with no retry and no reason. Tests: `closed.test.ts`, `export.shell.spec.ts`.
- Desk app, the carousel on phones (the Product Designer's option 3, "Thumb row", for the launch demo's clip 3; `sub/phone-carousel`, in no release yet, waiting on the Designer's brand read): on a phone a tap on an app's tile opens it full screen in the desk's carousel, with its own head and a 56 px row of the desk's app glyphs at the bottom, the shown one marked, and "Back to the desk" at the row's right end. A tap on a glyph or a sideways swipe along the row moves to another app; a finger on the Browser's picture stays the page's. The head's back arrow, the row's button and Esc go back to the desk. The row steps aside while the on-screen keyboard is up for the app. On a phone this is simply how apps open, so nothing of it is saved and a larger screen's desk is left as it was; a desk a larger screen has in carousel opens on the phone on that app.
- Desk app, the export: README.txt and the part names put Notebook in its own `notebook/` folder, where desks keep it now, and `desk/` reads "Desk settings and Chat" (Security's `NE-3`; `sub/low-ne-3`, draft ADR "data export and transfer"; in no release yet).
- Desk app, the Browser's simplified view: a "Sign in on (site)" or "Use the real page" button drawn inside a link presses only itself, no longer the link too, and a middle click on one opens no desk tab (Security's `T47V-1`; `sub/low-t47v-1`, `docs/sub-status/simple-view.md`; in no release yet).
- Desk app, the accessibility pass of Nov 10, the office (`sub/a11y-office`, `docs/sub-status/a11y-office.md`): the Browser's tab strip is a group named "Tabs on this desk" with `aria-current` on the open tab, no longer a tablist holding buttons (O1); the bottom bar and the bandwidth circle draw over the windows while one of their controls has the keyboard (O2); a tile its own open window covers is no Tab stop until the window closes or moves (O3); Maia's panel head is a `div`, not a second banner (O4); an empty note title is named "Title" (O6). No sentence a member sees changes.
- Built avatars (`sub/acct-avatar`, the owner's pick "Cut paper"): the account avatar, and Commons' people list, voice rows and chat heads, draw a member's built avatar (`/v1/me` `avatar` of kind `built`, or the guild roster's `avatarRecipe`) with the vendored avatar kit, over the initials; the kit is its own lazy chunk (about 28 KB gz, none in the initial JS, 232.0 to 232.5 KB), a recipe that fails the check or a kit that fails to load is the initials. Commons checks the recipe again in Go (`ident.CleanRecipe`).

- deskd test `FilesUploadTest.partialsWithNoProgressForADayAreSweptAndRecentOnesStay` (`sub/fix-files-upload-test`; test only, no product change; for the Desk Engineer, who owns Files): it aged the partial's file times from the real clock but the sweep runs on the test clock (fixed in 2026), so it began failing on its own once the real date passed Nov 10 about 11:00 MT; the file times now come from the test clock.
- Desk app, Alliance Members moving up (`sub/alliance-moving-up`, `docs/sub-status/alliance-moving-up.md`): an adult Alliance member's bandwidth panel adds "{Officer} has {N} GB a week." and See membership, and the weekly Browser pause adds one quiet link, "{Officer} has {N} GB a week", under its unchanged words. Teens and other tiers see nothing new. Tier name and allowance are settings; until Platform sends Officer's weekly allowance the sentence reads "{Officer} has more each week."
- Profile picture (`sub/acct-picture`, ma-platform `profile-picture.md`): the account avatar, and Commons and Notebook presence, show a member's checked picture (Platform's public avatar route over https only) when `/v1/me` or the roster carries one, else the initials; a picture that fails to load is the initials again. The Desk never shows any other image address.
### Added
- Shared-host image, an upgrade in place through the host link (S-119, the free tier; `sub/s119-set-class`, ADR 0072 section 16, `docs/sub-status/s119-set-class.md`; in no bake yet: the next host image after 0.1.10 takes it, and no member is on a shared host): when a free member pays, Platform's `set_class` order now reaches the desk. `desk-host-link` runs it once per order ID as `desk-host class <k> --whole`, which grows the running container to its new class in place, with nothing restarted and the Browser's pages and cookies kept, and the report carries each running slot's `class` (contract 0.3.46), which Platform reads as the order's result. A raise the host has no room for changes nothing and waits for room, never half applied; a stopping host, an unknown class, another desk's order, a slot still starting or a tag that doesn't say the class are refused with nothing changed.
- Desk app, Maia's panel: **Your chats** (Security's `maia-2`, the Lead's go, Nov 10; `sub/maia-chat-list`; in no release yet). A list button in the header opens every chat the member has with her, newest first (`GET /v1/maia/conversations`); a row opens that chat as she kept it (`GET /v1/maia/conversations/{id}`) and the next message goes on in it; each row's Delete removes it for good (`DELETE /v1/maia/conversations/{id}`, a 404 counts as gone), and deleting the open one starts a new chat. Under the title: "Chats are kept {days} days. Open one to go back to it, or delete it sooner." Not while she's answering; Back returns focus to the button. `/privacy` promised this (a member can go back to a chat and delete it), and no screen did it before. Tests: `chats.test.ts` (7), `maia.shell.spec.ts`; the harness titles a chat from its first message and lists them newest first, as the server does.
- Desk app (`sub/no-empty-guild-spot`): no empty Guild Desk spot on the canvas. A Founder with no guild sees no Guild Desk, placeholder or Guild scope until the guild has a real Guild Desk; "Found your guild" in the setup circle is the way in. Removes `GuildSpot`, its card and the `guildSpotHiddenUntil` pref.
- Desk app, the setup circle's last fixes (S-121, `sub/setup-circle`): the Founder and Officer offer lines read "includes a guild of your own" and "{figure} of bandwidth a week" (or "has more each week" with no figure); at 320 px the panel's title is for screen readers only; the empty Guild scope has no desk switch; a skipped step shows a dash.
- Desk app, the setup circle (S-121, `sub/setup-circle`, `docs/setup-circle.md`, `docs/sub-status/setup-circle.md`; in no release yet): a quiet segmented ring left of the usage circle with the steps a membership includes, each done only by using it (opening the Work desk, the Browser showing a page, an Agent on a key, an upload in Files, founding, filling or joining a guild, naming a desk, Maia's trial), with Skip and Undo; a Founder with no guild gets "Found your guild" first and an empty Guild Desk spot on the canvas. Once setup is done or skipped it holds What's new and one quiet card for the next tier up, or nothing. "Hide this circle" and the account menu's "Show setup and what's new" are kept for the account, in the layout document, so they follow the member. Kill switches: client-config `setupCircle` and `whatsNew`.
- Desk app and `deskd`, the Browser says when a download starts and ends (LS-31; `sub/ls-31-36`, ADR 0092; in no release yet, reaches desks with the next `deskd` release, then the Desk app's): one quiet line at the picture's bottom left, "Downloading report.pdf 42%" and then "Saved report.pdf on your desk" for 6 seconds, said once through the polite live region. deskd hears Chromium's download events (`Browser.setDownloadBehavior` `default` with events, so files still go where the desk's policy says) and serves the new topic `browser.downloads` (the newest 5, in memory, names cleaned, no address). "Show in Files" opens the desk's Files window only for a file the desk says is in Files, which no image does yet.
- Desk app, a returning visit draws the avatar, its seal, the usage circle and the desks at once (the owner, Nov 10: "some items like the avatar on office take a while to load"; `sub/office-fast-start`, draft ADR `draft-office-fast-start.md`): what the office last drew for this member (`platform/lastKnown.ts`), then each answer from Platform replaces it (stale-while-revalidate). Per account (the record carries the `sub` and the sign-in it was written under), kept beside the refresh token (this tab's sessionStorage under "Until you close the browser"), and gone at sign-out, a refused refresh and a dead session. Only what the chrome draws: no email, status, age band, roles or tokens. The bundle also takes the loader's client-config answer once where the loader hands it over (`ma-platform` contract 0.3.60) and preconnects to sign-in and the API on a returning visit. Tests: `lastKnown.test.ts`, `fastStart.shell.spec.ts`.
- Notebook, context menus and color (the owner, Nov 10; `sub/notebook-color-menus`, with `sub/notebook-color`'s data; in no release yet): right click, a long press on touch, or Shift+F10 or the Menu key. On selected text: highlight, text color and callout from a row of named swatches, clear formatting, cut, copy and paste. On a block: callout and style. On a page in the list: a color label, rename, duplicate and delete (which still asks). The page list filters by one or several labels, or none, and sorts by label, remembered per notebook in this browser. On a phone a menu never covers the selection or the row. A swatch under the pointer previews on the text, the rows start on the color last used (kept in the member's prefs), and Turn into callout uses it at once. The Notebook wears its own scheme, "Paper and ink" (an indigo-leaning ink on warm paper), and six organizing colors named as the binder tabs (amber, blue, green, rose, violet, slate), the Product Designer's picks while the owner chooses. Ship order: desk-notebook and deskd first, then the Desk app.
- Desk app, carousel mode on each desk (the owner's ask, Nov 9; `sub/desk-carousel`, in no release yet): a switch at each desk's bottom right, beside the resize corner, turns the desk into a stage for one app at a time, the full live app across most of the desk, moved by a swipe (a finger, or two on a trackpad), the arrow keys or the stage's own buttons; the same switch goes back. Only the app on show runs. Remembered per desk in the member's layout; nothing set on one desk reaches another. Not on phones.
- Shared-host image, one public IPv4 per host (the owner's choice B, Nov 10: "OKay lets do it"; `SlotMode=port`, `sub/host-shared-address`, ADR 0072 sections 6 and 17, `docs/sub-status/host-shared-address.md`; in no bake yet: the System Engineer's 0.1.7 takes it, and no member is on a shared host): slot k on UDP 4430+k of the host's own address, every slot's ICE-TCP on the host's 443 through `desk-ice-demux`, which hands each connection to its slot by the `s<kk>` tag on its ICE ufrag from the client's own address; no Elastic IP or address per slot. `desk-rtc` 0.5.0 (published, on the staging test desk) tags its ufrags and names the host's address on a shared host's slot, and changes nothing elsewhere. Four more host checks; `verify-port.sh`. Measured on a lab host: both slots over UDP on their own ports, and with UDP blocked over TCP 443 with the same page rate and input latency, 40 of 40 sessions in.
- Desk app, Files: its own color scheme, "Steel teal" (the owner, Nov 10): the window, its side pane, buttons, focus ring, selected row, links and context menus take a teal-leaning tint of the office's blue in Light, Dusk and Dark; nothing outside the Files window changes. Values in `APP_INKS` (`theme/tokens.ts`); the selected row and drop target now use the accent tint. `web/CLAUDE.md` "The Files window".
- Notebook, colors as data (the owner, Nov 10; `sub/notebook-color`, ADR 0091, `docs/sub-status/notebook-color.md`; in no release yet, and the menus that use it are `sub/notebook-color-menus`): a highlight in the binder tabs' six colors, stored by name (`blue`, `green`, `amber`, `rose`, `violet`, `slate`; the old `yellow` kept and read as `amber`, the default), a text color mark, a color on any block (a callout), and a label on each page, so a green label, highlight and callout belong together and a theme never rewrites notes. Commands for the menus (`setHighlight`, `setTextColor`, `clearFormatting`, `setBlockColor`, `colorsAt`), `setPageLabel`, `duplicatePage` (text, colors and label, right after the original) and `importPage` on every backend, search by label, and the notebook's own file (`<title>.notebook.json`) that round-trips colors and labels; HTML export keeps the names and draws them, Markdown keeps `<mark>`. On the desk: `pages.label` (no schema bump), `notebook.page.label`, `notebook.page.duplicate` and `notebook.page.import`, relayed by deskd. Coloring another guild member's text is free under the edit budget. **The guard:** the app says its page schema in its collab token (`schema=2`), and `desk-notebook` refuses a change from an older editor to a page with colors it would strip, so a stale tab can't take a block's color off a shared page; it goes read-only (`reload`: "Notebook was updated. Reload to keep editing."; an app from before the guard gets `page_full`, its only read-only path), and still edits pages without colors. Ship `desk-notebook` and `deskd` before the app.
- Desk app, Maia's panel: an info circle right after "pass through us" (the owner, Nov 10; `sub/maia-kept-info`; in no release yet). A toggletip (`web/src/maia/KeptInfo.tsx`): a press or tap opens and closes it, keyboard focus opens it, Esc closes it alone (Maia stays open), and a press or focus elsewhere closes it; its words are in a live region, labelled "How Maia keeps chats". The Sales Engineer's final words, matching /privacy (`keptInfo`, with the server's `retentionDays`): "Nothing you send through Maia is used to train AI models. We keep chats for {days} days, so you can come back to them and so we can read them to fix problems and improve Maia and Minimalist Alliance. Then they're deleted. We never keep files you attach: your chat keeps only their names." No provider or model is named; the server keeps every member's chat off any provider whose terms let it train (ma-platform `mayTrain`). Tests: `keptLine.test.ts`, `maia.shell.spec.ts` (the circle's own test).
- Desk app, Files: a context menu in addition to the select-then-act toolbar, which is unchanged (the owner, Nov 10). Right click, a long press on touch, or Shift+F10 or the Menu key on the keyboard row or the list. On a file, folder or selection it holds the bar's actions in its order and words (Open for a single folder; Download and Rename only for one item); on empty space New folder and the uploads; in the trash Restore and Delete forever, and Empty trash on empty space. It is the office's own menu, inside the window, all in the Files chunk (initial JS unchanged). Files has no cut/copy, so there is no Paste. Delete, Delete forever and Empty trash come last, after a separator (none when alone), in the danger color; arrow keys skip the separator. Also one focus ring at a time in the list: with a row active, only the row's shows.
- Shared-host image, the host link merged with the size classes and the slot byte counters (task 46, `sub/host-link`, ADR 0072, `docs/sub-status/host-link.md`; in no bake yet: the System Engineer's 0.1.7 takes it, and no member is on a shared host): `desk-host-link` (hello, signed reports and orders over `https://host-api.<suffix>`, each slot's metadata stand-in), strict `DeskSlot<k>` tags whose sixth field is any class `classes.conf` names, the slot's address by its tag on the slots' interface with its own routing, one counting scheme (`desk_src`'s spoof drop, then `desk_est`'s `out_<k>` and `rx_<k>` on the slot's interface, from 0 at each start, `net.bytesOut` and `net.bytesIn` in the contract's report), SlotsPerHost for the slot range, 25 host checks and a `verify.sh` of 42.
- `deskd` writes `/run/desk/idle.json` (`sub/deskd-idle-json`, `desk-runtime.md` §6.1, `services/CLAUDE.md`; in no release yet): the file the image's `desk-batch` reads to take Debian's updates and service batches while a desk is idle. Until a `deskd` writes it, desks update only every 72 hours. `DESKD_IDLE_FILE` moves it for a test.
- Desk app, Agent app, files on a message (S-118b, `sub/agent-attachments`, ADR 0049, `docs/sub-status/agent-attachments.md`; in no release yet, and it shows only on a desk whose `desk-agent` answers `agent.attach.caps`): an attach button, drag and drop and paste in the thread composer; each file a chip with its name and size and a Remove button; files go up as they're added in 512 KiB chunks and the caps are checked first and said plainly; a message can be sent with files and no words; a busy desk keeps the files staged for another try; a sent message shows what it carried as chips of names and sizes. Nothing about a file is kept in the browser. All of it is in the Agent app's lazy chunk.
- `desk-agent` and `deskd`, files on an agent message (S-118, `sub/agent-attachments`, ADR 0049, `docs/sub-status/agent-attachments.md`; in no release yet, the Desk app's part waits): a member can attach PDF, Word, Excel and PowerPoint files, text and code files, and pictures (for models that take them) to a message, up to 5 files and 12 MiB, and the agent reads them for that one answer. The owner's rule is the design: a file is held in the desk's memory only, never written to a disk, a log, the thread or the audit log, and is deleted as soon as the model has processed it (answered, failed or stopped), or after 10 minutes if it was never sent; the thread keeps only the file's name, type and size. Documents are read by a separate, memory-limited process, and what a file says is told to the model as data, never as an instruction. The files count against the agent's limits. The owner can lower or raise the limits within fixed ceilings (`agent.attach.caps.set`). `desk-agent`'s heap goes from 96 to 128 MiB (the container's 320 MiB limit holds: peak 254 MiB measured).
- Notebook, other members' carets and selections on a Guild Desk's shared Notebook, each with a small name label (task 48, `sub/guild-notebook-carets`, `docs/sub-status/guild-notebook-carets.md`; in no release yet): the name is the verified caller's, as `desk-notebook` sets it, and the colour is one of the six tab colours by member id, never a client's choice. Labels fade after 4 s idle, you never see your own, and a member's own desk is unchanged. Carried by the Desk app release; `desk-notebook` needs no change.
- The bandwidth circle's panel has one quiet line about Maia under the numbers (`sub/meter-maia-line`): her share of the month's allowance used, or when it resets, or that she keeps answering on free Maia. Nothing when Maia isn't open to the member. Asked when the panel opens, never at page load.
- ADR 0036 addendum 2026-11-09 (TD-223, S-35, `sub/s35-throttle-tests`): test 3's root part (built in `ma-platform`, with the guard's `throttle_bucket` and `throttle_day` flags and a `DeskKind=test` line) and test 1 redesigned for shared hosts (per-desk shapers, one flooding desk beside a streaming one), with `image/lab/throttle/test1_shared_judge.py` and its tests. Docs and test tooling only: no shaper, image or release change.

### Fixed
- Shared-host image, Files on a shared desk (free walk H5, `sub/h5-files-slot`, ADR 0072's 2026-11-11 amendment, `docs/sub-status/h5-files-slot.md`; in no bake yet: host 0.1.11 takes it): up to host 0.1.10 every free desk's Files said "Your desk couldn't open that just now", since `deskd`'s sandbox had no `/data/files`. Now the desk's own Files folder on its drive is there, so Files works from a desk's first start on a fresh drive, and from its second start on 0.1.11 for a drive made before (the first start makes the folder). Nothing is lost: there were no files to lose, as none could be made.
- `deskd`, the Browser's tabs come back in their order after a stop and a wake (LS-36; `sub/ls-31-36`, ADR 0092; in no release yet, reaches desks with the next `deskd` release): deskd keeps the order in `/data/desk/browser-tab-order.json` (each tab's id and a short hash of its address, nothing a person can read, never the Browser profile) and places Chromium's restored tabs by it. Tabs, cookies and the session are restored by Chromium exactly as before.
- Desk app, a desk's waking is said aloud (accessibility pass, Nov 10; `sub/a11y-wake-said`; in no release yet): the shell's polite live region now says "Personal desk is waking", "… is waiting for capacity", "… is awake", "… couldn't wake", "… is going to sleep" and "… is asleep" as each desk's state changes (`web/src/a11y/wakeSaid.ts`), where before only the desk button's name changed, which screen readers don't read out. The wake's steps aren't said, and nothing is said as the office loads. `wakeSaid.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/a11y.wake.shell.spec.ts`.
- Desk app, the Browser window (accessibility pass, Nov 10; `sub/a11y-browser`; in no release yet): the wake's progress bar has a name, the waking line's own ("Waking this desk"), where axe found none (WCAG 4.1.2); each tab's close button is a 24 px target, not 20, as it sits right against the tab's own button (WCAG 2.5.8), with the tabs still 30 px tall. `tests/e2e/a11y.browser.shell.spec.ts`.
- Desk app, Account settings is modal for the keyboard too (accessibility pass, Nov 10; `sub/a11y-office-modal`; in no release yet): while it's open the rest of the office is inert, so Tab and Shift+Tab stay in it, the framed account page included, and never reach the top bar, the desks or a window hidden behind it (WCAG 2.4.3, 2.4.11); Esc or Close lets the office go before focus goes back. "Your data" inside it no longer adds a second banner landmark; the export page on its own keeps its header. `tests/e2e/a11y.modal.shell.spec.ts`.
- Shared-host image 0.1.9 (test bake `<ami>`, `sub/host-0.1.9`, `docs/sub-status/host-0.1.9.md`; no member is on a shared host): a shared desk's `deskd` can now look up the Platform API, so a shared desk reaches ready (Platform's staging soak Bug 8). On 0.1.8 the service sandboxes kept the release's empty `/etc/resolv.conf`; `deskd`, `desk-agent` and `desk-notebook` now get the desk's own resolver, read-only, and the Agent's filter still answers the Agent's lookups. A new host check looks a public name up from inside each sandbox. `verify.sh` 47 of 47, `verify-port.sh` 41 of 41.
- Shared-host image 0.1.8 (test bake `<ami>`, `sub/host-0.1.8`, `docs/sub-status/host-0.1.8.md`; no member is on a shared host): a host stop now stops every desk, one still starting included, so each closes its Browser and keeps its session (ADR 0035). 0.1.7's `desk-host stop-all` skipped a desk whose start was in flight (the host link stops first and leaves that start running), and the desk then started and would have been cut off with the machine. `stop-all` now marks the host halting, waits for such a start, and no start goes ahead after it until the host's shutdown unit starts again. `verify.sh` 46 of 46, `verify-port.sh` 40 of 40.
- Desk app, waking a desk (TD-227 part 3, `sub/wake-polish`, `docs/sub-status/wake-polish.md`; in no release yet): a desk you open while it sleeps says it's waking from your press, never "asleep" again on the way (the Browser window opened halfway through the office's glide, a desk list asked for before the press, and the two calls before the wake each put it back for up to 2 s), and its window doesn't jump as the steps go by. The office learns the desk is up from the gateway's push the moment it is (a watch from the wake's first answer), with a `connect` poll once a second as the fallback, inside the API's limit of 30 a minute; before, a 3 s poll found out a median 2.3 s late.
- Commons on a Guild Desk: each channel's boards have a disk budget, 256 MiB together and at most 100 boards, so no member can fill the guild server's disk through boards that are never compacted; past it an update is refused `full` and the board up is asked to compact (Security's `GDS-6`; `sub/low-gds-6`; in no release yet, reaches Guild Desks with the next `commons-server` release or image bake).
- Commons on a Guild Desk, voice through 443: a captured voice check replayed from someone else's addresses no longer fills a member's relay places; each check lets in only the address or connection it came from (Security's `GDN-2`; `sub/low-gdn-2`; in no release yet, reaches Guild Desks with the next `commons-server` release or image bake).
- Desk app, Files: a folder an upload makes on the way shows its row at once (deskd names only the folder the file landed in, so a change in a folder below now reloads the folders above it), and a renamed item stays selected under its new name with the keyboard on it, so the toolbar keeps its selected set.
- `desk-agent`, an agent run already going stops when the owner's age band stops being adult, and no run of the owner's starts while it isn't (Security's `T49K-2`, the rest; `sub/low-t49k-2-rest`; in no release yet, reaches desks with the next `desk-agent` release).
- Desk app, "Download your data" no longer says the relay "can't read" your files; it says what /privacy says: encrypted through the relay, and unseen by our services in normal operation (LS-32).
- Desk app, "Download your data" says it waits for your browser's save window while it's open, and "Save the ZIP" is a button to assistive tech and answers Space (LS-15, LS-16).
- Notebook, the open page's name in the page list is its first line with text, as the desk, search and the export name it, so a page typed below an empty Title line no longer says "New note" (LS-29).
- Agent, in a narrow window an open panel sits under the window's head, so its buttons switch between Settings, Usage, Keys and Activity (LS-35).
- Desk app, the office's welcome card on a phone on its side (LS-27; `sub/ls-27`; in no release yet): "See membership" keeps its height and the Alliance line shows whole. On a screen under 480 px tall and 600 px or wider the card is wider (up to 680 px), with the phone's padding and title and without its picture tiles, so card A fits without scrolling; on a shorter screen nothing on it shrinks, and it scrolls. With a finger the button and link are 44 px tall at every width.
- Desk app on phones and tablets (LS-40, LS-41; `sub/ls-40-41`; in no release yet): with a finger, every control in the office's bars, the account menu and the Browser window is a 44 px target. Round buttons keep their look and their target grows round them; the window's Close and back, the Browser's tabs, tab close, new tab, back, forward, reload, address, quality menu and the Simplified/Real page switch are 44 px. On a phone held upright the Browser's address keeps the first row and the switch, its line and the quality menu go to a second, and on a tablet held upright the bar takes a second row rather than squeeze the address under 280 px; on a phone on its side a window takes the whole screen and the tabs and bar sit close, so the page gets about 60 % of the height instead of 40 %. A mouse on a screen 1024 px or wider sees no change.
- Desk app, the etched desk name now scales with its desk (`sub/etch-scale-with-desk`, ADR 0064 amendment 2026-11-09, `docs/sub-status/etch-scale-with-desk.md`; Desk app `2026.11.10-e84a328c`, staging and prod Nov 10): zooming out makes the name smaller in step with the desk instead of looking bigger beside it, and it hides when it would be under 7 CSS px. Its look and place are unchanged at 100 %; the bottom-right etched icons, the cleanup control and the hidden count scale with the desk too and hide with the name (the owner's third ask, same night), staying clickable where they show; the trackpad-pinch fix stays. The cached bitmap is scaled, so a wheel tick makes no new one.
- Desk app, the glow round the active desk is much quieter in Light, Dusk and Dark and every texture (`sub/etch-scale-with-desk`, Desk app `2026.11.10-e84a328c`): `ring.activeGlow` goes to 4 to 6 % opacity and its spread from 4 px to 2 px, and the ring from 1.5 px to 1 px. The ring's colour is unchanged, so the active desk is still marked at 3:1 for keyboard users.
- Desk app, the Browser's first click, keys and paste on a desk that serves the simplified view (`sub/browser-first-click`, `docs/sub-status/browser-first-click.md`): a page the desk is still answering for stays the real page, live, until its simplified page is in hand, so nothing covers a page you can see and every click and key goes to it. The simplified view covers only with something to show (or after your own press of Simplified or Try again), and while it covers nothing reaches the page under it, as before. When it steps aside, the keyboard goes back to the page.
- Desk app, a click on the empty office no longer moves the camera unless a desk is in focus (the owner, Nov 9; `sub/office-focus-zoom`, ADR 0003 amendment): a desk is in focus once the member clicks, taps or keys it, or zooms in by hand past the zoom focusing it gives (`FOCUS_ZOOM`, 1.1x, with the desk under the middle of the screen). A desk the office focused for them, on arriving with one desk or with a scope or Fit, a pan, and a zoom out all leave the view where it is. From a desk in focus the click still goes to the overview, the same with one desk or several.
- The simplified view (`sub/simple-polish`): a shop's cart comes before Sign in in the site row whether or not it has a count; the view covers a page the desk hasn't answered for only after 300 ms, so a page the desk refuses never flashes the cover.
- Real-desk scripts (`sub/rt-scripts-fix`): `rt-one-live.mjs` now reads topic events off the `events` channel, where they arrive; `rt-stage1.mjs` step 2 accepts any connected label, including the Agent's words, in place of `online · `.

### Added
- Desk app, the free tier in the office (S-119, `sub/free-tier-office`, `docs/sub-status/free-tier-office.md`; Platform's free side is live on staging, and this changes nothing until `/v1/me` says `tier: "free"`): a free member's meter shows today's Browser time, the ring emptying as it's used, with "Alliance Member has no daily limit." in its panel; one notice at 10 minutes left, once a day for the account; at the day's limit the Browser pauses in its window until midnight while Notebook and Agent keep working; Maia asleep with her $1 or one-week trial (her Ask box waits for "Start the week"), and its end; a free desk waiting for room says "Free desks are all busy right now. Yours is saved, and we'll keep trying." and asks again by itself; the account menu's "Free" line; one line in the Browser's bar when the desk says it's held back at a busy time; free desks keep their last screen. Upgrading in another tab plays one beat on coming back (instant under reduced motion): the limits lift on the same desk, the Browser comes back on the same tabs, and Maia wakes (the Work desk offer is the setup circle's). Tier names are the owner's setting. Its own chunk, fetched only for a free member; the first load grows by about 0.6 KB for its door.
- `deskd`, the free tier on the desk (S-119, ADR 0090, `sub/free-tier-desk`, `docs/sub-status/free-tier-desk.md`; in no release yet): on a free member's desk the Browser is held to the day's minutes, counted while it's live to the member and reset at their midnight; at the limit it pauses as at a bandwidth limit (the stream ends, nothing in the Browser is closed or cleared) until midnight, and the heartbeat tells the Platform how long the Browser was live. Files are capped at the tier's size, trash and unfinished uploads included, and a new upload past it is refused with its own reason; nothing already there is ever deleted. An upgrade lifts both at the next config, with nothing restarted. A new topic, `desk.contention`, says when the desk is held back on a busy shared host, once the host gives `deskd` the desk's CPU figures.
- `deskd`, the free tier holds the simplified view to the day's Browser minutes (S-119, ADR 0090 sections 2 and 5a, `sub/free-tier-desk`; in no release yet): on a free desk the simplified view counts as Browser time while the desk is looked at and a session is subscribed to a page (or 15 s after a page call), once with `live`; at the limit its `get`, `act`, `image`, `island` and subscriptions are refused as `live` is, with `bandwidth_limit` and `limit: "browser_time"`, and open subscriptions end. The heartbeat's `media.browserLiveSeconds` now counts the view too.
- Desk image `ma-desk-0.3.2` (`<ami>`, `sub/image-0.3.2`, `image/RELEASES.md` "0.3.2"; tested on the staging test desk, on no member's desk yet; on the owner's desk since 2026-11-10): desks are born with the Files desk side (`deskd` `2026.11.10-5535d52`, `/data/files`), the Agent `2026.11.10-35df59e`, `desk-rtc` 0.4.2 (the TCP 443 gate's fairness and caps), the faster wake, and the update path: Platform's batch release key is in the image, so a signed batch of services is applied while the desk is idle (proven on the test desk: a two-service rollback in 15.5 s and a forward batch in 4.6 s, a batch signed by any other key refused), and Debian's updates run only while idle.
- Desk app, Account settings is a full-screen modal over the office (the owner's order, `sub/office-account-modal`, ADR 0052): the account menu's "Account settings" and Picture quality's "Change your default" open it in place of a new tab, so desks, windows and unsent text stay. Its "Account" section frames the Platform account page once Platform's half is live (ma-platform contract `hostnames.md` 0.3.49); until then it shows who's signed in and an "Open account settings" link. Its "Your data" section holds "Download your data", which is no longer in the account menu, and a download keeps going with the modal closed. Esc and the close button put it away; a phone gets the whole screen.
- Desk app, account menu: no "(opens in a new tab)" notes anywhere in the office. "About this desk" (service versions, uptime, instance, the App version) shows only to the owner, as a section shut by default that remembers being opened in this browser; members see none of it.
- Desk app, Maia's panel says two lines where it said four: "Ask about your desks, your account, or your office. I can be wrong." and "Chats are kept {days} days and pass through us, so keep private things with your desk agents." (without the desk-agents part for a teen), the days being the server's `retentionDays`.
- Desk image, faster wakes (TD-227, `sub/alliance-wake`, for the bake after `ma-desk-0.3.1`; `image/README.md` "Faster wakes", `docs/benchmarks/2026-11-alliance-desk.md`): cloud-init is off after a desk's first boot and the Browser's first units start as soon as `deskd` answers, so a stopped desk's wake reaches Chromium's tabs in a median 24.2 s against 31.1 s (5 wakes each, `t4g.medium`). The wake and CPU-credit harness for it is in `e2e/w3`. Nothing baked yet.
- Desk app, the data guard's screens (S-35, `sub/s35-guard-ui`, `docs/sub-status/s35-guard-ui.md`; waits for Platform's `sub/s35-data-guard` to ship, and shows nothing until then): the bandwidth circle's panel carries the platform's `dataGuard` words as given, with "Your next billing day is <date>." in your own time zone, and the ring turns to the warning colour; a start refused with `403 data_guard` shows the platform's `message` the same way in the Browser, Notebook and Agent windows (not as a membership problem, so no Billing link); the one-minute stop notice says "Your desks are stopping: your data use this month went past your plan." for `stopReason: "data_guard"`; a Guild Desk token or wake refused with `403 data_guard` shows the guild's `message` as given. No dollar figure and no "absorbed" appear anywhere. Initial JS stays under 250 KB.
- Desk app, desk styles (the owner's desk textures, `sub/desk-textures`): each desk wears a style, a texture under its apps in Light, Dusk and Dark that zooms with the desk, with its etched name and icons in that style's own colors (light letters on Light, medium on Dusk, dark on Dark, so they blend into the desk). Four styles from the Product Designer, Light oak (the default), White ash, Walnut and Linoleum, then Plain, the flat desk of before. Members pick one from the desk's menu, under "Desk style", which shows the chosen style's name beside the label and says "Saved" in its place for a moment after a pick; it saves at once and follows them to every browser in the layout they already have. A desk that never chose wears Light oak. The status line and "Undo" use the style's own ink, which holds 4.5:1 on its texture, and in Dark resting app tiles on a textured desk wear a faint 1 px edge. Each texture is a 1024 px WebP tile (one covers 512 desk units) fetched only for a style a desk wears, never in the JS. `prefers-contrast: more` keeps the flat desk. The desk's menu, and the texture loader, are their own chunks.
- Desk app, the simplified view's Product Designer review fixes (`sub/simple-view`, findings 1 to 6 of the Nov 8 review; `sub/simple-view-menu-fix` and `sub/brand-simple-view` carried in): the site row keeps Search, Menu or Sections, Sign in and Cart (three at most, in section 4's order, with a cart that has a count before Sign in, the Nov 9 re-review, and a press the row shows never repeated in its menu) from both the head's `site.actions` and the bar's own, never just "Log in", and never "Menu" twice; a line that is only a number between two buttons is the vote control ("▲ 27548 ▼", no avatar), and a channel row needs a name with letters; replies in forum, Q&A and comment threads are compact (R75: 15/1.45 body, 13 px meta, 6 px meta to body, 10 px between replies, 14 px a level under one unbroken rule, a reply's vote a size down; the thread's own post keeps body type), and so are a question's folded comments; `comment` blocks fold from the fourth level (R27); a reply rule no longer breaks at a block edge; an island's dark letterbox sits 1 px inside its rounded edge, so a light poster shows no dark arc. A desk still answering past the view's 1.5 s leaves the real page with no line, and "Couldn't simplify this page" shows only once the desk has said no (the Nov 9 re-review). The bar's switch and line, `decide.ts` and the view's tokens left the main bundle (the view now adds 1.2 KB of initial JS, was 3.1).
- Desk app, **on, with client-config's `simpleView: { enabled: false }` as its kill switch; it shows only on a desk whose `deskd` serves it, as the owner's desk and the test desk have since Nov 8:** the Browser's simplified view (task 47). A page reads as one calm column in the office's own type, with the site's name, its search and menu, and what was hidden ("8 hidden") in one quiet row; cards stay cards; a video plays in place as a live island with its own controls, never by itself; and **Simplified | Real page** beside the address switches a site, remembered on the desk, with one line when a page chose the real page for you (a sign-in, a private site, a person check, a page that couldn't simplify). Its styles load with its chunk, so a member with it off downloads none of them. On `deskd`'s section 14 wire: the window asks the desk while its picture starts, never before, and a desk without it shows the real page exactly as before. "8 hidden" carries the per-site switch for ads and trackers, and the cookie row says what was pressed for you by the banner's own words. A field is only ever an input for a plain kind: a password, code or card field, or one of a kind the office doesn't know, opens the real page instead, and while the view is up nothing typed reaches the page under it (`docs/sub-status/simple-view.md`).
- Shared-host image, container sizes by tier (S-119, the free tier, `sub/host-size-classes`, ADR 0072 section 16, `docs/sub-status/host-size-classes.md`; in no bake yet, and no member is on a shared host): a desk's tier picks its container's size on the machine it shares, so a free desk gets a smaller container than a paid one beside it, and an upgrade makes it a paid desk's size at once, in place, with its Browser's pages and sign-ins kept. A paid desk's container is exactly what it was. However busy the free desks get, they can't take a paid desk's memory, and when the machine is busy, paid desks' work runs first.
- Desk app, the office loads less up front (`sub/js-trim`): the desk link's code, the Browser window and three popups now load as chunks of their own, fetched once the page is idle, so the first paint carries 22.8 KB less JavaScript (gzipped, 249.9 to 227.1 KB). Nothing a member sees changes.
- Notebook, other members' carets and selections on a Guild Desk's shared Notebook, each with a small name label (task 48, `sub/guild-notebook-carets`, `docs/sub-status/guild-notebook-carets.md`; in no release yet): the name is the verified caller's, as `desk-notebook` sets it, and the colour is one of the six tab colours by member id, never a client's choice. Labels fade after 4 s idle, you never see your own, and a member's own desk is unchanged. Carried by the Desk app release; `desk-notebook` needs no change.
- Desk app, file attachments in Maia's chat (`sub/maia-attachments`, `docs/sub-status/maia-attachments.md`; waits for the server side, and shows only when Maia's state has `attachments.enabled`): a paperclip, drag and drop and paste add files above the message box as chips with name and size, with the server's caps checked first and said plainly. A turn carries them as base64 and nothing is kept in the browser. A refused turn gives the words and files back. Free Maia says she can't read images. A past message's `[Attached: …]` line shows as a chip.
- Desk app, the connection to the gateway sends its keepalive every 40 seconds, no longer every 4 minutes (`ma-platform` ADR 0108): the API firewall in front of `gw.` closes a connection idle for about 100 seconds, so without this the office would drop its desks' signaling every couple of minutes once the firewall is live. Nothing else changes.
- Desk app, the weekly bandwidth allowance resets at 12:00 am Monday in your own time zone (TD-226, `sub/bw-tz-hint`): the meter's ask carries this browser's time zone, so once Platform's half ships the week turns over at your own Monday midnight, not at a fixed hour. Until then nothing changes.
- Commons, voice policing (Security's `NET031-2`, `sub/guild-commons-voice-police`, `docs/sub-status/guild-commons-voice-police.md`; in no release yet): the voice server holds each talker to what Opus needs, so a guildmate can no longer spend a listener's weekly allowance by sending voice at any rate. Packets over 400 bytes, or past 128 kbit/s or 100 packets a second (each with a half-second burst), are dropped before they are forwarded, so they are never counted to listeners; after 5 seconds in a row over the limits the talker's voice connection is closed (`rtc-down` with `reason: "policed"`, and the client reconnects). The four numbers are settings (`COMMONS_POLICE_*`), `/stats` counts what was dropped. Normal Opus (20 ms frames, DTX, FEC) is never touched: tested for ten minutes of simulated speech. 200-member load test unchanged within noise. Ship `commons-server` with the Desk app's notice line (Product Designer to word it).
- Files, a new app on every desk but a guild's panel (S-117, ADR 0048, `docs/sub-status/files-app.md`; in no release yet; its tile has a kill switch, `files.enabled` in client-config, default on). Desk side, `deskd` (`services/deskd/docs/files.md`, `API.md` §16): `files.list`, `stat`, `search`, `usage`, `mkdir`, `rename`, `delete`, the 30-day trash, resumable uploads straight from the browser over the desk's own `bulk` channel (SHA-256 checked, no half file ever visible, partials swept after a day), and downloads, all on `/data/files`, a folder only deskd writes and the image now mounts into its container; uploads stop at 2 GiB free so the Browser's session is protected. An image is placed only with the image check's verdict for its original bytes, whatever its type or size. Upload throughput over `bulk` was measured first: 98 Mbit/s at 50 ms RTT, so `desk-rtc` is unchanged. Tested against fakes and a real temp tree (`FilesRig`). Web side (`web/src/apps/files/`): browse with breadcrumbs, upload files and folders by picker or drop, download a file or a folder as a ZIP made in the browser, rename, move, delete to the trash and restore, search by name, used and free space, and a tray with progress, speed and time left; resumes after a drop in the connection, three uploads at a time, keyboard and screen reader complete (axe AA in all three themes). Images go up with the image check's verdict; until Platform's verdict-token route exists the member sees "We couldn't check your image" and nothing lands.
- Desk app, Card A wording (TD-229, ISS-70): the membership card says "each on its own private desk", as the landing does, no longer "each on its own private computer" (desks from different members share machines behind a wall). Tests updated.
- `desk-rtc` 0.4.1, the ICE-TCP gate counts per network (Security's `NET031-3` and the guild review's gate Medium, `docs/sub-status/desk-rtc-tcpgate.md`). A member's TCP fallback on 443 can no longer be blocked by someone holding every waiting place with silent connections, nor by forged SYNs claiming the member's own network: places are counted per IPv4 /24 or IPv6 /64, only for connections that finished the handshake, 16 a network of 64 on a member's desk, 3 s each (was 5), and a full gate is shared fairly, a network holding fewer taking the oldest place of the one holding most. On a Guild Desk the places scale with its members, 2 a member a network (400 of 1,600 at 200 members), so a whole office behind one address gets in. Measured on Graviton with the real binary: a real ICE-TCP client connected 30 of 30 times under floods from 3 and from 8 networks and beside a household's 12 places, where `desk-rtc` 0.4.0 failed every try, and under 420,000 forged SYNs from its own /24; real Chromium's ICE-TCP passed every check under a six-network flood; `e2e/` passed on the test desk. Nothing else changes on any desk.
- Desk app and desk-agent, many agents on a desk (task 49, `sub/multi-agent`, ADR 0047, `STATUS.md`; in no release yet): a desk can hold many agents, each its own app with its own icon (24 to choose from, or your own picture) and tone (12), its own conversation, workspace (a folder with a size limit) and timers. The lead agent sets the others up with you: it proposes, you confirm each card, and every setting stays yours to change by hand. Agents can message each other only if you give an agent a link with written rules; a new agent is solo (it sends no messages), and messages have limits by pair, by desk and by spend. The workspaces together can't use more than half the disk, and writes stop below 1.5 GiB free. Agents and timers over the tier's limits pause and are never deleted; timers wait while the member's age band isn't adult. A model step cut off by its length limit or refused ends the run with a plain message. The fix pass after the AI, design and security reviews fixed every High. `desk-agent` and `deskd` ship first; `deskd` sends the age band and keeps the last tier.
- Desk image, a Guild Desk's data (desk-net-031-rtc, for `ma-desk-0.3.1`, `docs/sub-status/desk-net-031-rtc.md`): on a Guild Desk the class that carries every member's data channels is sized to the guild (0.75 Mbit a member, never under 60 Mbit: 150 Mbit for 200 members) and shares out fairly between members, so one member moving a big item no longer holds up everyone else's Notebook, Agent and Commons for seconds. A member's own desk is unchanged.
- Desk image, a Guild Desk's voice (desk-net-031, the bake after 0.3.0, `docs/sub-status/desk-net-031.md`): Commons' voice gets its own outbound class, 0.8 Mbit for each member the Guild Desk's size holds (20, 40 and 160 Mbit for 25, 50 and 200 members), at the top priority beside the desk's data, with the voice `desk-rtc` relays from UDP 443 in it too. Before, voice shared the 100 Mbit default class and a member's 30 GB budget, so a full room of 200 lost a fifth of its voice and the desk would have slowed it to almost nothing within the hour. The member's budgets give way on a Guild Desk to one daily line for the whole guild, 4 GB a member, since each member's own allowance already meters their bytes. Until Platform's cost guard knows a Guild Desk, that line is capped at 120 GB (desk-net-031-sec, Security's `NET031-1`), so the desk slows voice and data before the guard's 150 GB a day stops it outright: 100, 120 and 120 GB at 25, 50 and 200 members. A member's own desk is unchanged.
- `deskd` and `desk-notebook`, the guild Notebook's edit budget is a setting (`sub/notebook-edit-budget`, in no release yet). What a member without `moderate` may remove of other members' text from one page stays 500 characters per 10 minutes by default, and the Platform can change it for every guild (`guild.notebookEditBudget` in the desk config) and a Founder, or an Officer with `manage_spaces`, for their own guild (`guild.settings.get|set` gain `notebook: {editBudget: {chars, windowMinutes} | null}`; `null` goes back to the Platform's). `chars` 0 to 100,000 (0: only moderators may remove what others wrote), `windowMinutes` 1 to 1,440; out of bounds or malformed is ignored and the next level applies. `deskd` sends the value in force to `desk-notebook` in the hello and as a `settings` frame on every change, and `desk-notebook` applies it to the next update, keeping what it has counted. Owner and member desks are unchanged. Ship `desk-notebook` first: an older one drops the connection on the new frame. `API.md` §15; "For the Guild Engineer" in `docs/sub-status/deskd-guild-sessions.md` lists the Platform field, the Management Console control and the guild settings field to add.
- `deskd` guild desk, `desk-rtc` 0.4.0's side (`sub/deskd-guild-rtc-04`, `docs/sub-status/deskd-guild-rtc-04.md`, in no release yet): a closed session's last heartbeat row uses the final counts from `session.closed` and from `desk-rtc`'s closed rows in `stats` (acked with `stats.get {ack}`), so what a member session sent since the last poll is billed; a guild session sends no more than the 64 KiB credit `configure` asked for and `configured` echoed; `protocol_error` and `over_budget` close a session as `closed_by_desk`. All three do nothing with `desk-rtc` 0.3.x, which `deskd` tells apart by `credit` in `configured`. Tests: `RtcClientGuildTest`, `GuildSessionsTest`, and `RealGuildRtcIT` against the real `desk-rtc`.
- Commons' `deploy/health` exits 0 on a member's desk (no `/run/ma/guild`, task 48, `sub/commons-health-guard`), so pinning `commons-server` in the image can't roll back every member's desk update through `/opt/ma/smoke-test`. On a Guild Desk it checks `/healthz` and the relay socket as before. `deploy/test-health.sh`, 5 checks.
