- Desk app, Guild Desks (task 48, `sub/guild-desk-app`, `STATUS.md`): each guild you're in, up to three, shows in your office as one more desk in the Guild scope, named for the guild: its one shared Guild Desk. It has Commons (the guild's channels, each with voice, chat and live boards), the guild's shared Notebook, with who else is on its pages, and your own Agent, whose conversations stay yours under the guild's limits; only the Founder and an Officer the Founder lets see the guild's key there (to replace or remove it) and its limits, and your agent's meter shows the guild's caps on each member. It has no Browser: a link in a guild page offers "Open on Personal" or "Open on Work". A sleeping Guild Desk wakes when you open it ("Your guild's desk is waking, about a minute"). The Founder, and an Officer the Founder lets, set the members' agent limits; the Founder and Officers see each member's runs and spending, never what they asked. A line on your first Guild Desk says what it can see, and if you leave a guild or are removed, its desk says so calmly until you put it away. The guild app's links open a guild's desk straight away (`?guild=`). Built against the guild contracts with a faked Platform and a mock Guild Desk; it waits on identity, Platform and `deskd`'s guild parts to reach a real one.
- `desk-rtc` 0.4.0, guild mode made safe for a Guild Desk (the System Engineer's review of `sub/guild-rtc`, `docs/sub-status/guild-rtc-fixes.md`). One member's client can no longer run `desk-rtc` out of memory and drop every member: `input` and `keys` are read and dropped, data on any other stream ends that member's session, and a session's SCTP holds at most 128 KiB and 1024 messages a stream (two settings added to Pion, `desk-rtc/third_party/`). A desk-wide budget, both ways, makes a slow member or a slow `deskd` wait or lose their own session instead of growing `desk-rtc`; measured on Graviton with 600 sessions, a tenth moving large items, stalled members and a flooding member: at most 406 MiB of the unit's 512, no restart, and other members' round trip +0 ms at the median and +11 ms at p99. A closed session's final bytes now reach `deskd` (in `session.closed`, and in `stats` until `deskd` acks them), so a member's last bytes are metered. Commons' voice on 443 makes a mapping only once Commons answers (Security's GDS-1), so strangers can't fill its places. 3 sessions a member by default. Every other desk is unchanged. `deskd` needs a few changes to use it (`desk-rtc/README.md`, "Guild mode").
- The Desk app meets the six app steps of `ma-platform` `auth.md` §3.3 (`sub/auth-33`): a refresh refused with `ma_session_ended` revokes the refresh token at `/oauth2/revoke` before clearing it; `signedInAt` (the ID token's `auth_time`) and the latest `preferences` (`staySignedIn`, `staySignedInSince`) are kept beside the token and move with it; before every refresh a 7-day, 1-day or browser choice counts from the later of the sign-in and the save, and past its length the token is revoked and cleared; the sign-in pages are told which choice ended the session (`notice=ended-7d`, `-1d`, `-browser`). Never applies to 30 days, never refuses an action, never ends a session on the spot.
- `desk-rtc` on a Guild Desk relays Commons' voice from UDP and TCP 443 to Commons on loopback (task 48, `sub/guild-rtc-443`), for members whose networks block Commons' own ports: Commons' ICE ufrags start with `cmns`, and nothing else on 443 changes. Capped per member's voice connection, per address and in total; mappings Commons doesn't answer go after 5 s, idle ones after 30 s. Every other desk is unchanged.
- Commons on the Guild Desk (task 48, `apps/commons/`, in no release yet): Commons becomes the guild's meeting and chat space. Members come in through `deskd`'s relay socket with the identity their token proves (name, role, grants, the Officer or Founder seal; never anything about age). A main channel and extra channels the Founder or an Officer with `manage_spaces` makes, up to a setting (10 by default, under the roster's ceiling), each with its own voice, live board, saved boards, people and chat thread. Chat is numbered and kept by the Guild Desk, with older pages, editing and deleting your own, moderators deleting anyone's (logged), 4,000 characters, plain links, and messages held as "Sending" until the server has them. "Join muted" is a guild setting. Members whose network blocks the voice ports reach voice through the guild desk's port 443, relayed by `desk-rtc`; if nothing gets through, the room says "Your network blocks voice here. The board and chat still work." with Retry. A systemd unit, env file and `release.sh` for the System Engineer, and `DataChannelLink` for the Desk app's `commons` channel. 200 members on the load test as before (`apps/commons/README.md`).

- `desk-rtc` guild mode (task 48, ADR 0077, `sub/guild-rtc`): a Guild Desk takes up to 600 members' sessions at once (200 members × 3, a setting), at most 4 a member, data channels only with no audio or video offered, and no screen, encoder or Browser needed. Each session carries its member's id; `stats` reports each member's bytes, so their Guild Desk traffic can count toward their own allowance; `member.close` ends all of one member's sessions at once when they leave or their role changes. `deskd` turns it on with `deskKind: "guild"` in `configure`; no other desk changes. Measured at 200 and 600 sessions with traffic (`desk-rtc/README.md`, "Guild mode"). In no release yet; needs `deskd`'s side.

- Desk app, "Stay signed in" (the owner, 2026-11-07; `sub/stay-signed-in`): signing in keeps you signed in, across reloads and browser restarts, until Platform refuses a refresh (30 days by default) or you sign out. The refresh token moves from IndexedDB to `localStorage` (`ma-desk-web-refresh`), or to the tab's `sessionStorage` for a member who chose "Until you close the browser" on `/account`, and moves at once when `GET /v1/me` says the choice changed. A token in the old store moves over with no sign-in. A refresh refused with `ma_session_ended` (past the member's choice) goes to sign-in as `invalid_grant` does.
- Desk app: "Sign out everywhere" in the account menu, under Sign out (Security's `signin-mfa-5`, `docs/sub-status/signout-everywhere.md`). It ends every session you have, on every device and app, for a lost laptop, then signs this browser out as Sign out does. If Platform doesn't confirm it within 15 seconds, nothing ends, you stay where you were, and the menu says "We couldn't sign you out everywhere. Try again." Sign out on its own still ends only this browser.
- Docs, task 48 (`sub/guild-docs`): ADR 0080 (was the draft `draft-guild-shared-desk.md`) is Accepted with the owner's decisions of Nov 6 (3 guilds a member, 200 members, Commons settings, statistics but never addresses) and the Guild Engineer's calls (guild chat in Commons channels, join by request or invite, the `manage_agents` grant, `guilds#current` settings, removal at once). ADRs 0024 and 0025 are Superseded by it. `desk-design.md` §4.5 and §11.2 point to it.
- `deskd` guild desk (`sub/deskd-guild-sessions`, `2026.11.08-8d940bc` on the test desk only): `guild.usage.get` rows carry `name`, the member's current roster name (else the name on a live session's token, else `null`). The heartbeat's `commonsBytesSent` per session is now what Commons' SFU sent to the session's voice connection, from Commons' `{"t":"usage","voiceBytesSent":N}` frames, summed over voice restarts and kept in the session's `closed: true` entry; other frames and malformed or negative counts are ignored.
- `desk-notebook` (`sub/deskd-guild-sessions`, `2026.11.08-8d940bc` on the test desk only): on a guild desk, presence names can't be spoofed. Every awareness update from a guild member's connection has `user.name` and `user.memberId` replaced with the verified caller's before it is stored or relayed (Hocuspocus's `beforeHandleAwareness`), other identity-looking `user` fields are dropped and colour and cursor stay, and a `caller` frame renames the member's existing presence at once. Owner desks are unchanged.
- `desk-stream` 0.3.2 and `desk-rtc` 0.3.6, video islands (ADR 0075, task 47): the desk can stream just a page's video player, scaled to the size the office shows it at, with its sound, and clicks on it land where the picture says; anything over the player stops a click. On one `c7g.xlarge` a 1080p60 video costs `desk-stream` 20% of a core as a 640x360 island against 58% full screen, still at 60 fps. Nothing uses it yet: `deskd` and the office's island frame come next, and islands go on by default, with a kill switch only, once the Security Analyst clears them (`desk-rtc` 0.3.7, below). Published, passed on the staging test desk with no island set, and pinned for the next image; on no member's desk.
- Desk app: Maia can dance. When the owner asks her to dance, the server adds the Product Designer's "Party" routine to her score (`ma-platform` `93b1f9c`, owner only), and her bubble plays it once, about 23 seconds, then settles into warm. Under reduced motion or a desk stream she smiles instead. Her avatar package is `0.2.0-dance.1` (`web/vendor/`), approved by the owner on Nov 7 after watching `web/maia-avatar/dance/party-package.mp4`; it also gains `playRoutine()`, and version 7 is otherwise unchanged. A routine the package doesn't know is dropped and the score plays as before (`docs/sub-status/maia-dance.md`).

### Changed
- Desk app, honest limits and Founder (`sub/honest-limit-words`; Sales Engineer's words, ADR 0114): the export page says the relay can't read the files that pass through it, the archive's README says the same, and the passphrase hint says it stays in your browser so we can't recover it if you forget it. Maia's Founder card now reads "Become {lead}?" with "Choose it on your billing page. You see the charge before you pay." and an "Open billing" button that opens the billing page in a new tab, since Founder is bought there and the old request answers `410 lead_by_checkout`.
- Desk image, every desk (desk-net-031-sec): TCP 443 stays as in 0.3.0. desk-net-031's per-network limits on new TCP 443 connections were taken out before any release, on both kinds, because they counted connections before the handshake, so a few forged connection attempts a second could shut a member's whole network out of the TCP fallback (Security's `NET031-3`). Counting per network after the handshake is coming in `desk-rtc` 0.4.1.
- Desk app, the desk's bottom-right corner (the owner, 2026-11-08; `sub/desk-corner-icons`): the hidden count's icon is an eye with a slash, cut like the other icons. From the right the row is now the grip, cleanup, then the hidden count, a gap apart with no empty slot inside it, so cleanup's sparkle sits beside the grip. With a mouse, cleanup's slot is held whenever the desk needs cleaning up, so the count doesn't move when the pointer comes. In Dusk the office's ground is lighter than a desk (`surface.worldHi`, `worldBase`, `worldLo`), so desks stand apart from it.
- Desk image (`sub/sink-48k`, the next bake, ADR 0073 addendum): the desk's sound server and null sink are pinned at 48 kHz (`/etc/pulse/daemon.conf.d/ma-48k.conf`, `rate=48000` on the null sink), so a suspend and resume of the sink can no longer leave it at 44.1 kHz under a 48 kHz monitor. `verify.sh` checks the live sink and its monitor, also after a suspend and resume, with a 44.1 kHz control sink to prove it can fail. `desk-stream` 0.3.3 still sends sound only while something plays with `gate-stops = sending`: stopping the capture too saves about 2 points of a core per idle desk but started sound 12 to 25 ms late, so it stays off.
- Data export (Security's `DA-3`, ADR 0042): the archive's README.txt has a "YOUR AI KEYS" section saying each desk's `agent/keys/` holds the AI keys added to that desk, in a form that works, so keep the file safe, and if it's lost, revoke them at their provider. A teen's README has none, as it has no Agent.
- `deskd` (`sub/deskd-stop-95s`, task 46, ADR 0045): a stop of `desk-browser` through `units.sock` now waits 95 s instead of 45, since the image quits Chromium first and the stop can take about 80 s; a hold for an export waits 100 s instead of 50 for it. Freeze, thaw and start keep 45 s.
- Desk app, task 46 (`docs/sub-status/one-live-desk.md`): the office tells each desk whether you're looking at it, so a desk you aren't looking at stops its picture and rests once the desk itself supports it (the Desk Engineer's part, in no release yet). A desk you switch away from keeps a still of its Browser, with nothing playing behind it, and coming back shows that still at once until the live picture arrives. If the desk takes more than a moment to wake, the Browser says "Resuming…". Music in a tab you've hidden keeps playing. Tabs, sign-ins and the page are untouched.
- Agent window, Activity: a time zone the app sets by itself reads "Time zone set" for good, across reloads, on a desk running the `desk-agent` that audits it as `limits.timezone`; the zone is added on the page that set it, since the row holds none. Older desks keep the page-life "Time zone set to ..." and then "Changed limits".
- `deskd` (`sub/desk-idle-freeze`, in no release, ADR 0045): a desk nobody is looking at stops capturing and encoding its picture after 5 s, and its Browser is frozen, every process at once, once nothing has used it for a minute and it isn't downloading or otherwise moving data. Looking at it again brings the picture back at once and thaws the Browser, with every tab, sign-in and page as it was. Freezing needs a desk image with the new `desk-units` verbs; the office says which desk is being looked at with `desk.attention.set`.
- `desk-stream` 0.3.3 (ADR 0073 addendum, task 46): a desk costs less CPU with nothing a member sees or hears changed. On arm64, x264 leaves out its ARMv8-only code (the same picture, byte for byte), and a quiet desk no longer encodes and sends silence: sound goes out only while a page plays something, and starts as quickly as before. One `c7g.xlarge` desk, 0.3.1 to 0.3.3: idle 9.9% to 5.7% of a core, reading 22.8% to 17.8%, scrolling 105.6% to 91.2%, 1080p60 video 123.3% to 113.2%, with frame rate, bitrate and input latency unchanged. Self-hosted amd64 desks get the sound change but not the x264 one. Includes islands (0.3.2). Published, passed on the staging test desk, and pinned for the next image; on no member's desk yet. `verify.sh` section 3d puts motion on the screen while it checks the stream profiles, which a still screen no longer feeds. Numbers: `spikes/desk-stream-0.3.3/README.md`.
- `desk-stream` 0.3.0 and 0.3.1 (ADR 0073, task 46): the desk's picture is captured and encoded only where and when the screen changes, so a desk that's still or being read costs a fraction of the CPU it did (one `c7g.xlarge` desk: 50.5% of a core to 4.0% idle, 27.8% to 9.6% reading, 30 fps video 67% to 36%), with no frame rate lost and key to frame 5 to 10 ms quicker at p50. When the machine is busy the picture gets smaller (900, 720, 540 lines), then coarser, before it drops to 30 fps, never lower, and climbs back within about 20 s when the machine frees up (0.3.1). `[adapt] order = fps` in `stream.conf` (off) caps at 30 fps first instead. A desk left about 0.8 of a core still can't hold 30 fps on 1080p60 video. Published, passed on the staging test desk, and pinned for the next image; on no member's desk yet. Numbers: `spikes/desk-stream-damage/README.md`.
- Desk image (`sub/desk-units-freeze`, the next bake, ADR 0045): the image's side of freezing a desk nobody is looking at. `units.sock` gains `freeze` and `thaw` for the Browser, which hold every Chromium process with the kernel's cgroup freezer (no CPU while frozen; a thaw shows the page's next frame in about 70 ms on a `c7g.xlarge`), and won't freeze while a download is being written or the desk's network is busy (an upload, a call). Every stop of the Browser, a restart's and a shutdown's included, thaws it first and then quits Chromium as its own Exit would, so a frozen Browser's tabs, history and cookies always come through, a cookie set seconds before a stop included.
- The corner seal on Officer and Founder avatars is always shown, no longer behind client-config's `badgeSeal` (the owner: a placeholder until better symbols). `chrome/Avatar.tsx` is the one place to swap it.

### Fixed
- Desk app: a desk's etched name and the icons at its bottom right stay one size on screen however you zoom, as ADR 0064 promised (`sub/etch-constant-size`). A trackpad pinch now zooms the office wherever it starts, and never magnifies the whole page: in Safari a pinch used to magnify the page, the name with it, and over the bottom bar or a notice Chrome and Firefox did the same. A pinch over an app window, a menu or a field magnifies nothing. And while a zoom out is still moving, a long name is cut at its desk's edges rather than running off the desk, until it settles and shortens or hides as before.
- `deskd`, the simplified view (`sub/minified-challenge`): a person check the distiller lets through now opens the real page as `challenge`, `person_check`, instead of a view of one block. Stack Overflow's human verification page did this on a desk's network. deskd now knows Cloudflare's interstitial by its own script, and a page that holds little by a check's words in its title or a heading, a check's form, widget or address, or a view whose only words are a check's prompt (`API.md` §14).
- `deskd`, in no release yet, the teen lock (the Security Analyst's `TD-2`, `docs/sub-status/deskd-band-keep.md`): a desk config with no `ageBand` no longer counts as `adult` and no longer rewrites `/data/desk/age-band`, so one Platform slip, a rollback or a missing setting, can't unlock a teen's Browser and Agent for good. A teen's desk stays a teen's until the Platform says `adult`; `teen` still applies at once. A band file that's damaged or can't be read now counts as `teen` until the Platform answers, instead of unknown. A desk that has never had a band, on a Platform that sends none, is an adult's as before, but nothing is written, so on an image with the teen lock its Browser stays filtered until the Platform sends `ageBand`.
- `deskd` and `ma-move`, moving a desk to your own computer (Security's `NE-2`, `docs/sub-status/export-move-fixes.md`): once the move has copied your Browser, your cloud desk's Browser stays stopped, through restarts, until you start it there again from your office, and the move's last line says so. Before, it started again on the same sign-ins, and a site that changes them on each visit could sign you out on your new desk. Your sign-ins and the profile are untouched. A cloud desk on an older `deskd` starts its Browser again as before, and the move says so. The Desk app's button to start it again is still to come.
- `deskd`, your data download during a bandwidth pause (Security's `NE-1`, `docs/sub-status/export-move-fixes.md`): a member paused at their bandwidth limit, who had also used up the free export allowance (3 times the desk's storage in 30 days), got a download that ended "done" with every file it hadn't reached skipped as "The desk couldn't read it". An export is now never refused for the pause, so a paused member can always take all their data away; past the free allowance its bytes still count on the bandwidth meter.
- Desk image (`sub/freeze-race`, the next bake; the Security Analyst's T46L-1 and a shutdown gap in IMG-1): a Browser's newest cookies now come through every stop, restart and shutdown, even when the desk was being frozen right then. A freeze asked while the Browser was being stopped could catch it mid-quit, and it was then killed without saving (on a lab box with the old files: 4 of 5 such stops, `Crashed`, a cookie set 8 s before lost); now a freeze is refused while a stop is under way, checked again after it's made, and undone by the stop itself if it slips through. And a reboot or an EC2 stop no longer lets Chromium end its own session before it's asked to quit: it used to hear of the shutdown from the system and close the way a kill does, losing cookies set in the last 30 seconds or so; it's now kept off the system bus, so it's quit cleanly, after `deskd` has stopped (`docs/sub-status/freeze-race.md`).
- Desk app, the weekend review's member-facing fixes (`docs/sub-status/desk-review-fixes.md`): the Browser's kept last-screen picture no longer outlives a sign-out on a shared computer (a copy saved as the desk closed could land after the delete), and a picture past 7 days is deleted, not only left unshown; the account menu opens over a Browser window under it instead of under it; Back, Reload, a tab or Enter in the Browser bar shows the page at once while video plays, rather than up to a quarter second late; the Browser's pause says "Notebook and Agent keep working" ("Notebook keeps working" for a teen), never naming Chat, which isn't built; a screen reader is told of the 80% heads-up and the pause; and the Browser's selected tab has a visible edge in every theme.
- Desk app, three review fixes (`docs/sub-status/bandwidth-client-fixes.md`): a picture quality level chosen in a desk's Browser is that desk's alone, so Low on Personal no longer makes Work's Browser Low; a desk without its own follows the account's level, and the choice is kept in this browser until Platform has a place for it per desk. The Browser on a desk hosted on your own computer no longer pauses at the bandwidth limit, which counts only cloud traffic. When a desk itself says the Browser is paused at the limit, the window shows the pause with the desk's own reset time and stops asking, instead of "Starting the browser…" until the real reset.
- `deskd` guild desk (`sub/deskd-guild-sessions`, Security's `GDS-5` and `GDS-7`, `2026.11.08-8d940bc` on the test desk only): `DeskKind` is read once: deskd and `docker/run` use the image's `/run/ma/desk-kind` (`guild` or `member`) when it exists. `guild` needs IMDS to say `guild` too; `member` is never a guild desk and takes its kind from IMDS (a `guild` tag is refused); anything else is refused (the System Engineer's boot unit writes it: `docs/sub-status/deskd-guild-sessions.md`). Commons' uid in the docs is 1110, the image's, not 1107.
- Desk image, ad blocking (`docs/sub-status/adblock-sites.md`, ADR 0076): the blocker no longer makes Yahoo, Fandom and allrecipes slower or breaks allrecipes' recipe video. Each page tested whether an ad library loaded and, when the test failed, started a heavier ad player from throwaway domains; a short list of site exceptions (`image/recipe/adblock/site-allow.json`, allow rules for named sites and one exact request each, so the ads themselves stay blocked) answers the test. Yahoo scrolls at 54 fps instead of 43, Fandom at 35 instead of 29.5, allrecipes at 60 instead of 51, and allrecipes' video plays. Tasty's recipe video, found in a wider check of 23 sites, is the same fix. Across the 12 sites the blocker now saves 45% of the CPU over a visit and 45% of the data. The blocker's download of its pinned release is retried when GitHub answers with a server error.
- `desk-rtc` 0.3.7, video islands (Security's T47S-1, ADR 0075): a click on a video island now goes through only when the page's own window is under it and nothing else is over any part of the island, and a key only when the page's window would get it. In 0.3.6 a popup bigger than a small island, or a full-screen window a page opened on the first click of a double-click, could take the member's next click, and a window that took the keyboard focus their typing. Proven on real kiosk Chromium; published, passed on the staging test desk, and pinned for the next image. Islands still aren't used by anything: `deskd`'s part comes next.
- `deskd` and `desk-agent`, account limits (`docs/sub-status/agent-defaults-keep.md`): `deskd` keeps the last `agentDefaults` it read in `/data/desk/account-defaults.json` and uses them while a config leaves the field out, so a boot whose Platform read failed still applies an account's lower caps; a fresh value, nulls included, replaces them. `agent.limits.set` with only `timeZone` is audited as `limits.timezone`, so Activity can say "Time zone set to ...".
- Moving a desk to a new image (a root swap) no longer loses cookies a site set in the last 30 seconds or so: the close before the swap asks the desk's Chromium to quit as its own Exit would, which writes them, then flushes the data drive (`image/desk-browser-close.sh`, `docs/sub-status/image-review-fixes.md`). A planned stop and a restart still can, until the next image. Also on the image side, for the team: `verify.sh` checks that couldn't fail now can, `count-address.sh` exits on its verdict, `prune.sh` refuses an image a desk or env uses, and `ma-update` refuses `desk-rtc` 0.2.0 on images from 0.2.2.
- Dusk `text.danger` lightens from `#F7BEB7` to `#F9CBC5` (the Product Designer, kit `b10b107`), so a hovered Delete row (`surface.fill`) clears 4.5:1 instead of 4.37. Light and Dark are unchanged.
- Notebook (`desk-notebook`, in no release yet): a page closed and opened again quickly, such as paging away from a note and straight back, could stop saving on that desk, with "not saved yet" until the desk's `deskd` restarted. No text was lost. The release check saw it after restarting `desk-notebook`, but it needs no restart. Now the page syncs every time (`docs/sub-status/notebook-reset-fix.md`).
- `desk-agent` (review finding 22): the usage ledger behind spending limits no longer grows for ever. Rows of finished model calls older than 100 days go at each start and once a day, which no limit reads, as the longest is a month.
- Desk image, from `ma-desk-0.2.9`: a clean stop of `deskd` or `desk-agent` (a restart, a root swap's close, an update) no longer leaves its unit marked failed. Their Java exits with 143 when Docker stops it, which systemd counted as a failure; the units now accept it (`SuccessExitStatus=143`), and `verify.sh` checks a stop and a start of both and of `desk-notebook`.
- Desk app, your data download (`docs/sub-status/export-fixes.md`): an unfinished download is only ever the member's who started it, and goes when anyone else signs in on that computer or it starts with nobody signed in; a full disk fails the download honestly ("There isn't enough free space…") instead of saying it's ready; a computer that sleeps through 6 hours keeps a download that's still running; a desk that never opens no longer holds the others up, and after 30 s can be left out; a closed account's desk that's stopping is asked again at its own pace; the archive offered to save goes at a sign-out in any tab; a session that ends forgets the last member's desk names and pictures, and a tab never shows the last member's name; a stored guild desk isn't offered; the page names the file you picked; and Back from the sign-in pages no longer loops.
- Notebook: a page's snippet in the lists joins its blocks and list items with " · ", so "Pack the tent · Charge the lamp · Order seeds" no longer reads as one sentence (the Product Designer's look 8, `web/notebook/src/model/derive.ts`). The desk's `desk-notebook` bundles that code, so desks show it once it's rebuilt and released; search snippets are unchanged.
- `desk-agent` and `deskd`, three review fixes (`docs/sub-status/agent-deadlock-fix.md`): opening the Agent window while a reply streams no longer can freeze that conversation until `desk-agent` restarts, with every later send answered `busy`; a deskd that stops reading is dropped without holding up another topic; a change to an agent (a lowered spending limit, its unseen count, its name, badge or binding) can no longer be undone by another change to the same agent at the same moment, which could bring an older limit back at the next restart; a Browser hold that comes while Chromium is restarting off its port now puts Chromium back on release.
- `deskd`, five review fixes (`docs/sub-status/deskd-review-fixes.md`): a repeated revoke of a user now closes any session opened since the first; `data.browser.hold`'s stored reply is good only in the session that holds, so a retry from another session holds for real; an `export` session's `ACTIVITY` no longer moves the desk's last-input time; a `data.read` whose call ended just after the helper answered no longer starts a transfer or keeps its slot; `collab` and `mdoc` DATA from a non-owner session is dropped in one place, with `bulk` unchanged.

### Added
- `deskd`, task 47 Stage A, on by default with a kill switch (`/data/desk/minified-switch` saying `off`; ADR 0046, `API.md` §14, `docs/sub-status/minified-stage-a.md`): the simplified browsing view's desk side. `browser.minified.get` makes a clean, typed document (MDoc) of a tab's page in deskd's own isolated world, with video as live islands and images from what the tab already loaded; `browser.minified.act` does the member's clicks and typing on the real page through an action proxy that refuses rather than guess, with every frame guarded; password, code and card fields are never fields; a per-tab topic sends patches; and the member's choice per site is kept on the desk. `browser.adblock.site.set` turns ad blocking off or on for a site (ADR 0073's switch), kept in `/data/desk/adblock-off` at 0640 and emptied with the member's history. In no release yet.
- Desk image, from the bake after 0.2.9, and self-hosted desks with their next install: **the desk's Browser blocks ads and trackers**, on for every desk, a teen's included (ADR 0076). The blocker is our own: the network lists of uBlock Origin Lite at a pinned, reviewed version, built into the image, and nothing else, so it can't touch a page, its cookies or its storage. Pages load less, use less of the desk and scroll more smoothly, and the simplified view never sees what was blocked. It sends nothing about the sites you visit anywhere and doesn't update itself. A site can have blocking turned off, and on again, without restarting the Browser, and it stays that way through restarts; clearing history clears that list too. The office's switch for it is the Desk and UI/UX Engineers' next part (`docs/interfaces/desk-runtime.md` §8.9). Cookies, history, tabs and your own extensions are untouched.
- Desk app, **dormant until the Alliance tier opens, where `/v1/me` has no `ageBand`:** teens 13 to 17 in the Alliance tier (task 40). A teen's office has Browser, Notebook and Chat, and no Agent or Claude app by any path. An unpaid teen's card speaks of one small desk, with a "How joining works" button rather than "See membership", and a teen waiting on a parent or guardian gets a calm card with a link to send the email again. "Report a safety concern" joins the account menu where the age rules are on.
- Docs: ADR 0071, shared desk hosts pack 4 desks per `c7g.xlarge` (the owner, 2026-11-07), a per-env setting; supersedes the 6-per-`m7g.xlarge` target. Nothing built yet (S-56).
- Docs: ADR 0072 (Proposed), the host side of shared desk hosts (S-56): what runs in each desk's container and what stays on the host, each desk's identity on the self-hosted model, its own volume and public address, stop and wake, its bytes counted and shaped on its own, moves between hosts, and the build estimate. Platform writes its own section. Nothing built.
- Desk image, from `ma-desk-0.2.9`, and self-hosted desks with their next bundle: the Agent reaches only its providers' names (`api.anthropic.com`, `api.openai.com`, `api.x.ai`), through `desk-agent-filter` (ADR 0070). Before, it could reach anything on those addresses, which for OpenAI and xAI meant other sites on Cloudflare. If the filter stops, the Agent can't reach anything until it's back.
- Desk app, the Agent's Usage tab (ADR 0043, `docs/sub-status/agent-limits-ui.md`): set what the whole desk team may spend each day and each month, in dollars, counted in your own time zone. A desk follows your account's limits unless you set its own; the form says where each value comes from ("From your account", "No limit", "$20 · built in"), and "Use my account’s", "Remove the limit" or "Use the built-in $20" goes back to them. Only what you change is saved, so a save no longer pins the month to this desk. Your time zone is taken from your browser the first time, with no question (Activity says "Time zone set to …"), you can change it, and resets read in it ("Resets at midnight"). Prices say the date they're from, and a model with no listed price says it counts at its provider's highest. The export page says the file holds your AI keys too.
- Desk image, from the next bake, and self-hosted desks with their next bundle: the Agent reaches only its providers' names (`api.anthropic.com`, `api.openai.com`, `api.x.ai`), through `desk-agent-filter` (ADR 0070). Before, it could reach anything on those addresses, which for OpenAI and xAI meant other sites on Cloudflare. If the filter stops, the Agent can't reach anything until it's back.
- Desk app: the Agent window works. Add your own provider key, chat with your desk's manager and stop a reply, with what each reply cost, your limits and the activity log, on that desk only. On a desk that doesn't yet keep keys through a restart the key screen says so before you type one, a key that went says it needs adding again, and a key from another provider than the one picked is caught before it's sent, naming that provider.
- Desk app, task 45 (`ma-platform` contract tiers.md 0.3.25): every tier name comes from the owner's setting (client-config `tierNames`, with Alliance Member, Officer and Founder until it's set), and "Pro" is gone. The account button is your initials avatar, with your tier's badge in its corner for Officer and Founder (behind a flag until the owner picks a look), and the menu says your tier. Officer and Founder rename their own desks from the desk's menu: the name becomes a field in place, Enter saves, Esc cancels, and the new name shows everywhere at once. A desk's kind never changes, and a renamed desk still says it.
- Docs, task 45 (`docs/sub-status/tier-words-desk.md`): the tier words follow `ma-platform` contract `tiers.md`. The guild leader is the Founder (id `lead`, was "officer") and the paid tier is the Officer (id `officer`, was "Pro" or "member"); "member" stays the plain word. Changed in `docs/decisions/draft-guild-shared-desk.md` and three `docs/sub-status/` files; the lines left for their owners (`desk-design.md`, `web/`, `desk-rtc`, `spikes/`, ADR addenda) are listed in the status file.
- Docs, task 45: `docs/desk-design.md` takes the same tier words (56 edits: Founder for the guild leader tier, Officer for the paid tier). Words only; the host sizing for a Founder-tier account that is a plain member of another guild still needs a re-read.
- Self-hosted desks (staging only): the bundle carries `desk-notebook`, Notebook's sync and storage service, so a desk on your own computer keeps your notes the same way a cloud desk does. `selfhost/build-bundle.sh` builds it for amd64 from the same commit as the cloud release, (`2026.11.07-0e1fb6c`, with the `deskd` of the same release, which reaches it), and refuses to build a bundle without both. Proven on 2026-11-07 on an updated desk and a new one (`docs/sub-status/selfhost-notebook.md`). `selfhost/install.sh` installs it on a new desk and adds it when it updates one, creating only `/data/notebook` on the data disk; a bundle from before it still installs. Its container has no network, as on a cloud desk. See `selfhost/README.md`, "Notebook".
- Desk app: **your Notebook opens in your office, and your notes live on that desk,** with nothing kept in your browser. Binder tabs, pages, search, versions and the day-by-day view all work. If the connection drops while you type, your text stays on screen, a quiet line says it isn't saved yet, and it saves itself when the connection comes back; leaving the page first asks you. Until a desk has the update that brings the Notebook, its window says so and there's nothing to type into. (`sub/notebook-app`, needs `desk-notebook` on the desk.)
- `desk-agent`: your LLM keys now last. A key is sealed on your desk's own disk (`/data/agent/keys`, AES-256-GCM, ADR 0042), so it survives restarts, updates and reboots, moves with a data download or a move to your own computer, and goes when the desk does. No Platform service can read it, and it needs no AWS, so a self-hosted desk works the same. `agent.key.list` gains `available`, and `agent.key.add` and `agent.key.delete` can answer `unavailable` with `reason: storage` (`services/deskd/docs/API.md` §11). A key added before this reads as unavailable and needs adding again.
- Agent app backend (`desk-agent`, `deskd`, ADR 0043): spending limits run on the member's local time and region, and `agent.limits.set` can set a time zone and a new limit on the whole desk's spend in a day. `deskDay`, `deskMonth` and the zone follow the account unless the desk sets its own. Prices now come from the Platform, which `deskd` checks about every 6 hours and hands to `desk-agent`, with the bundled table until one comes. A model the table doesn't list now counts at its provider's highest listed price, so dollar limits always bind it, where it used to count as $0. Not on a desk yet, and the Agent app's settings for it are the UI/UX Engineer's.
- `deskd`, in no release yet, with the teen Browser lock: on a teen's desk, a site the family filter stops now shows "This site is filtered" and "Your Browser uses a family filter until you're 18." with a Back button, in the office's look, instead of Chromium's "refused to connect". deskd asks the same family resolver about that one host, and keeps Chromium's own page if the answer is slow or doesn't come. An adult's desk is unchanged. See `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §10 and `docs/sub-status/deskd-teen-block-page.md`, whose brand review is still to pass.
- `deskd`, in no release yet: its part of the teen Browser lock for the Alliance tier (`ma-platform` contract `age.md` §8). It reads `ageBand` from the desk config and keeps `/data/desk/age-band` for the image to read before Chromium starts. Chromium doesn't start until the band is known, a desk that becomes a teen's restarts Chromium with its session carried over, cookies and history untouched, and the Agent app answers `forbidden` for a teen. An adult's desk only gains the file. Takes effect with the image's part. See `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §10 and §11, and `docs/sub-status/deskd-teen-lock.md`.
- `deskd`: the relay to `desk-notebook`, the service that stores and syncs Notebook pages on a desk (S-13, `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §13): `notebook.*`, the `notebook.tree` topic and page text on `collab`, for owner sessions only. A stalled or absent `desk-notebook` costs only Notebook. `run` gains `-v /run/desk-notebook:/run/desk-notebook`, `/health` gains `notebook`, and `-Xmx` goes from 128m to 208m to cover what `collab` can hold and the Platform's price table (ADR 0043). Tested against a fake here; the service itself isn't in this change.
- `desk-notebook` (S-13), the service behind the Notebook on a desk: page text syncs through Hocuspocus and is saved in `/data/notebook/notebook.db`, with versions, activity, search and a 30-day Trash, plus plain Markdown of every live page in `readable/`. It has no network and no way in but `deskd`, and a restart, even a crash, costs at most about 150 ms of typing, which an open tab gives back. Not on any desk yet. `services/notebook/README.md`, ADR 0044.
- Desk image, from `ma-desk-0.2.8`: a teen's Browser lock (task 40). Before every start of Chromium a root helper reads the band `deskd` writes to `/data/desk/age-band`. Exactly `adult` leaves the Browser as it is. Anything else, no band included, adds a family DNS filter that can't fall back, SafeSearch, YouTube's restricted mode, no proxies and no extensions, and if that can't be put in place the Browser doesn't start. Cookies, history and tabs are untouched either way. Cloudflare sees the names a teen's Browser looks up; nothing goes to us. It needs a `deskd` that writes the band, and teen sign-ups stay off until `desk_image#current` has it. A member who owns a self-hosted desk has root and can remove it. See `image/README.md`, "Teen Browser lock".
- Desk app, "Download your data": the file can be protected with a passphrase you choose (AES-256-GCM, key from PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256), saved as `….zip.enc`; we never see it and can't recover it. `tools/export-decrypt/decrypt.mjs` opens it with Node, and its README (and README.txt inside the archive) documents the format. The archive also holds `account/maia-chats.json`, `activity-log.json`, `support-tickets.json` and `sign-in-factors.json` when the platform sends them (EXP-4).
- `ma-move` signs its claim with the target desk's own key (`targetKey`), so a move code is good only from the desk it was made for (EXP-1).
- Self-hosted desks (EXP-8): the installer and `ma-selfhost pair` show whose office the desk just joined, with the name and address partly hidden, and ask y/N before the desk goes online; `--yes` skips the question. A desk paired before this isn't asked.
- Self-hosted desks (tasks 31 and 32): the office says "<desk name> started at <time>" for each new boot of a desk on your own computer, and "Another copy of <desk name> is trying to start" while the platform turns a second copy away (`bootFirstSeenAt`, `previousBootFirstSeenAt`, `bootRefusedAt` on its list row, contract 0.3.43). `sudo ma-selfhost status` and the installer say the same refusal (`boot_in_use` in deskd's log) in plain words. Tests: `selfhost/tests/`, `boot.shell.spec.ts`.
- Desk app, the move: Cancel on the code screen and on a move under way, and, after a finished move, an offer to remove the cloud desk with a calm confirmation (the data is kept 30 days, the Browser session came with the move).
- Desk app: the Alliance Member's hosted small desk (`ma-platform` ADR 0097, task 39). A desk whose list row says `startsWithOffice: true` is woken as the office opens, once a page load and only while the page is seen, instead of at the Browser's click. Its Browser keeps a copy of its last screen in the member's own browser (IndexedDB, about 100 KB, never sent anywhere) and shows it at once under the waking panel; the first real frame replaces it. Sign-out deletes the copies. The office card's Alliance line (task 27) shows once Platform sends the tier's price.
- Desk image `ma-desk-0.2.9` (`image/RELEASES.md`): 0.2.8 with the Agent's outbound filter (`desk-agent-filter` 0.1.1, ADR 0070), the `desk-data` review fixes, and `deskd`, `desk-agent` and `desk-notebook` `2026.11.07-98316db` (the review fixes, a teen's filtered page and the fix for the Agent window's freeze), so a new desk, or one moved to this image, has them from the start. New desks in staging are born on it, and the test desk runs it; prod's new desks stay on 0.2.8 for now.
- Desk image `ma-desk-0.2.8` (`image/RELEASES.md`): Notebook built in, with `desk-notebook` and the `deskd` and `desk-agent` of `2026.11.07-0e1fb6c` (lasting Agent keys, spending limits in your own time), so a new desk, or one moved to this image, has Notebook and the new Agent from the start, and the Browser's teen lock, which stays off for an adult. New desks in staging are born on it, and the staging test desk runs it, its browser session and notes carried over. Not in prod yet, and not on the owner's desk. It carries `desk-data` from before its review fixes, which come with the next image.
- Desk image `ma-desk-0.2.7` (`image/RELEASES.md`): 0.2.6 with `desk-agent` built in, so the Agent window works on every new desk from the start, and a desk moved to a new image keeps it. New desks in staging and prod are born on it, and the test desk runs it.
- Desk image `ma-desk-0.2.6` (`image/RELEASES.md`): `desk-data` built in (so "Download your data" works on every new desk), task 33's `deskd`, `desk-rtc` 0.3.5 and `desk-stream` 0.2.4. New desks in staging and prod are born on it, and the test desk and the owner's desk run it, his with his session carried over. `desk-agent` `2026.11.06-60828be`, its first release, runs on both, so the Agent window talks to a live agent service. "Download your data" is on in prod.
- Desk image: before a root swap, `image/desk-browser-close.sh <instance-id>` closes the desk's Browser cleanly (deskd first, then Chromium's own stop), so a swap never cuts Chromium off while it writes a member's session; swap only on its exit 0. From the next bake the helper is on the desk as `/usr/local/lib/ma/desk-browser-close`, and a shutdown stops `deskd` before Chromium.
- `deskd`: the desk honours the Platform's bandwidth pause (`ma-platform` ADR 0095). While a heartbeat reply or the config says `streaming.streamProfile: "paused"`, `browser.stream.set live` gets `unavailable` with `reason: bandwidth_limit` and `resumesAt`, and a stream already open is ended, until `resumesAt`, when it ends by itself. Chromium keeps running, so tabs, cookies and history are untouched, and `warm`, Notebook, Chat and Agent keep working. Until now only the Desk app paused itself.
- Desk image, from 0.2.6: `desk-data`, the read-only helper that "Download your data" and a move to your own computer read your files through, is part of every desk, so a new desk or a root swap no longer leaves a desk without it (`image/README.md`, "desk-data"; `verify.sh` section 3b3).
- Desk app, with `desk-rtc` 0.3.3 and `desk-stream` 0.2.4: **stream quality** (ADR 0039). The Browser's bar has a Picture quality button. Its menu offers "Use my default" and Low, Medium, High or Max, each with what it looks like and "Up to N GB an hour" (1.2, 1.8, 3.6, 5.4), and a link to change your default on your account page. The picture changes within about a tenth of a second, with no reconnect: Low sends 1280x720, the others full size, and every level stays at 60 frames a second and just as quick to answer. Your choice follows you to every desk and browser, and Platform keeps it. If another of your devices watching the same desk asks for more, the menu says so. An older desk stays at High, as today.
- `desk-rtc` 0.3.3: `QUALITY` (type 7) on `keys` sets the stream's level, the highest any viewing session asks, under `deskd`'s `stream.limit`, at most every 500 ms, with a `0x82` reply. `desk-stream` 0.2.4: `level low|medium|high|max`, applied in the running pipeline from `stream.conf`'s `[level.*]` sections. Both pinned in `image/recipe/releases.pin`. Measured on the test desk (`e2e/stream-quality/`). Docs: ADR 0039 and `docs/interfaces/stream-quality.md`.
- Desk app, **staging only until the owner approves, and nothing at all in prod until client-config has a `maia` block:** Maia, the AI assistant, at the bottom left of the office. Her animated bubble opens a chat and hides again with a press or Esc. She answers in plain formatting, with links only to our own pages, and can offer a card to open one of your desks or a page, or to remember how you like answers, which you confirm or decline. "What Maia remembers" shows what she has kept, and each can be deleted, or all at once. A turn sends only your message and four fields about where you are in the office, never anything from your desks. Her motion can be paused, and follows reduced motion. She stays awake while you work anywhere in the office, and sleeps only once the whole office has been idle for a while.
- Desk app, **not yet live, off in prod until Platform's `export#current` turns it on:** "Download your data" in the account menu opens `/export`, which saves everything on your desks (Notebook, Chat, Agent, the Browser's profile with its cookies and history, and your files) and what your account holds into one ZIP on your own computer. Your desks send their files straight to you, encrypted; if your connection needs our relay, they pass through it still encrypted, and we can't read them. The file holds your Browser's saved passwords and sign-ins, so keep it somewhere safe. It picks up where it was after a dropped connection, and a closed account's kept desk wakes just for the download. With `export.moves` on too, "Move a desk to your own computer" gives a one-time code for `sudo ma-move` on a self-hosted desk (task 32). The page shows the time left, and a download carries on where it stopped after a reload or a closed tab. While it waits to carry on, what's saved so far is kept encrypted in your browser, and it's deleted after 6 hours without progress, when you sign out, or when you choose Discard.
- `deskd`: `data.parts.list`, `data.manifest`, `data.read` on `bulk` (SHA-256 trailer, resume from an offset), `data.read.stop`, `data.browser.hold` and `release`, `data.browser.cookies` (digests only), and the `export` token scope (no video or input, only `desk.status.get` and `data.*`). Files come from `tools/desk-data`, a read-only root helper that only an image or `install.sh` puts on a desk; without it these answer `unavailable` (`reason: helper`). The heartbeat's `media.freeBytesSent` keeps exports and moves off the bandwidth meter. See `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §12 (task 32).
- Desk app: the account menu lists every keyboard shortcut, under Keyboard shortcuts.
- Self-hosted desks (task 31, staging only): `selfhost/install.sh` turns a Debian 13 amd64 virtual machine into a desk with the same software as a cloud desk, built for amd64 (`selfhost/build-bundle.sh`), pairs it with a member's office through a code, and `ma-selfhost` stands in for the cloud's metadata service and signs the desk's DTLS identity with the desk's key. It streams on port 4443, since Chrome won't use a home-network address on a port below 1024. Guide: `selfhost/README.md`. Decision: `docs/decisions/draft-self-hosted-desk.md`.
- Browser: sound and picture line up while you watch a video. When sound is playing and you haven't touched the mouse or keys for a few seconds, the picture waits for the sound, usually by about 50 ms and more with Bluetooth headphones, at most a quarter of a second. The moment you move, click, scroll or type, it lets go, so nothing you do is slowed. Needs `desk-rtc` 0.3.2 and `desk-stream` 0.2.3 on the desk; with older ones nothing changes. See ADR 0037.
- `desk-rtc` 0.3.2: sender reports on `desk-stream`'s capture clock, so a browser can tell which sound goes with which picture, and a `HOLD` event on `keys` that sets the session's playout-delay (`desk-runtime.md` 8.6). `desk-stream` 0.2.3: RTP timestamps from CLOCK_MONOTONIC, and smaller audio buffers (5 ms Pulse fragments, 10 ms Opus frames), about 9 ms off the sound's delay. See ADR 0037.
- Desk app: a small bandwidth circle at the bottom right of the office shows how much your desks have sent you this week against your limit (30% of the month's). Hover, tap or press Enter on it for this week's and this month's numbers and when each resets, in your own time. A gentle notice comes once at 80%. Where limits are on (staging now; prod when the owner says), reaching one pauses the Browser with a calm message and the time it comes back, while Notebook, Chat and Agent keep working. Platform does the counting (`ma-platform` ADR 0095); see ADR 0051.
- Desk image `ma-desk-0.2.5` (`image/RELEASES.md`): 0.2.4 plus sound and picture in sync, the Agent relay in `deskd`, `desk-agent`'s place in the image (dormant until it's released), and a check that Japanese, Korean and Chinese pages show real characters. On staging's new desks and the test desk; not in prod yet.
- Desk image `ma-desk-0.2.4`, the launch image (`image/RELEASES.md`): a new desk boots with `deskd`, `desk-rtc` and `desk-stream` already in it and is ready about a minute after it's made, with nothing rolled onto it. It also brings copy and paste on the desk's side, the outbound throttle, a much faster first Browser open on a new desk, and no trace of the machine that baked it. On staging's new desks and the test desk; not in prod yet.
- Desk app: a new desk says "setting up…", with no time, until it's ready, and nothing on the page asks it to start: it starts on its own, and the office connects to it as soon as it runs.
- Desk app: when your membership isn't active, one calm notice at the top says so, with a link to Billing, and each desk says "membership not active". Nothing retries, signs you out or sends you anywhere. Come back to the tab after paying and your desks are back, with no reload.
- Desk app: a desk that can't be opened shows only the support address, and offers nothing to press.
- Desk app, **not yet live, as it needs a desk update that isn't out:** copy and paste between your computer and the page in the Browser, both ways, text only, up to about 1 MB. More than that pastes nothing and says "That's too much text to paste at once." Ctrl or Cmd+C or X, or a page's own Copy button, puts the text on your clipboard; Ctrl or Cmd+V pastes yours into the page, in places that couldn't take a paste before. Your clipboard is read only when you paste, and written only right after you copy, so a page on the desk can't reach it by itself. A desk without the update pastes as it does today.
- Desk app: an open tab now notices when a new version ships, and says "A new version is ready, reload when you like" just above the bottom bar, with a Reload button and a way to dismiss it. Nothing in the top bar moves for it. It never reloads by itself, so nothing you have open is lost, and it doesn't check while the tab is hidden.
- Desk app: the Inter and Jost font licences (SIL OFL 1.1) now ship beside the font files.
- Desk app: when a desk won't start its browser because the browser is older than the one that last opened your profile, the Browser window says it's waiting for an update and that your sign-ins, tabs and history are safe, instead of "Starting the browser…" for ever. It doesn't keep asking the desk by itself; Try again asks once. See ADR 0035.
- Desk app, **not yet live:** it can send a signed-out member to our own sign-in pages instead of Cognito's, when Platform's client-config says so (`auth.signInPath`). Until then, nothing changes.
- Desk app: the account menu has an Account item, which opens your account page in its own tab.
- `deskd`: the desk's clipboard for the Desk app's copy and paste, `browser.clipboard.write`, `browser.clipboard.read` and the topic `browser.clipboard`: text only, up to 1,000,000 bytes both ways, owner sessions only, never `PRIMARY`, and the text never logged. It needs a `desk-rtc` with the clipboard (handoff 15 B). See `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §10.
- `deskd`: a Chromium start the image refuses, to keep the desk's browser profile safe (ADR 0035), gets its own `reason` from `browser.stream.set`, and shows on `/health` and the heartbeat, which now also carries the data volume's `volumeId` and `formattedAt` from image 0.2.3 on. See `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §10.

### Changed
- Desk app: Maia now sits in the right-most bottom corner of your office, to the right of the weekly-use circle (above it on a narrow screen), instead of at the bottom left. Her chat opens above her, on screen and clear of the circle. Nothing else about her changed. Staging only, as before.
- Self-hosted desks (task 35): they now run the same `desk-rtc` (0.3.5) and `desk-stream` (0.2.4) releases as cloud desks, built for amd64 with nothing patched, so they get sound and picture in sync and the Browser's picture quality menu too. `desk-rtc` takes its port from the desk's config (`port=` in `/etc/ma/desk-rtc.conf`, which its `run` passes and its `health` reads back); a cloud desk has no such file and stays on 443, exactly as before. This replaces the self-hosted bundle's `desk-rtc` `0.3.0-sh1`. Updating a self-hosted desk is running its installer again with the newer bundle: it keeps the desk's settings, data and pairing, and moves the port from the old bundle's file to the config. `sudo ma-selfhost port <n>` changes the port later, firewall included, and `ma-selfhost status` shows it. `desk-rtc` 0.3.5 is on the staging test desk and pinned for the next image; a cloud desk on it listens on 443 as before. The bundle is `desk-rtc` 0.3.5, `desk-stream` 0.2.4 and `deskd` `2026.11.06-4919e5d`; a task 31 desk updated to it passes `selfhost/e2e`. Record: `docs/sub-status/selfhost-bundle-refresh.md`.
- Browser: music on your desk keeps playing when you switch to another tab, for as long as the desk plays it. A hidden tab used to stop the sound with the picture after 5 minutes; now it waits until the desk has been quiet for a minute, and a tab hidden for over an hour keeps its desk open while the sound plays (24 hours with no input still closes it). Until the desk can send sound on its own, the picture keeps coming too while it plays, unseen. Coming back shows the picture at once. See ADR 0038.
- Desk app: single-key shortcuts (`\`, `[`, `]`, `1` to `9`, `0`, `+`, `-`, Enter and Space) work only while your desks have focus, as `m`, `r` and `.` already did. Click the desks, or Tab to them, and they work as before; the arrows still work from anywhere and take you onto the desks. So a word you dictate, or a key pressed by accident, can't wake a desk or turn on the privacy shield. The hint at the bottom names `\ privacy` while it works.
- Desk app: the account menu's Account item is now "Account settings (opens in a new tab)".
- Desk app: the desks are the page's main landmark, after the top bar, so a screen reader reaches the top bar first and can jump straight to the desks.
- Desk app: an office with no membership yet welcomes you instead of saying "Your membership is not active". "Welcome to your office" shows what a membership gives, a Personal and a Work desk, with one button, "See membership", to your account page once membership opens, and "Membership opens soon." until then. On the waitlist it says "You're on the list" and that we'll email you. A membership that has stopped still says it isn't active, with only the support address. Nothing shows until your membership has been read, so a paying member never sees one of these, even on a slow connection. All three themes, phone and desktop, keyboard and screen reader.
- Desk app: when you're signed out, `office.` takes you straight to the sign-in page, with no screen and button first. If your session has ended, it does the same. If a sign-in comes straight back without finishing, or you've just signed out, you still get the calm screen with its Sign in button, so nothing can go round and round.
- Desk app: the Browser's address can't be typed into while the desk is being set up, can't be opened or your membership isn't active, as when it's asleep, since nothing typed there could go anywhere.
- Desk app: `/account` on `office.` goes straight to your account page on `platform.`, signed in or not, rather than asking you to sign in here first.
- Desk app, **staging only, where our own sign-in pages are on:** a sign-in that fails on its way back goes to the sign-in form with its error, and when your session has ended you land on the form with "Please sign in again." and come back to where you were, rather than stopping at a screen with one button. A deliberate sign-out still ends at "You're signed out."
- Desk app: the new ribbon mark at the top left, in all three themes. See ADR 0066.
- Desk app: when an app is completely covered by the ones above it, with nothing left to press, a small icon with a number appears at the desk's bottom right, cut into the surface like the desk's name. It's gone when everything can be reached. Click it, or press Enter on it, for a list of what's hidden, and choosing one brings it to the front with a brief ring so your eye finds it. Clean up takes the count to zero, and Undo brings it back. Screen readers hear "3 hidden apps on the Personal desk", and changes are announced. Phones keep their grid, so there's no count there. See ADR 0064.
- Desk app: apps can go over each other. A dragged app or a dragged corner follows the pointer with no stop at another app, a drop never moves anything else, and arrow keys in move mode stop only at the desk's edge. Apps still stay out of the desk's bottom strip. Pressing an app brings it to the front, each desk remembers its stacking order across a reload, and right click offers Bring to front and Send to back. An app that takes keyboard focus comes to the front, while the keyboard still goes through apps by where they are, so a covered app can always be reached. Phones keep their grid, and nothing done on a larger screen changes its order. See ADR 0064.
- Desk app: an app that sits over another, or that you're dragging, gets a stronger shadow and a visible edge, so white on white still reads, in all three themes. See ADR 0064.
- Desk app: Clean up treats any overlap as something to fix, takes even apps stacked exactly on top of each other apart with no shrinking, and puts the stacking in reading order. Undo restores positions, the desk's size and the stacking.
- Desk app: a desk's etched name is more opaque and properly cut in, with a shaded upper wall and a lit lower lip, and it stays the same size on screen at every zoom, so it stays readable zoomed all the way out. The resize grip and the cleanup sparkle are cut the same way, at the same size at every zoom, each in a fixed place. When a desk is small on screen, its icons go first, then a long name gets an ellipsis, and it hides only if fewer than four letters would fit. Each stays pressable at every zoom it shows at, and an app over an icon takes the press. See ADR 0064.
- Desk app: members call the app "your office". Screen readers now hear "Your office, show all desks" on the button at the top left, and "Minimalist Alliance" on the logo, in place of the old app name. See ADR 0063.
- Desk app: the sign-in card says "Sign in to your office", rather than "your desk", since your office is what you sign in to and it holds all your desks. See ADR 0063.
- Desk app: a new desk is 584 x 236, with its apps in a row at the top and a free band under them, so you can move apps past each other and resize them to widget size from the start. A desk you already have keeps its size.
- Desk app: your arrangement follows you between browsers, including how apps are stacked. Your desks paint at once from this browser's copy, and a change made elsewhere arrives without moving anything under your pointer. Your current arrangement is uploaded the first time you load this version, not wiped. A browser where nothing was ever arranged uploads nothing, so it can't replace the one where you arranged things, whichever you open first; if two browsers each hold an arrangement, the first one opened is the one kept. If you're offline, it keeps working here and catches up later, with no notice.
- Desk app: a desk goes exactly where you drop it, instead of snapping to the nearest 8 units.
- Desk app: desks never overlap. A desk you move or grow stays under your pointer and pushes its neighbors aside, smoothly and through whole rows, keeping a gap of 24 between them. Back off during the same drag and they come back; drop and they stay. The keyboard's arrows and cleanup follow the same rule, and cleanup's undo brings them back too. A saved layout that overlaps loads clear, by the smallest moves, and one that doesn't is left exactly as it is.
- Desk app: a desk's name is etched into its bottom edge in spaced capitals, where a laptop's maker engraves its name, instead of sitting in a label above the desk. A problem shows at the left of that same strip. The smallest desk is a little wider, so every message fits.
- Desk app: apps move and resize freely on a desk, with no grid. An app shows more detail as it grows, and from the keyboard, `m` moves a focused app or desk and `r` resizes it. Phones keep their grid.
- Desk app: desks grow and shrink freely from their corner, with no snapping to cells, and can't shrink past the apps they hold. Saved layouts carry over with nothing lost.
- Desk app: a desk now shows nothing unless there's a problem or you're waiting on it. The "online · size · up X min" line and "asleep" are gone, and a saved desk is a little shorter without the empty row. You still see waking, reconnecting, "can't reach desk", going to sleep and the like. Screen readers still hear the full state, and "About this desk" has the details. See ADR 0062.
- Desk app: clicking the empty canvas outside a desk zooms out to your whole office, even with one desk. A drag, a scroll or a pinch doesn't count, and an open app window keeps the view where it is. Esc also zooms out with one desk now.
- Full logo: the mark is a little smaller and the name a little bigger and one weight heavier (Jost Regular, from Light), so the two balance. Minimum width is now 225 px horizontal and 185 px stacked. See ADR 0061.
- Dusk's whites are neutral rather than warm (ADR 0050).
- Desk app: smoother zooming and focus moves on machines without graphics acceleration. A slow canvas now drops to lower resolution during motion sooner, and returns to full sharpness when the camera stops.

### Fixed
- `desk-data`, from `ma-desk-0.2.9` (`docs/sub-status/data-review-fixes.md`): a file rewritten in place while "Download your data" or a move read it could arrive as old bytes and new, and pass every check, as the end-of-file hash is of what was sent. The helper now checks the file didn't change (size, times, inode) from its first byte to its last, and if it did the read ends without a hash and is asked for again. A file that shrank or grew while it was read ends the same way.
- `desk-data`, from `ma-desk-0.2.9`: several big databases read at once could fill your desk's disk past the 2 GB it keeps free for the Browser, as each read checked the free space alone. Copies in progress now count against each other.
- `desk-data`, from `ma-desk-0.2.9`: a download or a move whose reader paused for 30 s had its read cut off and had to ask again, a database from the beginning. The helper now waits as long as `deskd` does, 5 minutes, as `deskd`'s API says.
- `ma-move`: a move could say done with your Browser copied while it was running. A pause of the cloud desk's Browser that it refused was skipped, one that ended with a dropped connection wasn't noticed, and it wasn't renewed past 30 minutes. Now it's asked for again, renewed, and if it's lost the Browser is listed again and its changes copied under a new one; a pause it can't get stops the move with nothing changed.
- `ma-move`: a file whose name isn't UTF-8 (a byte like `0xe9` from an old system) was left out of a move that said done, a database included. Names now go across byte for byte, and a name it can't carry stops the move instead of being left out.
- Agent app backend (`desk-agent`, `deskd`), from a review, in no release yet (`docs/sub-status/agent-review-fixes.md`):
  - An LLM key picked under the wrong provider, such as an Anthropic key (`sk-ant-…`) as OpenAI's, is refused on the desk with `bad_request`, `reason: key_malformed`, `detail: provider_mismatch`. It used to go to the other provider's API to be checked.
  - A `desk-agent` that stops reading no longer holds up a session: the Browser's calls stay quick, at most 16 of a session's calls wait on the Agent at once, a stalled request fails after 5 s and the link reconnects, and a desk stopping never waits on it.
  - Cancelling an Agent call, its deadline or a session ending no longer cuts the Agent's connection for every other session.
  - A long Agent conversation no longer loses its live updates: its snapshot is the newest messages that fit, with `older: true` when there are more, and `agent.thread.get` pages back with `before` (`services/deskd/docs/API.md` §11). It used to answer `too_large` past 1 MiB.
  - A model the Platform's price table lists at $0 in and $0 out counts at its provider's highest listed price, so a dollar limit still binds it, and `deskd` keeps only a price table `desk-agent` can use.
- Desk app: the fine borders the design always had now draw, in Light, Dusk and Dark: round the top bar's buttons, the desk switch and zoom, the bandwidth circle and its panel, menus and their dividers, the Picture quality menu, notices, the Browser window's header and bar, the welcome card and its tiles, and the export page's lists. They were missing, so edges were softer than intended, most of all in Dusk.
- `ma-move`: a move could say it was done with the Agent's recent threads and ledger left behind. A write to a SQLite database in WAL mode doesn't change the main file's size or time, so the last pass skipped every database outside the Browser. The last pass now reads every database in every part again, and one it can't read stops the move before anything on this desk changes. What's written on the cloud desk after a file's last read stays there, as `ma-move`'s README now says.
- `deskd`: reading files from a desk over and over no longer gets past the bandwidth limit. Only an export or a move is free, up to 3 times the desk's data volume in 30 days, which covers a full export, a move and retries. Past that, or on an ordinary session, the bytes count like any others. While a member is paused at a limit, those reads wait for the pause to end, with `reason: bandwidth_limit` and `resumesAt`, and an export within the free amount always goes through, so a paused member can still take their data away. See `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §12.
- `desk-rtc`: a `stats` asked for just as a session closed could leave out what that session had sent, so the desk's reported total briefly fell. A closing session now moves into the totals in one step.
- `deskd`: with no `desk-agent` on the desk, a refused Agent write (`agent.create`, `agent.key.add` and the rest) now says plainly that the Agent isn't available and nothing changed (`unavailable` with no `mayHaveApplied`), instead of that it may have applied, and the same request goes through once the Agent is there.
- Desk app: the Space bar presses "See membership" on the welcome card, as it does any button. Only Enter did.
- Desk app: the bars fit small screens. On a phone with several desks, the desk switch sits above the zoom, so Fit no longer runs off the edge, and on a 320 px screen the Personal, Work, Guild and All switch keeps clear of the edge. On a tablet the zoom sits at the right, beside the bandwidth circle, rather than mid-screen. At 1024 px the hints at the bottom left stay on one line.
- Desk app: signing out finishes ending your session before it leaves the page. Leaving could cut it off halfway. It waits a few seconds at most, a second press does nothing, and the Sign out button on the "can't open this office" screen now says "Signing out…" while it works, as the account menu's does.
- Desk Browser, copy and paste (not yet live): copy on the desk, click straight into another field and paste, and you get what you just copied. A quick paste could put your older clipboard there instead. If the copy never comes, the paste goes as usual within 2 seconds.
- Desk app: the keyboard's focus ring on an app or a desk is 2 px with a 2 px gap at every zoom, like the rest of the page. On a desk it drew under 2 px.
- Desk app: finishing a sign-in while another tab is refreshing can no longer bring the old session back.
- Desk app: shadows on desks and apps draw at the strength they were designed for. They were much fainter than intended, so the whole canvas has a little more depth now.
- Desk Browser: every character types. A letter that isn't on the US keyboard, like ü or ß, could act as an arrow key, Delete, Back or Forward, or reload the page. Arrow keys, Home, End and Delete now also report the right key to web pages.
- Desk Browser: scrolling moves the page by what you scrolled, not over twice as far. It moves in steps of 120 pixels, so a very slow trackpad movement waits for a full step.
- Desk Browser: pages get the full 1920x1080 picture, without a 1-pixel black strip at the right and bottom.
- Desk app: a failed token refresh no longer signs you out. Only a token that has really been refused does. During a network problem or a sign-in outage you stay in your office with "Can't reach Minimalist Alliance right now", open desks stay connected as long as their own token lasts, and everything comes back by itself.
- Desk app: clicking away from a desk's menu only closes it, instead of also zooming out or opening the app under the pointer. The keyboard's focus ring on "Undo" now wraps all of it, and an app's name no longer peeks out from under its menu.
- Desk app: a reload no longer wakes a desk because a second one is too new for this browser to know about. Only an account that really has one desk wakes it on arrival.
- Desk app: while you drag or resize, other apps no longer show their name and resize corner as the pointer crosses them.
- Desk app: the hint in the bottom bar names the privacy key again (`\\ privacy`).
- Desk app: letting go of a desk's corner no longer zooms or re-centers the view, so the corner stays under the pointer. If the corner reaches the edge of the screen, the view pans gently and the desk keeps growing. Fit still fits.
- Desk app: a widget-size app with nothing to show is blank, instead of saying "Not connected yet" on a healthy desk. The "going to sleep" countdown now counts down instead of sitting frozen.
- Desk app: when a backend update restarts a desk's service, an open tab reconnects by itself in a few seconds instead of up to a minute. If the service takes longer, the tab keeps trying, says it can't reach the desk, and reconnects as soon as the service is back. It says a desk is going to sleep only when Platform says so, and reconnecting never wakes a desk. The "Can't reach Minimalist Alliance" notice also clears as soon as Platform answers again.
- Desk app: signing in no longer wakes every desk. A stopped desk shows "asleep" and wakes only when you focus it or open one of its apps. With one desk, arriving counts as focusing it.
- Desk app: an account with no membership no longer sees "Your desk is on its way". It sees that its membership is not active, with no billing link until billing is live.

### Added
- Desk app: a small cleanup button appears in a messy desk's bottom strip while you're on that desk. It lines the apps up from the top left, keeps every size, and grows the desk downward only if it has to. The view doesn't move. It turns into "Undo" for 10 seconds, and "Undo clean up" stays in the desk's menu until you change something on that desk.
- Desk app: right click on a desk or an app, or press `.` or the menu key on a focused one, for a small menu with Clean up, Undo clean up, Move and Resize.
- `deskd` can start the Browser stream and drive real Chromium tabs: `browser.stream.set`, `browser.tab.*`, `browser.text.insert` and the live `browser.tabs` list. See `services/deskd/docs/API.md` §10 and ADR 0041.
- Desk app: the Browser icon opens a real window, with the desk's Chromium as live video and sound. It asks the desk to start the stream when the window shows, and stops it when the window closes or its desk is put aside. A hidden tab keeps streaming for 5 minutes. A desk that's asleep, waking or going to sleep says so in the window, with a Wake button, and a dropped connection holds the last frame until the picture comes back. A browser that can't play the stream is told so plainly. You can't click or type in it yet.
- Desk app: the Browser icon opens a real window, with the desk's Chromium as live video and sound. It asks the desk to start the stream when the window shows, and stops it when the window closes or its desk is put aside. A hidden tab keeps streaming for 5 minutes. A desk that's asleep, waking or going to sleep says so in the window, with a Wake button, and a dropped connection holds the last frame until the picture comes back. A browser that can't play the stream is told so plainly.
- Desk app: a tab you've forgotten no longer keeps a desk awake. After 60 minutes hidden, or 24 hours with no input even if visible, it closes its connection to that desk and just watches it. Coming back or clicking reopens it, and only a click on a sleeping desk wakes it.
- Desk app: the Browser window has tabs, back, forward, reload and an address bar, since the desk's Chromium shows none of its own. Type an address or words to search (DuckDuckGo). An address typed while the desk wakes opens when it's ready. If the desk can't tell which tab is on screen, the bar says so and what you typed is kept. Pasted text waits for you to send or discard it. A session that may only watch is told so plainly. If a page hangs and the desk can't say whether your action went through, nothing is sent again by itself: the bar asks you to check the page first.
- Desk app: real input in the Browser window. Mouse, wheel and keys reach the desk's Chromium, and the desk's pointer shape shows locally with no delay. Click the picture to type into it; a ring shows it has your keyboard. Ctrl+L or Cmd+L takes you back out, and Shift+Esc closes the window. Pasting and text from an input method go in as text. A phone can watch but not control yet.
- Walking skeleton of the real Desk: sign in at `office.minimalistalliance.com`, and a desk panel shows live status from a Spring Boot service on the member's own EC2, over a direct WebRTC connection. No apps or video yet.
- Desk app: runs on `office.`; the old `desk.` hosts redirect there. There are no per-member hosts, so `<name>.desk.` and its kind are gone from the app.
- `deskd` release packaging: a Docker image with AppCDS, and the release artifact for `ma-update`. Not yet built on arm64. See `docs/runbooks/deskd-build-and-run.md`.
- Desk app: the top left shows the mark alone, by theme, instead of "Minimalist Home" (the owner's request). With several desks it's also the way back to all desks. The hints and zoom controls hide while an account has no desk.
- Full logo in `docs/assets/full-logo/`: the mark with MINIMALIST ALLIANCE in Jost capitals, horizontal and stacked, for light and dark backgrounds. See ADR 0061.
- Avatar images in `docs/assets/avatar/`: the mark on white (the owner's choice) or near-black, as square PNGs sized to survive a circular crop, for profile pictures on other sites.
- Desk app: a real connection to the desk, over WebRTC through the gateway. The panel shows live status from the desk (`online · c7g.large · up 3 h`) and reconnects by itself, showing "reconnecting…" and, after 30 s, "can't reach desk". "About this desk" has the full details. See `web/CLAUDE.md`.
- Brand kit in `docs/assets/brand/`: the Minimalist Alliance mark, lockups, app icons, palette and usage rules. Not applied to the app yet. See ADR 0060.
- Desk app shell in `web/`: React, TypeScript and Konva. Sign-in, the desk list from Platform, the canvas, app windows, Light / Dusk / Dark, keyboard and screen-reader access, and calm screens for a wrong account, no desk or an API error. Apps show that they aren't connected yet; no mock data ships. See `web/CLAUDE.md`.
- Added: prototype v1 — Light / Dusk / Dark themes. A theme switch in the top bar, one semantic token set shared by the CSS and the Konva canvas, the system preference as the default, the choice saved per browser, and a plain background on very large high-DPI screens. See `prototype/README.md`.
- Clickable front-end prototype v0 (mock data), published as a private Artifact. Source and build in `prototype/`; see `prototype/README.md`.
